Berta Lomax reads the clauses out to the people at the back and reads the word found three times in the first reading and once in the second. The room goes out into the lane and Aren Kest walks about four hundred paces further down the made road to a crossing two people had put in, stands at the boards on the near side of it and does not go over, and if anybody had asked he would have said both of the obvious reasons were true and neither was the reason. Nobody asked.

**635 — Thawmonth the seventeenth, YR 321, a Saturday and a market morning, the third hour in the room, a man of about thirty-one who wants a clause in that will cost him the most, 2,363 words, column 259 pence.** The man of about thirty-one from a road office says before he is asked that his office proposes closures, that the burden sits with him, and that he wants it. He then describes how it will be obeyed: a letter to the keeper, the keeper's words as the road's words, the keeper as the witness, the boxes full, the closure lawful, nothing signed. The badge-man says the clause would not have stopped what happened on the night of the twenty-ninth of a month and says that first, and that it is written for the next one and not for the last one. The office man describes the rider and the clerk who does not know her own name is on the book and says he has watched the notice be right in four cases out of five and wrong in the fifth in a way nobody found, and that he has never once written the name of a road on the bag and had always thought that was a thing about the bag. The copyist says he has just described the whole of the last four and a half years. The man of about thirty-four with a sheet turns the clause round so that the burden sits with a person and not with an office. The woman of about thirty-six notes that two men have now made the rule about a person instead of about a road. The clause is written. Berta Lomax says it is the only one of the three a person could keep in their head. At the fourth hour the man of about fifty-two says he has four days of road in front of him and will be back on the ninth day; the keeper of the room asks him one question, whether he wants the clauses sent after him, and he says he will take them if she brings them herself and not from a boy, and she says the room is shut on the ninth day and they will be standing in the open, and he says he knows and it is the correct arrangement. The road office man asks which road and is told no and apologises. The copyist did not write his name or his road down.

**636 — Thawmonth the eighteenth, YR 321, a Sunday and not a market morning, a day and a half off on a made road, a bridge over a drain, six men and a cart, a fourth course not laid, 2,177 words, column 238 pence.** The room is shut and nobody is arguing and the nine people who had been arguing for three days are not in the lane. A day and a half off, where the waterworks country takes the water off the made road, there is a bridge over a drain that a cart's off-side wheel went into in February and that has been silted and weeded for two months because there is no money for a bridge on nobody's list. Six men, a boy of about seventeen and a cart, and nobody sent them. The boy says they came off their own road and that a carter's boy paid for the cart out of his own wage. The man of about forty-four who keeps that road was in the market town arguing about a burden clause and did not know until a carter told him four days afterwards. The man of about fifty lays it himself, clears it joint by joint down to the stone and not to the mud, lays three courses in lime, sits down and does not lay the fourth. He gives the load reason first because he has given it four times, and then gives the reason nobody has asked him for in thirty years, about a man coming with a bar in thirty years and finding courses, three or four, and a fourth being the one nobody can account for and therefore the one that goes, and that they are writing a rule about a person nine days' ride away and he would like somebody to write down a course of stone. The wages go into seven hands and the boy counts his own twice. Aren Kest is four days off at the works, hears about it on the Monday from a carter buying a basket, does not go and look, and nobody told the bridge-man either.

**637 — Thawmonth the twenty-first, YR 321, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the fifth hour at the boards, a man who is right for the fifth time, and a chair with nobody in it, 2,411 words, column 317 pence.** Aren Kest is at the low end of the boards from the fourth hour and nobody says anything to him. The badge-man stands in the open on a Wednesday and says there is no market and no band and that about nine of them are here because they came and that he will not have a man's reason for standing in the open written down. The road office man does the arithmetic of one closure and refuses to put a figure on the whole of it and says why, because the moment there is a figure on it his office will do the cheapest thing that satisfies it. The man of about forty-seven says all three clauses will be satisfied, lawfully, by a letter to a keeper, and that he has been right five times and would like it written down with no head over it. The man of about thirty-four asks what the nine days bought and is told nothing, and that in about four months a man who does a thing he is not allowed to do will know that nine people wrote down what ought to have happened first and wrote it in nine hands. The woman of about fifty says that on the day it happens the notice will be read to her and she will be the witness. The copyist then says the man of about fifty-two is not here, that she is not alarmed and is not going to make anybody alarmed, and that the copyist noticed and nobody else did. The badge-man says what the road is, is told no, and says he is not going to ask twice and gives the reason, and then says the wanting was the part he had to be careful of and not the asking. The woman of about fifty says he wants to know because he would like to be able to send somebody. The man of about thirty-four writes the absence down in words with no head over it.

**638 — Thawmonth the twenty-second, YR 321, a Thursday and a market morning, the second hour with the door open, three clauses written out nine times in nine hands, 2,237 words, column 285 pence.** The room does the thing it has refused for four and a half years, which is hold a rule, by not holding it: nobody writes a copy, nine people write the three clauses in nine hands and keep them, and the copyist gives the reason that a decision that lands here has nowhere to be challenged and a rule that landed here could not be brought in and held up. The keeper of the room says a room cannot hold a rule either and that the answer is nine hands, and that the ninth register question is still unanswered and nothing that morning answered it. One of the nine reads his own copy back and finds he has written may where he meant must and says so out loud first, and says why, and asks that it be written down that one of the nine said may. The woman of about thirty-six names the hole: the rule covers roads that can be written to, every clause requires somebody to be found, and in her county a list of names was burned by men who did not hate anybody because they had a sheet, and a sheet with good manners on it is still a sheet. The man of about forty-seven asks for it to be written down and says he wants it in the world and not in a book. At the fourth hour Berta Lomax reads all three out on the boards and five people hear the second clause for the first time. Then a carter puts a load down and says nothing has come from the end of it since the middle of last week and that he has been the only thing going up and down. Nine people speak at once. The badge-man asks which road once and does not ask twice and says afterwards that a man who asks twice is a man who has begun to want an answer. The road office man offers a rider and the copyist tells him not to, twice, and says what she will write down today, which is nine clauses on nine pieces of paper and one carter.

**639 — Thawmonth the twenty-third, YR 321, a Friday and not a market morning, the third hour at the boards, the ninth of nine days, 2,206 words, column 274 pence.** The badge-man reads the three clauses out twice in the open and about four people want to hear them and five do not. Aren Kest is at the low end of the boards and nobody is talking to him. They try for about two hours to find out which road went quiet and this is the whole of what they get. The road office man offers a rider and is refused twice. Then they go at it from the copyist's side and she answers four or five people in about nine sentences and says she did not write it down and would not have written it down anyway, because if she had put the name of a road in a book because a man said a word to her in a lane then the only thing anybody would have about that road would be a line, and a line is a thing you can put beside another line, and she will not start on a Friday morning because it would be useful. The badge-man tells her twice she is right and says that both things are true, that the standing which cost a road its address is the reason a man is not in that lane, and that he has been waiting thirty-one years to pay a bill in that shape and that nobody was cruel to anybody and a road went quiet anyway. The man of about forty-seven says the rule will be a rule about roads that can be written to and that the shape of the thing is not a small hole. Then the copyist hands out one piece of paper to each of nine hands and keeps none and says there are nine and not ten, and says the sentence the ninth day is built on, that a rule is not a decision and a decision has nowhere to be challenged and nine people can all be argued with. The man of about thirty-four puts his in a satchel and does not open it again. The chair is empty at the fourth hour and the badge-man says nothing about it. At the seventh hour the lane empties and the man of about fifty-two did not come and nobody sent anything.

**640 — Mudmonth the first, YR 321, a Saturday and a market morning and the first of a month, 2,251 words, column 348 pence.** The hearing stands in the street and about nine people are on the boards and four had been there on the first of a month in the winter. The room is open because it is a market morning and nobody came up the stair but the four people who keep it, and the copyist says so out loud at the top of the stair and nobody repeats it after her, and the keeper of the room says she had not expected a crowd and that a crowd would be worse, because a crowd is people who came to look and not people who keep a road. Berta Lomax reads all three clauses out twice on the boards and a man at the back asks whether the rule covers a road nobody in nine counties can write to, and she says at once that it does not and says what the rule does say instead, without looking at anybody. Nobody argued with her and the badge-man calls it the correct answer and the worst sentence anybody has said in that lane in about a month. The woman of about fifty says that the three clauses are an answer to the letter that came in on the tenth of a month from a road that will not send anybody, and that one road went quiet in the middle of the nine days and has not been heard from since, and that nobody in this market knows which road it is and no clause they wrote will reach it and they are not going to find out in a month. The copyist says the man put his chair back himself on the seventeenth and said he would be back on the ninth and is not here and nothing was sent and there is nothing in nine counties that can be sent, and that the reason there is nothing is the reason she did not write his road down. Then she says it plain: a road is here and nobody knows anything about it, not whether it is shut and not whether it is open, and she did not put that there, and she has kept it, and she would not take it back and is not going to be the one who says it is somebody else's. A man asks her out loud in the market whether the road is shut and she says she does not know and asks him write one about what. The badge-man says the hearing stands and that a rule in nine hands in a market town of about two thousand people is not finished, that it is nine people who can be argued with, and that the next one ought to be harder. The man of about forty-seven goes down the lane with his page and gives it to nobody and takes nobody's. Aren Kest is at the low end of the boards for about an hour and nobody tells him anything. Nothing has come from that road since the middle of the month before, and the volume does not tidy it up.

## Volume 13, Batch 0003 verification pass — Chapters 621 to 630, two sentences repaired in 0627 and 0628, no chapter rewritten

**This pass was handed `workspace/volume-13/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, which instructs the writer to produce Chapters 621 to 630 and asserts that those ten chapters do not exist. They do, and Batch 0004's ten stand complete behind them and `workspace/volume-13/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` already exists. Nothing was written. The block was verified in full and two sentences were repaired.** Full record at `state/continuity.md` 1504 to 1508 and the hand-off is `state/open-threads.md` 504 to 510.

**`chapter-0621.md`, 2,652 words, unchanged.** The works fail at the second hour of the night on Hearthmonth the twentieth: the third of the six goes cold, the water comes over the road instead of under it, and there are two scours, an old one with clean lime above and below it and a new one with cut stone in it. Nobody is named.

**`chapter-0622.md`, 2,918 words, unchanged.** The man of about forty-seven is right about the number and wrong about the order and says both out loud, and nobody thanks him. The water went over a road with a keeper and a name against it, which is the worst available vindication.

**`chapter-0623.md`, 2,260 words, unchanged.** The recovery beat, and the volume's ordinary work is the volume's catastrophe: a new sill in three courses, the middle one the best laid of the three and the one that will go, and the man of about fifty who lays it says so to a boy of about seventeen and not to the protagonist, who holds the string for three hours and says nothing on purpose.

**`chapter-0624.md`, 2,456 words, unchanged.** Nell Craddock comes four days with a cart of quicklime, nobody sends her, nobody asks her anything, she asks *Do you want it* at a counter, and he does not answer and she does not repeat it.

**`chapter-0625.md`, 3,539 words, unchanged.** The nine minutes in the hired room over a saddler's, on his own word, with nobody conferred and nobody able to stop it. The one document is a sheet with no head over it. **This chapter holds the block's longest run against the prior twenty chapters, 36 words, and it is scene prose against `chapter-0620.md` and not the cost coda as the existing records state; it is a deliberate recurrence and must not be repaired on the strength of the record's category.**

**`chapter-0626.md`, 2,560 words, unchanged.** There is no address: no list of keepers in nine counties, so no way to tell anybody anything, said out loud by the copyist for the first time in four and a half years, and she does not take the standing back because the standing is right.

**`chapter-0627.md`, 2,263 words, REPAIRED, one word.** The first of a month, the hearing stands in the street, the arithmetic is paid and not enjoyed, and the pressure is named: the fifth time there will not be a gap. **Repaired: the badge-man's *it is the whole of the block* is now *it is the whole of the answer*, because the block is the production unit and not a thing in that lane, and the sentence he is answering is Garvin Slade's about the answer to the only question he has been asking for two months.** No figure, date, name or beat moved and the file's word count did not move.

**`chapter-0628.md`, 2,585 words, REPAIRED, three words.** Four days later, in a market, she finds out from a man buying a basket, and what she says is not a complaint: *you have all been very careful, and there was nobody in nine counties who could have been uncareful, and that is not the same as anybody being careless.* **Repaired: the narration's *it is the sentence this block is built on* is now *it was the sentence she had come up the lane to say*, which keeps the sentence's place in the scene and removes the narrator's reference to its own batch. This is the file that accounts for the whole of the three-word move in the manuscript figure.** Hester Vane is named here and nowhere else in the block.

**`chapter-0629.md`, 2,804 words, unchanged.** Nobody is hurt, nothing went late, nothing rotted, no wages went anywhere, and the copyist refuses the good news in front of about nine people: that is the absence of a report and not a good one, and the room's own four-and-a-half-year rule is the reason a shut road and a working road look identical in nine counties.

**`chapter-0630.md`, 2,810 words, unchanged.** Thawmonth the first, YR 321, the year turns and nobody in the lane turns with it, the register stands at nine questions and no finding under any of them, and the answer to the question nobody asked is given in four hundred paces of lane: *I wanted it.*

## Volume 13, Batch 0003 review-fix pass — Chapters 621 to 630 verified again, one sentence repaired in 0629, nine unchanged

**This pass was handed `logs/batch-0003.review.log` and applied its three findings. The chapter that changed is `chapter-0629.md` and it changed by one word; the other nine were read against the same instruments and none of them moved. The full record is `reviews/volume-13/batch-0003.md` and the state's is the Batch 0003 review-fix section of `state/batch-summaries.md`.**

**`chapter-0629.md`, 2,805 words, REPAIRED, one word.** The review found the review-fix pass before it had missed a meta frame inside the very block that pass was verifying: line 59 read *Then the badge-man said the last thing of that morning and it is the thing Block 0004 stands on*, which prints the production unit's own numbered label in scene prose, and the chapter had been certified *2,804 words, unchanged*. **It now reads *and it is the thing the thirty-one years stand on***, which costs one word and points the narration at the speech two paragraphs below, where the badge-man says this is the first of three occasions in thirty-one years on which a man who could see a fault acted on it instead of being right alone. **Nothing else in the file was touched.** The scene stands as it was: the copyist of about twenty-six refuses the good news in front of about nine people, that is the absence of a report and not a good one, a woman of about forty-three walks four days to say her road asked to be counted and it was counted and nothing came of it, Hester Vane is named once in the room and by herself only, and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet writes *Three* in words because a figure in a column would have been a number by Friday. Its money column was footed separately because it is the file that was touched: 1 + 5 + 27 + 29 + 31 + 33 + 35 + 37 + 39 + 40 + 41 = **318 pence**, six marks and thirty over at forty-eight a mark, as printed.

**`chapter-0621.md`, 2,652 words, unchanged.** The works fail at the second hour of the night on Hearthmonth the twentieth: the third of the six goes cold, the water comes over the road instead of under it, and there are two scours, an old one with clean lime above and below it and a new one with cut stone in it. Nobody is named.

**`chapter-0622.md`, 2,918 words, unchanged.** The man of about forty-seven is right about the number and wrong about the order and says both out loud, and nobody thanks him. The water went over a road with a keeper and a name against it, which is the worst available vindication.

**`chapter-0623.md`, 2,260 words, unchanged.** The recovery beat, and the volume's ordinary work is the volume's catastrophe: a new sill in three courses, the middle one the best laid of the three and the one that will go, and the man of about fifty who lays it says so to a boy of about seventeen and not to the protagonist, who holds the string for three hours and says nothing on purpose.

**`chapter-0624.md`, 2,456 words, unchanged.** Nell Craddock comes four days with a cart of quicklime, nobody sends her, nobody asks her anything, she asks *Do you want it* at a counter, and he does not answer and she does not repeat it. **This is the file that carries the block's longest run against the six hundred and twenty chapters before it, 43 words on the basis `state/continuity.md` 1512 names, and the run is the cost coda's own recited frame and not a lift.**

**`chapter-0625.md`, 3,539 words, unchanged.** The nine minutes in the hired room over a saddler's, on his own word, with nobody conferred and nobody able to stop it. The one document is a sheet with no head over it. **The 36-word scene recurrence against `chapter-0620.md` stands and is not a defect.**

**`chapter-0626.md`, 2,560 words, unchanged.** There is no address: no list of keepers in nine counties, so no way to tell anybody anything, said out loud by the copyist for the first time in four and a half years, and she does not take the standing back because the standing is right.

**`chapter-0627.md`, 2,263 words, unchanged.** The first of a month, the hearing stands in the street, the arithmetic is paid and not enjoyed, and the pressure is named: the fifth time there will not be a gap. **Its repair by the verification pass, *it is the whole of the block* to *it is the whole of the answer*, is one word for one word and stands.**

**`chapter-0628.md`, 2,585 words, unchanged.** Four days later, in a market, she finds out from a man buying a basket, and what she says is not a complaint: *you have all been very careful, and there was nobody in nine counties who could have been uncareful, and that is not the same as anybody being careless.* Hester Vane is named here and nowhere else in the block.

**`chapter-0630.md`, 2,810 words, unchanged.** Thawmonth the first, YR 321, the year turns and nobody in the lane turns with it, the register stands at nine questions and no finding under any of them, and the answer to the question nobody asked is given in four hundred paces of lane: *I wanted it.*

**The block is 26,848 words in ten chapters on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file on its own and the ten added, and a concatenated `cat` of the ten returns the same figure.** Bodies, which include the title line and the dateline, are 2,170 / 2,418 / 1,779 / 1,980 / 2,970 / 2,041 / 1,756 / 2,014 / 2,189 / 2,207; codas are 482 / 500 / 481 / 476 / 569 / 519 / 507 / 571 / 616 / 603; `about` is 603 at 22.46 per 1,000; the thank family is 40 and every one of the forty is a negation or a plain statement that a person was not thanked; curly doubles are 284 and 284 with 45 straight apostrophes and no curly ones; there are no em dashes, no en dashes, no semicolons and no exclamation marks, 20 question marks, bold parity is even on every line of every file, and no two of the ten share an exact paragraph of twelve words or more. **No plot beat, dateline, weekday, money figure, name, outline requirement or ending moved anywhere in this pass, and no chapter was restarted.**

## Volume 13, Batch 0004, verification pass — the ten chapters as they stand after the one repair, and the chapter is the record

**This pass was handed a prompt for Chapters 631 to 640 and found all ten already written. It verified them on the files, repaired one measured formatting defect in `chapter-0639.md`, and rewrote no prose. Per-chapter figures are unchanged by that repair: the file totals are 2,573 / 2,595 / 2,638 / 2,268 / 2,363 / 2,177 / 2,411 / 2,237 / 2,206 / 2,251 and the scene bodies are 1,992 / 2,064 / 2,090 / 1,773 / 1,861 / 1,637 / 1,893 / 1,715 / 1,683 / 1,702, the body including the title line and the dateline and therefore not in fact a scene body. The block is 23,719 and the manuscript is 1,777,744 in six hundred and forty chapters.**

**`chapter-0631.md`, 2,573 words, unchanged.** Thawmonth the third, YR 321, day 1958, a Saturday and a market morning. The badge-man has no head with which to call a meeting and says so in the market street to nobody in particular; the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room states what the room is and refuses to have it opened on a non-market day; the copyist sets her condition that she will read out anything written and sent and keep the three series and put no rule in a book of questions. Five of the seven roads send a person. A man of about fifty-two keeps a road and will not name it. Two roads send writing instead of a person, the copyist does not open either letter in the room, and they are read out on the sixth morning. The woman of about fifty gives the three sentences — local notice in the road's own words, a human witness who can be named and found, and the burden on the person proposing the closure — and says she is not saying it is right.

**`chapter-0632.md`, 2,595 words, unchanged.** Thawmonth the sixth, day 1961, a Tuesday with the room shut and the argument at the boards in the open. The argument is set off the past. The man of about forty-seven is the hardest person in the room to argue with because he is right and says so.

**`chapter-0633.md`, 2,638 words, unchanged.** Thawmonth the tenth, day 1965, a Saturday and a market morning, the door open, two pieces of paper read out loud, and the first clause. Hester Vane says the letter *is the argument against the rule you are writing* and refuses it as a complaint, a request and an example, so her road's request is not made a precedent.

**`chapter-0634.md`, 2,268 words, unchanged.** Thawmonth the fifteenth, day 1970, a Thursday and a market morning, nine days given out loud, the first of the nine. The badge-man refuses to retell what the last nine days were and nobody asks him again. The witness clause takes four of the nine days and Hester Vane will not let it go. The man of about fifty-two explains he was holding out against being anybody's witness.

**`chapter-0635.md`, 2,363 words, unchanged.** Thawmonth the seventeenth, day 1972, the third of nine. The burden clause is got, the man of about thirty-one wants in the five words that would cost his office the most, and the man of about fifty-two goes home saying he will be back on the ninth.

**`chapter-0636.md`, 2,177 words, unchanged.** Thawmonth the eighteenth, day 1973, a Sunday with the room shut, one and a half days off on a made road. **The recovery beat: a bridge over a drain, six men and a boy of about seventeen and a cart nobody sent, a carter's boy who paid for the cart out of his own wage, nine barrows of silt, the man of about fifty clearing it down to the stone, cutting the string and laying three courses in lime, and refusing the fourth.** The reason is given and not explained, and it ends in a claim that somebody in nine counties ought to write a rule about a person who can be named and found, which is not a rule about a course of stone.

**`chapter-0637.md`, 2,411 words, unchanged.** Thawmonth the twenty-first, day 1976, a Wednesday, a Wednesday at the boards. The man of about thirty-one refuses to price the burden, the man of about forty-seven is right for the fifth time about a letter to a keeper, and the badge-man does not ask a second time. The empty chair is the whole of what happened about it.

**`chapter-0638.md`, 2,237 words, unchanged.** Thawmonth the twenty-second, day 1977, the eighth of nine. The rule is finished in nine hands with no copy and the copyist refuses to hold a tenth. **The imperfection is named by somebody who is not the protagonist: the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a road in the county where a list of names was burned says the rule covers roads that can be written to, and nobody argues with her.** Then a carter says nothing has come from the end of it.

**`chapter-0639.md`, 2,206 words, REPAIRED.** Thawmonth the twenty-third, day 1978, the ninth of nine. Two hours of trying to find the road and not finding it, nine pieces of paper handed out with no second copy, and the handover takes about nine minutes with no speech. **The repair is a formatting defect and not a change of wording: line 25 held `**“**and you were right then as well.**”` and now holds `“**and you were right then as well.**”`, so the bold marks the speech instead of the opening quote and the line's `**` count is even.** The word count of the file is unchanged at 2,206 and so is every figure in the block.

**`chapter-0640.md`, 2,251 words, unchanged.** Mudmonth the first, YR 321, day 1986, a Saturday, a market morning and the first of a month, and the block's only first of a month. The hearing stands in the street and the room is open and nobody comes up the stair but the four who keep it. Berta Lomax reads the three clauses out on the boards to whoever wants them. A man at the back asks whether the rule covers a road nobody can write to and she says it does not, and says it plainly and at once. A man asks the copyist about the quiet road and she says *I do not know*, and that is the answer. **The woman of about fifty says the three clauses are an answer to the letter that will not be sending anybody, and in the same breath that one road went quiet in the middle of the nine days and has not been heard from since, that nobody in the market knows which road it is, that no clause reaches it, and that they are not going to find out in a month.** Nobody thanked her. The volume does not tidy it up and no chapter after this one is going to say which road it was.

## Volume 13, Batch 0005, Chapters 641 to 650, "Two Beds, One Piece of Work", days 1999 to 2040

**`chapter-0641.md`, 2,713 words.** Mudmonth the fifteenth, day 2000, a Saturday and a market morning, the second hour in the market street. **The meeting is called in the open because there is no head.** The badge-man has a badge, a chain of office and thirty-one years and no head, and says so; the copyist refuses a Crown's form with a fourth ruled line and no head and says what she would have to write under each of the four; the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room says the room cannot call anything and will not be opened on a Tuesday. **It is called for a market morning in seven days with nothing at the top of it and no paper about the calling.** A man of about thirty-one from a road office asks who tells a road four days off and the answer is nobody. A woman of about thirty-four who came four days for one sentence says at the fourth hour that she has come to have a road put in a column, and is not told she cannot.

**`chapter-0642.md`, 2,490 words.** Mudmonth the twentieth, day 2005, a Thursday and a market morning, the room over a weigh-house with the door open. **More than nine people, and five of them wrong on purpose.** The copyist counts fourteen off the doorway and says the five out loud. The newcomer says she wants her road counted and is told that a figure that gets into one of the three series gets into nine counties inside a week. The woman of about thirty-six names the hole again in a room instead of a lane. Hester Vane says for the second time that her road's count is hers and that citing her is using her. The badge-man rules that the meeting hears anybody on the same terms and that a person who came for a figure will be refused in the open.

**`chapter-0643.md`, 2,696 words.** Mudmonth the twenty-second, day 2007, a Saturday and a market morning, the market street at the boards. **The first meeting, and it is not written down.** The newcomer asks the meeting to find the road that went quiet and say its name, and the badge-man says no and gives the reason he arrived at over about three weeks: a rider inside four days would put a stranger on a quiet road inside a week, sent by a man with a badge who has no head. The man of about forty-seven asks whether the three clauses bind anybody who has not got a clause. **The badge-man asks Aren Kest whether he will be the middle of it and is refused, in about nine words, on the ground that a question that stops at a man is a road shut with better manners than the last one.** The right to refuse is asked a third time and refused an answer. **The meeting decides to write nothing at all**, and the badge-man names the two things left.

**`chapter-0644.md`, 2,291 words.** Mudmonth the twenty-seven, day 2012, a Thursday and a market morning, about the fifth hour at the far end of about four hundred paces of lane. **The separation, agreed and painful, in a lane, without a scene, and neither of them says the word.** She says three things: the room is the room and the two rooms over a saddler's are his; there will be no part of this in which the two of them are both in the middle; there is no document. He says the one thing that is his, that saying no on Saturday made the last arrangement impossible and that he is not sorry for the no. Separate allies and the right to disagree. **Then they do the ninth gate**, and a woman of about sixty says she is too old to walk a street and stand at a wall, and he tells her that there will be a man at the boards. They walk back about the length of a cart apart. The four and a half years are not referred to as four and a half years about the two of them anywhere in the block.

**`chapter-0645.md`, 2,333 words.** Greenmonth the first, day 2016, **a Monday and not a market morning, and the block's only first of a month**, the room over a weigh-house shut behind its own door and the hearing standing in the street with no bench in it. The badge-man does the thing he has never done on a first of a month and says the three clauses out loud in a street, to about nine people who keep no road. **The imperfection is named in the open and is not called small: the meeting's answer to the road nobody can write to is that it is going to do nothing, and she says that in a street is the worst place to say it and the only one she has been asked for.** A woman of about forty-five asks for a piece of the rule and is refused, correctly, because a piece handed round is a rule nobody is responsible for. A man of about thirty-eight keeps a road that sends four sentences a month and not one of them is in any of the three series, and the copyist's own answer is that the series are right.

**`chapter-0646.md`, 2,264 words.** Greenmonth the fourth, day 2019, a Thursday and a market morning, about the third hour at the town's own gate. **The recovery beat, the same masonry and the same refusal: three courses of sill in lime and no fourth, at a drain taking the water off about four miles of made road.** Six men, a boy of about seventeen and a cart nobody sent. **The reason is new and it is about lime and not about accounting: three courses and a bed is what a lime bed will take in that ground, and a fourth would have to go on lime that has not been through a winter and would come up in about four months and take the third with it.** The stone for the fourth course is laid out in a line on the bank with the mallet on top of it, where anybody can see it. The wage is counted out loud at the boards from nine households, one puts in nothing and is not argued with, and the boy is paid a day and a half and says he would rather have had the work and is given it.

**`chapter-0647.md`, 2,141 words.** Greenmonth the seventh, day 2022, **a Sunday and not a market morning**, a market street with about four carts in it and the room shut. **Three attempts to mend the rule from the outside, each refused by one person and not voted on.** A fixed day is refused because a fixed day is a notice. A second set of books in a road office is refused. A letter carried by a carter is refused, and the woman of about thirty-four says she has already written in her own hand and carried it herself. **The copyist says the volume's second cost out loud in a street: four years and a half of correct practice is the reason two warnings about one channel sat about eleven paces apart unread, and she is not going to be talked out of it.** The man of about forty-seven puts the page back inside his coat in front of about nine people.

**`chapter-0648.md`, 2,192 words.** Greenmonth the eleventh, day 2026, a Thursday and a market morning, the market street at the boards. **The answer to the question left open on the fourth of the nine days, given out loud and not softened.** The woman of about thirty-six repeats her question and her own answer first. **The badge-man says yes, it is dangerous, and the danger is not that a road says no: in about four months a man is going to do something he is not allowed to do, and if a road can refuse then that man will be able to say the road refused, and nobody will ever be able to reach inside the refusal to find out whether the road refused or was asked in a way it could not say no to.** He says he wanted to put something in a rule about it and is not going to, and that this is the first thing he has wanted to put in a rule in thirty-one years and has not. **About nine people look at Aren Kest and nobody asks him anything.** The finding out loud is that they have thought about it and it is worse and not better.

**`chapter-0649.md`, 2,156 words.** Greenmonth the thirteenth, day 2028, a Saturday and a market morning, the market street at the boards. **Who will hold the sentence about the right to refuse, and the answer that it is not a person.** The copyist will not be the tenth. The woman of about thirty-six says a sentence is kept by every person who heard it and could say it back, and about nine people agree one after another, and nobody counts. The badge-man asks Aren Kest once and does not ask twice, for the reason he has given before. Hester Vane says her two sentences for the last time in that street. The man of about fifty-two is still not there and nobody has been sent. **Berta Lomax says she has had the words since the seventh of Mudmonth and that nobody gave them to her, and that she will read them aloud in the room on a market morning.** The copyist says what she will have in about four years instead, and it is nothing.

**`chapter-0650.md`, 2,061 words.** Greenmonth the twenty-fifth, day 2040, a Thursday and a market morning, the second hour in the market street and then a room over a saddler's. **The volume's last main-world page, and it is not a settlement.** The hearing stands in the street on a day that is not the first of a month and the badge-man says it anyway. **It does not go to the room over the weigh-house and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room says why, out loud, that it is up thirteen stairs and a woman of about sixty is not going up thirteen stairs.** About nine people stand in the inquiry's rooms. There is no bench. **The floor has been washed by somebody nobody in that room knew and nobody was thanked for.** A half leaf lies face down on a table and nobody turned it and nobody said what is on the other side. The register is at nine questions with no finding under any of them. She reads the standing of the hearing and the state of the book aloud and nobody thanks her. The badge-man says the meeting is not finished and that there is no head to call the next one and that he is not going to make one. **Then Aren Kest goes out of the room and down to the lane, and nobody comments, and that is the arrangement and it is not in a document. And the last thing anybody hears is Berta Lomax of the fruit valley reading the clause about the right to refuse aloud in a room with about nine people in it, and nobody thanks her: a road may refuse to name a witness, and if it refuses then nothing happens to that road, the man who said the road might be shut has not shown that it may be shut, no road has to say out loud that it has refused, and nobody is going to be able to get inside it afterwards and find out.**

---

## Volume 13, Batch 0005, the review repair — the same ten chapters after it, per chapter, and the word counts in the section above are superseded

**A review of this block found the prose to be summary rather than scene, the cast to be demographic slots, the mandated coda to be a spreadsheet, the block to close on its thinnest pages, and the state files to be 3.0 MB. Ten chapters were repaired in place, no chapter was restarted and no beat was moved. The canon of every chapter above is unchanged and every event in the ten entries above still happened; what changed is how it is written and how long it is. The full finding is `reviews/volume-13/batch-0005.md`, the figures are `state/continuity.md` 1531 to 1537, and the shape of the repair is below per chapter. THE WORD COUNTS GIVEN IN THE SECTION ABOVE ARE THE PRE-REPAIR COUNTS AND ARE SUPERSEDED BY THE LINE UNDER EACH CHAPTER HERE.**

**`chapter-0641.md`, 3,086 words** (was 2,713). The meeting is called in the open, unchanged. **Added:** the market assembling before the light, the carts and the drain and the wet stone; Aren Kest counting the people who come to the boards and look for somebody they know, and why that is the one thing he is good at that nobody wants paying for; the badge-man turning the badge over like a coin he has carried too long and putting it back inside his coat; the copyist's thumb on the corner of a sheet, which is what she does to every sheet anybody puts in front of her for four years and a half; the door behind the woman of about twenty-nine standing open on purpose; and his reason for not going up the thirteen stairs, which is a room with a bed in it and a woman he has not asked anything of, and the week that has been standing in the same place without moving. **The cost of the chapter is now a broom, a swept floor, and four ruled lines coming up in the grit and going out with it.**

**`chapter-0642.md`, 2,730 words** (was 2,490). Fourteen people in a room built for nine, unchanged. **Added:** thirteen stairs and four minutes and the market audible from the top of them; the discovery that the room is a room about the room; the copyist saying the last part of her own speech to the table rather than to the woman, so as not to be the second thing that made her feel small that day; a man at the back writing something down and rubbing it out on his own knee, which took longer than writing it; Aren Kest nine steps up the stair once, and not seeing her face from the doorway, and understanding at the time that that is the only reason he came up; and about four people going up in the afternoon, reading a notice that says a room is shut on the day it is shut, and turning round.

**`chapter-0643.md`, 2,901 words** (was 2,696). The first meeting, and Aren Kest refuses the middle, unchanged. **Added:** the sun making a hole in the crowd because a man in the light is a man you can see the face of, and the street making the place without anybody deciding to; the dog; the boy selling nothing; and the interior beat the chapter had none of, which is the arithmetic he had been running for two hours, that every question with no answer would stop at him, and that a question which stops at a man is a road shut with better manners than the last one, and the two failed attempts to put down the road he shut in nine minutes in about four months.

**`chapter-0644.md`, 2,687 words** (was 2,291). The separation, unchanged in every particular, and the word still printed nowhere. **Added:** the lane wet through and drying on top, one puddle with a rim of mud round it because four days of carts have gone round it; the four seconds of getting up; what she does at a gate, which is stand with her back to the hedge so a person is not obliged to look at a face, and eight gates without once looking at what she came to look at; **the part of the first of the three things that takes him hardest, which is not the two rooms and is not the room but the sentence about the floor somebody washes, and his understanding that the two sentences are about the same thing and that there is no arrangement for asking her about that;** the one thing he brought to the end of the lane and did not say, which is that he knew the two rooms were hers and had gone on putting his coat on that hook for about four months, and that a man who says a thing like that out loud turns himself into a person who wants something; and the carter's wheel going through the puddle and the water up the wall a hand's width and coming back down.

**`chapter-0645.md`, 2,637 words** (was 2,333). The hearing on a first of a month with the room shut, the three clauses said in a street, the imperfection named and not called small, and the piece refused, all unchanged. **Added:** what a Monday morning in that street actually is, with the market's old nails showing in the stone and the two carts going through without stopping; the fifth of the people at the edges who have never come before and do not know whether they are allowed; and Aren's interior beat at the request for a piece, that he looked at the ground not from shame but because he could not think of one thing to say to a woman of about forty-five worth her having been told the truth for.

**`chapter-0646.md`, 2,725 words** (was 2,264). Three courses and no fourth, the reason about lime, the stone on the bank, the wage, all unchanged. **Added:** why the drain has been standing three weeks and why nobody sent anybody, which is on nobody's list; lime itself, cold to make and hot to work, the two courses of bed, the heel of the hand burned twice a year and not mentioned; the sound a dressed sill stone makes going down true; **and the hour of laying stone, and the second and a half before he understands why his chest has gone tight, and the recognition that a man refusing to lay a fourth course in front of the same nine people is doing the one thing he did in a room with nine people in it, and that the difference is that the man with the mallet will still be there in four months to be asked about it.** A carter's direct question about who pays the boy was added to open the wage.

**`chapter-0647.md`, 2,592 words** (was 2,141). Three attempts and three refusals, and the copyist's cost said in a street, all unchanged. **Added:** the worst-lit street in the county on a Sunday, the four carts as the only tall things in it, the gutter nobody stands on; the badge-man saying that nobody is to write down who came, because a list of the people who tried would be worth nothing if it went into a room; **and the copyist's own interior beat, that the last part of the longest thing she has said in a street came out last and had not been said in a room either;** and Aren's reason for saying his one sentence to her and not to the market, that a thing said to a market is a thing the market is asked to hold, and the beat after it, where he wanted her to say the words to him and did not ask.

**`chapter-0648.md`, 2,782 words** (was 2,192). The answer, given out loud and not softened, unchanged. **Added:** the market going on saying it underneath the four minutes, the carter, the laugh that stopped when she turned her head; **and the interior beat the chapter most needed, that he heard the man in the badge-man's sentence and he is the man in the sentence, and that the shape is now available to about four hundred people and he is still nobody, and that he was glad of it and about four minutes later was not, and that a street like this holds both without picking;** and the man of about forty-seven's second speech, about taking the page out of his coat twice and putting it back twice, and that the second time he was not deciding anything, he was finding out whether a rule could stand up in a street without a page in the room, and that he has known that for about four months and did not want it. **The mark conversion in this chapter was wrong and is now right: three hundred and twenty-three pence is six marks and thirty-five over, not thirty over.**

**`chapter-0649.md`, 2,673 words** (was 2,156). Who will hold it, and the answer that it is not a person, unchanged. **Added:** a smaller morning than the last one, everybody ready to say no, sheaves at the high end and the smell of cut corn; **the half hour of nobody volunteering as the worst of the morning and the badge-man letting it be the worst on purpose, not naming anybody, two people coming and going without being told and a carter asked twice about a sitting;** the man of about thirty-eight saying it in the middle of a sentence he had changed his mind about nine words earlier; the last of the nine saying it too quietly and being made to say it again; **and Aren's interior beat, that he had about four answers ready and used none of them, and that this was the first time in two volumes that being asked had not gone into him like a nail, and that nothing in nine counties was going to be easier for him because of it.** The exchange between the badge-man and the copyist was also untangled: the quoted line is his question and her answer follows it.

**`chapter-0650.md`, 2,841 words** (was 2,061). The volume's last page, and every one of its required elements is on it. **Added and fixed:** the stair of the saddler's is nine steps and inside the shop, so everybody comes in out of the dark four feet wide at a time and about nine coats go up on the back of a door and the door is not shut all morning; **and the paragraph the last page most needed, that it is his room, that he has slept in it eleven nights and has a spare blanket on the sill and the key, that he has a quarter of an hour of it, and that a man who owns the room and is not the owner of the thing in it is a visitor in his own doorway.** The copyist's hand on the register. The woman of about thirty-six saying, at last, that the words are hers, which the badge-man promised on her behalf and the block had left unlanded. The question that opens the badge-man's account of why the meeting is not finished: a man of about forty-four asking who calls the next one, and being told nobody, and being asked not to arrange it in nine days. **The scrambled attribution is repaired, and the missing possessive is reworded, and the last two paragraphs of the chapter now stand after the money column so that the volume's last line is the clause itself.**

## Volume 14, Batch 0001 — Chapters 651 to 660, "The Instrument That Shuts Nothing", days 2041 to 2094

**Ten chapters, all of them new prose, written from `outline/volume-14.md`, which this batch also wrote. The name of the antagonist is printed once in this block and only once. Every figure below was counted on the ten files as they stand after this phase's last edit, and the two bases are named separately.**

**`chapter-0651.md`, 3,752 words.** Greenmonth the twenty-sixth, a Friday and not a market morning, the second hour in the market street and then four lanes. The errand of nine gates is four lanes done. A cart's wheel off at the low side of the fourth gate and nine people not helping. **The naming: the copyist of about twenty-six says the name of the woman who keeps the room over a weigh-house out loud in a lane, flatly, because a carter has asked whose sheet a cart is on, and about nine people heard a name in a lane at about the third hour and four did not know it.** The cart's load is three people off the coast road after eleven days and a woman of about thirty-eight asks whether she is to be an army or to stand there. He says he will not tell her which, and says why, and it is the first time the shape of the answer has come out before the question has finished. Chapter body 3,252.

**`chapter-0652.md`, 2,933 words.** Longlight the first, a Wednesday, and the first of a month, so the hearing stands in the street with about nine people. **Salla Dunnock, 38, of the coast of returning roads, is named, and the first thing she says is that she is not the one who holds the post.** The coast wrote in Mudmonth that it would send nobody and changed its mind in Hearthmonth for a reason given out loud. **The question is asked in the open: are we to be an army.** The badge-man's answer is that nobody has asked her anything and that the Crown has not asked her anything either, and that he has stood in that street for a year with about nine people in it and not one of them has ever been asked what a road is for. **And the copyist refuses to put the question in the register**, on the ground that it came from outside the nine counties and would be a tenth question with nobody able to say who had standing to ask it. Chapter body 2,334.

**`chapter-0653.md`, 2,582 words.** Longlight the fourth, a Saturday, a market morning, about four hundred people. **What a road is for, asked in the open and answered by the only person in nine counties who has counted one: not getting anywhere, but the people who cannot go round, and two numbers about the same road that are not one number and will not be made into one.** The badge-man refuses to make it into a rule, and says out loud that a question asked at the same time as you want it answered has already been softened. **Then a clerk of about thirty-one from the road office comes through the market and says there is a cart on the coast road with the originals in it, and that the district has told him to put up a notice and has not said where.** Chapter body 2,102.

**`chapter-0654.md`, 3,243 words.** Longlight the eighth, a Wednesday, the ferry stone at the low end of a road a woman has kept nineteen years. **Six men in the water putting a gauge on it, and a Crown officer of about thirty-one who says plainly that he is not shutting anything, and is marking where the water stops rather than where the road is, and says so out loud to a bridge carpenter because the carpenter said it first.** **Tovan Kest returns**, twenty-nine, with a barrow and a false baulm and about four men. **The first use of the kin-term in eleven years, in a refusal: give me the bar, brother, when you can tell me what the load is doing.** A baulm built wrong on purpose for a day the order names, a wedge, a stone on the end of it, and the reason stated as a length of road and a number. Chapter body 2,672.

**`chapter-0655.md`, 3,683 words.** Longlight the twelfth, a Sunday, the room shut, the boards, about nine people. **The sheet, read out in full, and the name at the top of it read out loud in a market street for the first time in about eleven years.** **The block's turn: the instrument shuts nothing, and the badge-man says so out loud and finds that not one of the three clauses is touched, because there is no road in it.** The three clauses are read aloud in the street by the people who hold them. **Aren Kest is asked to be the name a warrant goes in a place for, and says no, and gives the reason, and the badge-man says in a street that he is not going to ask twice.** **And he does not say the thing he has found, in front of about nine people, and the chapter states the price of not saying it.** The copyist refuses to put the sheet in any of her three series and says why, out loud. Chapter body 3,253.

**`chapter-0656.md`, 2,984 words.** Longlight the sixteenth, a Thursday, a market morning, the room open. **A road stood down for two days without being shut**, by a man of about forty-four who read the sheet and did the one thing that could not be argued with. **Mara Vey named in prose, on the page, saying that there is nothing anybody can bring the sheet to, and that the hole is that nine counties have nobody to ask.** The badge-man counts out loud that what he called about four months was twenty-one days, and says that being right early about a thing you were right about is the same as being wrong about how long you had. **Aren Kest is in the chapter throughout and says nothing at all, and the chapter says what that costs him.** Chapter body 2,579.

**`chapter-0657.md`, 3,336 words.** Longlight the twentieth, a Monday, and four miles in the dark to look at four marks and four miles back. **The marks are at the height of a thumb and they are where the water stops.** The crossing: **a foot of water at the third mark at about the seventh hour, the palm of the left hand warm at the fourth hour on the lane and again at the seventh hour in the water, the whole four hundred yards coming up warm for about a second and a half, and two things said out loud to a stone in the water and to nobody.** The ferry city's answer arrives, three weeks of contact and the conversion made exactly once, out loud, by a man with a sheet, and never again. **We will not come. We will not be moved. Do not ask us again this year**, and a city that was never severed does not take instruction from a paper about a city that was. Chapter body 2,890.

**`chapter-0658.md`, 2,830 words.** Longlight the twenty-fifth, a Saturday, a market morning, a wagon going out of the market town at the fifth hour with four people and nine hundredweight on it. Eleven marks for four days of a wagon in spate. **The cost: a man of about forty-nine asks the badge-man to send a rider, and the badge-man says no in about nine words, and says that none of the three clauses is about a man who sends a boy along a road.** **And the delegation goes home along the road that a Crown officer has been marking with four marks for about four days.** Tovan Kest puts two boards across the approach and leaves a stone on them. A clerk with a book writes down four persons going out on that road on that date, and nobody has asked him to keep it. Chapter body 2,188.

**`chapter-0659.md`, 2,845 words.** Rainmonth the second, a Saturday, a market morning, a wagon coming back empty with a boy of about nineteen on it. **The delegation went home on a road with the column on it, all morning, and the boy counted it from the tailboard and will not say the number of the ones that did not go past.** **The work at the ford, six men and a boy of about seventeen and a cart nobody sent, and Aren Kest barrowing on the off side and in the water in about eleven minutes.** The false baulm held and the hard ground printed a quarter of an inch, which is what Tovan Kest said it would do. **And in the room over a weigh-house, the only person he tells, who refuses to help him and says so and says why.** Chapter body 2,472.

**`chapter-0660.md`, 3,660 words.** Rainmonth the nineteenth, a Tuesday, the low end of the boards at the town's own gate, and the three courses of sill with the fourth course's stone still lying on the bank with the mallet on it. **Halvard Rees, 44, named, comes up the lane at about a walk and asks for the name of a road and is told there is no head in nine counties with which to give him one.** He breaks a seal at a board in the rain. **The copies on the boards are a quarter of the instrument and his is not, and the county has known it for a week and has said nothing.** He reads out that any person asked which place is meant shall answer, and says out loud, unprompted, that it is the right instrument and why. **Nine people turn round and look at Aren Kest and the badge-man says in a street that they are looking at him and is not going to ask him, and Aren Kest gives one true small thing about a ford and says nothing else.** Tovan's yard has taken a contract from the advance party. Chapter body 3,312.

## Volume 14, Batch 0002 — Chapters 661 to 670, "Where the Delegation Goes", days 2095 to 2148

**`chapter-0661.md`, 3,546 words.** Rainmonth the twentieth, a Wednesday, not a market morning, the room shut. **The batch's engine, reported first hand and not from the coast road: a woman of about forty-four walked two days in the wet to ask the copyist whether a Crown officer had the right to ask a person at a gate which place a paper meant, and the copyist reports that he wrote her answer down, read it back, and said that it was very good of her.** The badge-man's finding, out loud: a paper that asks nothing gets an answer anyway, because a man with a small book can stand at a gate and ask a question with no right in it and no wrong answer in it, and a person at a gate answers. **Both of the answers the delegation went home with are named and neither of them answers her question**, and the badge-man says so out loud about the truest thing he has said in a year. ****The badge-man declines to read the three clauses again and says he has got nothing and has had it since the seventh of Rainmonth, which is the change the review-repair pass made to this chapter**, and Aren Kest's only question is whether she was told to go. Chapter body 3,077.** Chapter body 3,077.

**`chapter-0662.md`, 3,760 words.** Rainmonth the twenty-third, a Saturday, a market morning, about four hundred people. **The first letter any of the seven roads has ever sent anybody in this county, and it is about a horse.** Carried by a man of about thirty-one nobody knows, and the copyist recognises the hand and refuses in the open to say where she has seen it, and gives one thing that is not where. The carrier is a man of about thirty-seven, not a second man of about thirty-one. **The letter asks for nothing and its last two lines are the only thing anybody wanted: we want somebody in this county to write down that there was a horse.** A boy of about eleven keeps the small road's count on a stick about the length of his arm, nine crossings open and four shut weeks in two years, and it is not converted into days by anybody including the man of about thirty-four with a sheet. **The turn, and it belongs to this chapter and to no other: the second clause is the one that is working.** A man in a livery asked a woman of about fifty-nine for a horse and said that it was very good of her before she had got the word out, and about four people in that market understood in about four seconds. **The copyist refuses the register, with perfect standing, and says why: a book that roads write to is a head at the top of a thing.** Chapter body 3,373.

**`chapter-0663.md`, 3,768 words.** Rainmonth the twenty-eight, a Thursday, a market morning, a market town of about nine hundred two days off. **Maud Trenchard, 56, named, the only new name this block spends, a Crown clerk of six years who drafted the instrument and has come down with a bag because a man in a livery wrote back that this county has not complied.** She names herself in a cold room to one witness and knows what a name at the top of a thing does inside a week. **Her hands are the chapter: nine pages of a fair copy, the right hand stiff, the last three pages written with the left, and the left is worse.** The clause she is honest about: stand taken from the hour of this sheet, which hour is the hour of the sealing of it. She was told it was a matter of form. **She cannot leave the fair copy anywhere, because a county is not a place a paper is kept**, and she will not make the woman of about twenty-nine the keeper of the Marshal's hand. Her want is said out loud: nothing I write is ever obeyed by anybody who has not chosen to obey it. Chapter body 2,987.

**`chapter-0664.md`, 3,445 words.** Harvestmonth the first, a Sunday, the first of a month, the hearing in the street with the room shut behind its own door. **The copyist keeps the promise of the twenty-third of Rainmonth and reads the road's letter out loud on the first of a month, in the rain, in front of about eleven people.** The woman of about thirty-six returns, and the badge-man asks her the question nobody in nine counties has ever asked anybody, which is whether anybody has ever offered her a job, and the answer is no and it costs her. **She has been offered work three times and said no three times, and all three were offers to go round with a list and get names on it, which is what her own county did for a year before the burning.** She names the second hole in the open and refuses to let it be called small, and she extends it: the paper has turned into a man, and a man at a gate can be answered, and the answer goes in a small book, and there is not one clause about a book. The badge-man confesses in the street that he asked her the wrong question and says he will not ask anybody anything for a month. Chapter body 3,122.

**`chapter-0665.md`, 3,265 words.** Harvestmonth the seventh, a Saturday, a market morning, the sixth hour on a mile and a half of lane that stops being made, about nine people and a boy of about thirteen at a barrel drain in white frost. **The volume becomes a march in fact: a culvert, a frost, a lime crust that breaks along the lime and not along the joint, a raised cart and a man of about fifty-one who does not want to be helped and is helped anyway, in the way that consent looks like between two people who have done it before.** Tovan Kest is four hundred yards up the made road on a contract from the advance party and the brothers say the standing of a barrow without the word. **The turn: a man in a livery asks the road's own people for a barrow, is given one, does not pay, does not think it needs paying for, and says that it was very good of them.** Four people in that lane understand in about four seconds what is about to happen to this county and nobody in that lane says anything. **The protagonist understands the finding is a craft and not a discovery, and a county can say no to a man and not to a craft.** A barrow goes up the lane with a man of about twenty-two on it. Chapter body 2,916.

**`chapter-0666.md`, 3,357 words.** Harvestmonth the fourteenth, a Saturday, a market morning, the fourth hour at a gate on a made road in the second county. **A man of about thirty-one, not the man who marked the ford, pegs a board on the inside of a gate kept by a woman of about fifty-eight in about four minutes and it does it correctly: the county, the gate described by its fields, the day, his own name, and one line recording that he asked which place or road was meant and that the person at the gate said she had not been told.** The word nobody in that lane says is not printed in the chapter. **The badge-man does not say it**, and says in the lane that he has not not said a thing since Thawmonth and has not got a good reason. Four people understand and five do not. **The woman of about thirty-six is offered the sentence she gave in a market street on the first of the month and declines to say it a second time in nine days, and says so out loud in the lane, and four people at that gate understand what has just been offered to her and five understand that a woman has declined to say something.** The man says there are forty-one gates on that made road and that another man in another county has a page for each of his own and that about four years from now a man will be asked to compare them. The copyist came two days to find out where a county keeps a paper and found out at a gate. Chapter body 3,055.

**`chapter-0667.md`, 3,367 words.** Harvestmonth the twentieth, a Friday, not a market morning. **The cost, and it is a person.** A barrow came down the lane in the dark on the seventh with one handle wet and a carter's mate of about twenty-two not on it, and it took nine people fourteen days to be able to say so out loud. Aren Kest walks nine miles and finds a waggon and four men who came from the coast side, and one of them says the lad came up the made road behind the crew at about the fourth hour of the afternoon and went on with them for about a mile, and that at about the seventh hour, when the crew turned off the coast road and went to quarters, the lad was not with them, and that this was not entered in the book, because there is nothing in the book for a boy on a road. **And then he corrects himself, out loud, and says that the half he has been asked about four times in a fortnight is the half he has not got, and that the barrow came down the lane without him and he did not see that either.** **The badge-man reads out nine reasons, in the open, on a Friday, and the ninth is that every one of the eight is a reason and not one of them is a person.** He asks one question and does not ask it twice, and says out loud that a man who asks a second time does not like the answer he has got. A woman of about forty-nine is not asked anything, and says so before anybody does. Chapter body 3,047.

**`chapter-0668.md`, 3,520 words.** Harvestmonth the twenty-sixth, a Thursday, a market morning, fourth hour to ninth at the market town's own gate. **The recovery beat, and it is long and it is ordinary labour in bad weather done by the people who keep the road.** A man of about fifty goes down into the culvert on his hands and knees with a lamp and establishes that the middle course is gone between two clean joints and that nobody lifted it out, and that it was laid in lime in a wet month by a man in a hurry, and that it was not a fault. **The sum is counted out loud at the boards and it foots on the page: two households at the low end of the lane at elevenpence each, three behind the weigh-house at twopence each, three pennies from the four that keep the two yards, and one of those four gives nothing and says so and is not argued with. Twenty-two, six and three is thirty-one pence.** **The fourth course stays unlaid and the mallet stays on the stone and the badge-man says in the open why he is not going to ask the man of about fifty about it in a street.** The protagonist is on the bucket end for ninety barrows and has the first thing put in front of him in a year that he had not already had. Chapter body 3,158.

**`chapter-0669.md`, 3,433 words.** Fallowmonth the fifth, a Saturday, a market morning, about four hundred people. **The third thing, and it is worse for this county than an army.** A woman of about forty-four who keeps a crossing four hundred miles off comes through on her way back and says her city has resolved that if anybody in nine counties asks which place a paper is meant, the city will answer and will name the place and will not refuse to name it, and that nobody asked it and it did not ask. **It is not an army and it is not a refusal to be an army, and a city that was never severed does not take instruction from a paper about a city that was is now going to be talked about in a room nine counties cannot get into.** The badge-man says that nine counties cannot refuse a thing a city says about it. **The protagonist is four miles down the lane and is told at about the sixth hour by a man who says he did not send for him and would not have, and works out that he is about five hours late for the market and on time for the crossing, and that there is no arrangement that would have put him in the room where a county thinks.** A question is asked in that market and nobody answers it: whether to ask them. Chapter body 3,095.

**`chapter-0670.md`, 3,526 words.** Fallowmonth the thirteenth, a Sunday, not a market morning. **The climax, and the block's cost, and a decision.** The Saturday's question at the boards in front of about four hundred people, with the serjeant's line entered as **asked at the boards, the twelfth of Fallowmonth, and no place given**, which is the Saturday, is set down first. **A chain goes across the ford at about the seventh hour and the captain comes down the bank on foot and says four things.** The one that matters: a thing that is shut has an end to it and a thing that is taken has no end to it and no moment. He admits he has looked for nine days for something in the three clauses that stops him and did not find it. **And the finding is that none of the three is broken and the ford is shut, because the clauses are about a man who proposes and a man carrying out a paper is not a man who proposes.** **The decision is the woman of about fifty's, and it is the batch's last beat: nobody had thought to ask her whether she wanted to give the name of her own four miles, and she gives it out loud, in the rain, before about nine people, and says the cost of it out loud too, that a road with a name can be argued with and a road without one can only be taken.** The captain writes it down and reads it back. Nobody thanked anybody. **The block's last page is not a rerun of Chapter 0668's: the woman of about fifty stops half a mile up the road at a gate where the water comes over the crown, puts the lamp down on the dry side, stands in four inches of water, and says out loud that there has never been a name on that road and that there is one now, and that the difference is that in about four years a man will be able to say she had been told and she will not be able to say she had not.** Chapter body 3,047.

**`chapter-0671.md`, 3,231 words.** Fallowmonth the nineteenth, a Saturday, a market morning, the second hour at the boards, four hundred people in the street and about two hundred stopped. **The decision to ask, and it is a decision and not an errand.** A woman of about thirty-eight of the coast of returning roads, named as a return, comes up the made road on the Wednesday and names the four people in nine counties who could get to a crossing four hundred miles off, and **three of the four say no out loud in that market street, and the reason none of them will go is the reason nobody can give a name to, which is that there is no head with which to send anybody and sending is making one.** The badge-man says it in nine words. **A chandler of about thirty-three says out loud that the hearing cannot ask a question, and a man of about twenty-nine who keeps the inn says that is the first time anybody has said the hearing is the wrong size for a thing.** The woman works out nine words that ask for nothing and that a man in a livery cannot write down as consent. **Then she says she will go, and nobody in that street had agreed with it before she said it and nobody agreed with it afterwards.** Nobody thanked anybody. Chapter body 3,012.

**`chapter-0672.md`, 3,188 words.** Fallowmonth the twenty-fourth, a Thursday, the second hour four miles up the made road, where four bridge crews are working forward. **A person goes to a crossing, and it is not a delegation and not a rider and not a man with a badge.** Salla Dunnock goes out of the market town on the Monday with a cart and a carter of about forty-one and about nine days of road, and **about four of the nine who watched her go understand where she is going and about five do not, because there is nothing four days up that road.** The second clause is on the road in a man's mouth, in about two hundred words, and it takes about nine seconds and it is lawful: a man of thirty asks her for her own cart out of her own gateway, tells her the whole of what he has, and she says yes without stopping, **and she is the woman who said in a market street four days before that a man in a livery writes down what was said and not what was meant.** She stops on the made road at about the fourth hour and says the nine words out loud to nobody once. Chapter body 2,905.

**`chapter-0673.md`, 3,425 words.** Fallowmonth the twenty-ninth, a Tuesday, not a market morning, the room over the weigh-house shut behind its own door, and **the chapter is the writing.** A man of thirty-two writes about nine hundred words in nine numbered clauses at a settle in a full inn over five hours. **It is the best thing anybody in nine counties has written in about a year and it is competent and it is specific and about four of the nine people in that room can see the shape of it and about five can only hear the words.** It requires the seven roads to accept Crown authority for the length of the emergency, in writing, with a named person on each side, **and it says in the ninth clause why that is the only shape, and the reason is good.** He gives it to nobody: not to the woman who keeps a room, not to the copyist, not to the man with a badge, **because a man with a badge who carries nine hundred words is a head.** The copyist says out loud that the three series cannot carry it and that the fourth ruled line is the one for it and she is not going to rule it. Nobody was thanked and nobody was asked to sign it. Chapter body 3,138.

**`chapter-0674.md`, 3,208 words.** Embermonth the first, a Thursday, a market morning, the first of a month, the hearing in the street, four hundred people and about two hundred stopped. **The counter, and it is not a person who hates him.** Nine clauses are read out loud at the boards and again in the hired room over a saddler's and again in a lane in the wet. **The copyist does not read the letter for a third time and says why in nine words.** A woman of about thirty-six agrees with eight of the nine, gives the number of the one she does not, **and says the clause cannot be complied with by a road that does not take orders.** The answer is on the page and the answer is worse than the objection: **the badge-man says in nine words that a person who can be found afterwards is what the burden has been waiting four and a half years for and that this county has just written nine hundred words handing it over.** The plan is not withdrawn. A man of about fifty-one at the top of a cut says he worked it out at about the fourth hour, before he read it. Chapter body 2,916.

**`chapter-0675.md`, 3,596 words.** Embermonth the eighth, a Thursday, a market morning, the third hour at the boards. **THE STANDING DEFECT PAYS ITS SECOND HALF, AND IT PAYS TWICE.** A printed list of about thirty counties in order with one road against each, read out loud in a market street, with a name at the top of it printed once, and the badge-man says in nine words that he has not said that name in this street in about eleven years. **The captain says three things and the third is that a list does not let him be wrong about anything.** The woman of about thirty-six says it is the same shape as the ford. **The badge-man does not move the figure and says that being early was a kindness. A man of about thirty-three asks the question out loud and the woman of about fifty answers it, and the answer is that it was a kindness and it was to him, and that the twenty-one days went on a rule that has taken a horse and a barrow and not one man with a paper.** The woman of about fifty says afterwards that she got her own four miles wrong and gave it anyway. Nobody thanked anybody. Chapter body 3,349.

**`chapter-0676.md`, 3,648 words.** Embermonth the tenth, a Saturday, a market morning, the fourth hour in the hired room over a saddler's. **The midpoint reversal.** A woman of about thirty-four who is a ferrywright and keeps a crossing and the approaches to it walks nine days into a room with about nine people in it and stands up before anyone has asked her anything. She names herself as a name from about eleven years ago and a city that was never severed. **She says four sentences about a gate, and they are the reversal: a road cannot be told anything because there is nobody at the end of it to be told, so the day one of seven roads puts a name on a paper a man has a name to say at a gate for the rest of his life, and that is what a Sundering is.** The protagonist answers her question at length first and is wrong and does not know it. **He does not ask her to say it again or to put it another way and she does not soften it and one sentence each is on the page.** He leaves the nine hundred words on the table and does not take them. Nobody thanked anybody. Chapter body 3,426.
