The man of about thirty-eight who puts boards on the inside of gates says the word nine and that nobody has ever asked him to use it about a road. **The ordinary half is at full volume, and the ninth mile stone is cleaned by one man about nine hundred times a year, and about four people in that street worked out that a drove went up that lane on the Thursday before and about five had not.** The broom is five days off.

**`chapter-0723.md`, 3,469 words.** Longlight the thirtieth, YR 322, a Sunday and not a market morning, the room shut, the last day of a month, four days out at the head of the made road. **The ninth question about that lane, and the first of the nine anybody in nine counties could answer.** Somebody asks, out loud, kindly, whether anybody is going up before the broom is on, and the man with the satchel says out loud that he does not know and that he is the man who says the finding of things in that market town and that he does not know. **A young man of about nineteen buying twine at the chandler's nine doors down is asked whether he is going up on the thirty-first or on the first of Rainmonth and says no, and then says in forty-two words that he is not going up on the thirty first or on the first and is not going up on the second, and that there is a broom on the fourth and it is the only broom within a day of his yard.** The man of about forty-one has carried about sixty loads of stone two miles on his own in eighty-eight days. The hives stand at three frames of brood at a hundred and twelve days. About nine people were in that market street and four at some boards.

**`chapter-0724.md`, 3,424 words.** Rainmonth the first, YR 322, a Monday and not a market morning and the first of a month, the room shut behind its own door and the bolt still on from the Saturday, about four people at the boards, three days to a broom. **The seventh first of a month in a row with nothing at those boards.** The roadkeeper says out loud, in his own trade, forty-four words: **he is not going up before the broom is on and nobody is going to send him and there is nothing in this county that would make him, and he said on the first of Longlight that he would go and he is going.** A question is asked on the made road four hundred yards from the town, kindly, about whether the young man is going to have any help with it, and nobody answers it. **The young man then says it out loud in that market town at the eleventh hour, in his own trade, forty-eight words: I am going up on my own account on Thursday and I will cut what four bundles of twine will hold and I will carry four bundles down two miles of lane and I am not asking anybody for anything and I have not got anybody to ask.** Nobody thanked him and nobody sent anybody. **Nine people in nine counties have said since that this is the first time anybody in this county has said out loud that he has got nobody to ask, and about four people have said that a man who says that in a street is asking everybody in it and about five have said that he did not say it in a street.**

**`chapter-0725.md`, 3,438 words.** Rainmonth the fourth, YR 322, a Thursday and a market morning, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open. **The broom, and the block's gift.** The young man of about nineteen is in that street at the fifth hour with about nine yards of tarred twine over one shoulder and a sickle and about nine feet of new waxed cord, and says out loud, in his own trade, thirty-nine words: **the broom is on the east rise this morning and I am going up at six for it and I am going on my own account and I am not waiting for anybody and there is nothing to arrange.** Nobody said anything back and nobody thanked him and nobody sent anybody with him. **This is the first time in about four and a half years that anybody in this county has said out loud in a market street that they are going up that lane for the thing the lane is actually for, and the thing is a broom.** The cloth seller refuses to be asked about a broom a third time in forty words. Asked kindly what he will do with the rest of it, the young man says out loud in fifty-six words that he will cut what four bundles of twine will hold, tie the rest in nine bundles, leave them on the stones for whoever wants them, and that there has been one bundle left there every year since he was eleven and it is never the same man. **At the sixth hour the drove goes out through the gap and up about nine hundred yards and stands in the field, and the young man goes up with the drove, and the man of thirty-two goes out of that market town up the made road at the same hour, the way he does every morning of the year. Nobody thanked anybody and nobody sent anybody.**

**`chapter-0726.md`, 3,836 words.** Rainmonth the eighth, YR 322, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut, four days on a shoulder above a drove road with about nine acres of broom on the east rise about four hundred yards up. **The finding, in his own order, and the midpoint reversal, in one paragraph.** Two people are on that shoulder and they are not a party. The drove came back down at about the fifth hour of the fourth and neither of them went with it. **The young man stops cutting at about the seventh hour on the fourth day and says three things out loud, in the open, to one person, in his own trade: sixty-nine words on what is on the flat, seventy-two words on why the line is nine feet wide, and seventy-eight words on who keeps it. The third of the three is that the nine households past those nine stones keep a rota of that flat that has been kept about nine years and has not been broken once in the nine years, and the grass on the road is cut with the flat and not as a road, and nobody else cuts any of it, and the kerb is older than the cutting and nobody in nine counties can say by how much.** Asked out loud what it is for, the answer is thirty-four words: **it is not for anything, it is a boundary first and it is nine hundred yards of it.** **Then at about the fourth hour of the fourth day the left palm of the man of thirty-two was warm, and there was a thing at it that would have taken him to about the ninth hour of a night he cannot put a year to, and he looked at the ground at his own feet for about as long as it takes to turn a cart, and it was a transfer and he witnessed it and did not conduct it, and he told nobody and nobody on that shoulder noticed anything.** Nobody crossed the nine stones. Four bundles were tied on the seventh evening.

**`chapter-0727.md`, 3,365 words.** Rainmonth the thirteenth, YR 322, a Saturday and a market morning, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open. **The ninth thing anybody in nine counties has said out loud about that lane, and it is said in a market street by the man who said it on a shoulder, and he says it a second time and not in the same words.** Nine people took a broom or a bee brush off a board with nothing written on it and about five did not. Asked kindly where the broom came from, the young man says out loud in forty-seven words that it is off the east rise, that he cut it on Thursday with a sickle, that he carried it down in four bundles and a man with a bad leg carried two of them. **Then he says three things out loud to about two hundred people, and the first of them is a place where the kerb stops, and about four people in that street worked out inside about nine seconds that it answers a question asked in that street on the twenty-ninth of Longlight and about five did not.** The cloth seller says out loud that there is a man in this street who will not sell a yard of anything to anybody who has not got the money and that he is one of them. **The third of the three is seventy-six words and it is not the sentence he said on the shoulder, and it is the reason there is no claim on that road: he has watched nine households cut that flat for nine years from the top of it and is the only one who has watched it from the top and not from a gate, and the cutting is not for the road, it is for beasts, and the kerb was under the grass before the first of them ever cut, and that is the whole reason nobody has ever put a hand on that road.** The man of about thirty-four with a satchel says out loud in fifty-seven words that it is a young man's account and not his, that he has said nine things out loud in this county in about four and a half years, and that he is not going to say the tenth and is not going to say this one twice. The roadkeeper says out loud that about nine hundred yards of a line in the grass is a road a man mows and that he could not tell anybody who keeps the other. **About nine hundred yards of an old road is now in about four hundred mouths and in nobody's hand.**

**`chapter-0728.md`, 3,498 words.** Rainmonth the seventeenth, YR 322, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut, four days out at the head of the made road where about three yards of a wall is down. **The broom is used, and that is the half of the finding the finding was not.** There is a broom standing in the house of the man of about forty-one and he did not buy it and nobody has said that he did and nobody has said that he did not. A question is asked out loud on a made road, kindly, about where that broom came from, and nobody in that house answers it. **The young man of about nineteen says out loud in his own yard fifty-seven words: I have nine frames of wood and about nine feet of wax and I make nine frames in about two days and I do not make ten, and I am not going to make more bees than the bees I have got, and the three frames I have are the three frames I am going to have.** Nobody came up that lane between the eighth of Rainmonth and the seventeenth, and a third household did not come and nobody asked it for a reason. **The man of about thirty-two stands about nine feet off that gap for about as long as it takes to turn a cart and does not help and does not ask and does not go up the lane, and the man of about forty-one comes to the head of the gap and does not watch him and goes back in, and neither of them said anything.** Nine yards of wall are still down and the stone has been carried two miles on his own for a hundred and five days.

**`chapter-0729.md`, 3,401 words.** Harvestmonth the first, YR 322, a Wednesday and not a market morning and the first of a month, the room shut, about four people at the boards. **A finding about nine hundred yards of an old road that is in nobody's hand.** The badge-man comes down at a slow walk, puts his hand flat on the wet rail, and says out loud in seventy-eight words: **I have three clauses in nine hands with no copy of them and they are about a man doing a thing to a road, and a paper does not do a thing to a road, and there is no clause in nine hands about a road that nobody has done a thing to.** Asked kindly what he calls it, he says out loud in forty-six words: **I call it a road and I have called it a road in nine hands and nobody has ever heard me use the word. That is the whole of what I have got and I have had it since the middle of Longlight the year before.** The roadkeeper says out loud in fifty-eight words that there are nine clerks in the road office in this county, that about nine hundred yards of that old road has been in this county's mouth for eighteen days, and that nobody in nine counties has any way of putting it in front of a clerk, that he has not asked one and he is not going to. **Nobody in that market street, on that made road, or in nine counties has asked the man of thirty-two one word about the fourth of Rainmonth or about the fourth hour of the fourth day, and nobody is going to.**

**`chapter-0730.md`, 3,524 words.** Harvestmonth the twenty-fifth, YR 322, a Saturday and a market morning, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open. **The block's last page, and it is two trades pricing about four and a half years out loud, and it is not the shoulder and not the stones and not the broom.** The roadkeeper says out loud in seventy-eight words that about four and a half years of this county's business has produced about nine hundred yards of a road on a hilltop and a hearing that has made nothing, and that he mends nine yards of wall in about a hundred and forty-three days because a wall has to be mended, and that he has got no trade at all for nine hundred yards of a road and that is not a roadkeeper's job. **The cloth seller answers in eighty-one words that it has produced about four hundred people who have heard nine things and nobody anybody could find about any of them, and that he can find a person who has heard a thing in about nine seconds and has never once found one who has not heard it, and that a finding in four hundred mouths is worth four hundred shops and three clauses in nine hands with no copy are worth nothing.** The badge-man says out loud in fifty-nine words that not one of his three clauses is about a road nobody has done a thing to, that he said that on the first of Harvestmonth in this street and is not going to say it twice, and that there are two trades in it today and he is the third. Asked out loud what happened to the nine bundles, the young man says in fifty-four words that eight of them were gone on the ninth of Rainmonth and the ninth was on the stones with the twine cut off it in nine places and the stalk end split, and that this is how you tell whether a man has cut a bundle or taken it, and that nobody has ever left one whole. **And then the question the reader is left with is the one the protagonist cannot ask, and it is whether nine households that have cut the grass along nine hundred yards of a road for about nine years without knowing that the thing they were cutting was a road are a thing anybody in nine counties can be asked a question about, and he worked it out at about the tenth hour at the sea end of that market street and did not say it out loud, because asking it would be making a claim, and a man with no standing who makes a claim about nine people who have never been asked anything about anything has made nine people into a body, and those nine households are not a body and have never been asked to be one and keep a rota about beasts between them and not a head and not a paper. Nobody thanked anybody.**

## Volume 15, Batch 0004 — Chapters 731 to 740, "Asked, and Refused Twice"

**`chapter-0731.md`, 3,312 words.** Harvestmonth the thirtieth, YR 322, a Thursday and a market morning, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open. **The block opens on a man saying out loud that he is going to ask two people for one thing, and naming nobody.** He says it in forty-two words and nobody asks him what the thing is or who the two people are. A cart with paper in it goes up the made road on the Wednesday and does not stop at the weigh-house. The drove goes out that morning and that is twenty-two drove since the second of Mudmonth, and eight of nine yards of wall are back in, and the brood stands at three frames, and the broom in the house of the man of forty-one has been standing there since the eighth of Rainmonth. A person at the third door asks him, kindly, whether it is about a road, and he says in thirty-three words that it is about him and that it is the first thing he has wanted in four and a half years.

**`chapter-0732.md`, 3,183 words.** Fallowmonth the sixth, YR 322, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, four days out at the head of the made road. **The first of Fallowmonth came and went with nothing at the boards and that is the ninth first of a month in a row, and two men go up the lane on a Thursday behind a drove and neither of them knew the other was going.** The man of about fifty-four who keeps the far end of that lane comes up behind the drove at the seventh hour with a stick and nine feet of new cord, and the two of them stand nine hundred yards apart on a shoulder and say about a hundred words. **This chapter decides, on the page, that nothing on the far side of the nine stones is to be shown in this block and why.** The fourth frame in the walled yard was drawn on the second of Fallowmonth and about a third of it is brood, and the young man put it in because it was drawn and he is not going to make more bees than the bees he has got.

**`chapter-0733.md`, 3,308 words.** Fallowmonth the fourteenth, YR 322, a Thursday and a market morning with no market on the made road, the sixth hour at the head of that road, a day on a shoulder. **THE FIRST OF THE TWO REFUSALS.** The man of thirty-two asks in twenty-six words for one thing. The man who keeps the far end of that lane refuses in eighty-five words with his reason inside the same breath, and says among other things that he cannot answer for nine people and is not going to be asked to be a head. The acceptance is thirty-one words and takes about as long as it takes to turn a cart. **Nobody put a foot past the ninth of the nine stones and nothing on the other side of them was seen.** About a hundred and seventy words were said on that shoulder that day, which is the most that has ever been said on it in one day. The tenth question anybody in nine counties has asked about that lane is asked by the man who has just been refused at it and he answers his own question in thirteen words.

**`chapter-0734.md`, 3,224 words.** Fallowmonth the twenty-second, YR 322, a Friday and not a market morning, the room shut, four days out. **Eight days after a refusal, a man of thirty-two says out loud that a refusal belongs to the person who gave it and not to the road, and says that he has one thing left to ask for.** The brood is at four frames and the young man says out loud in his own walled yard that the fourth frame was drawn on the second of Fallowmonth and that he put it in because it was drawn. The man of about fifty-two who keeps four miles of made road says nothing at all that day, and about four people in nine counties have said since that a man who has said out loud that he is not going to ask is not the man anybody looks for. **Nobody asked the man of about forty-one a second question about a road and nobody is going to.**

**`chapter-0735.md`, 3,176 words.** Fallowmonth the twenty-sixth, YR 322, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut, nine people in the market street. **THE SECOND OF THE TWO REFUSALS, AND IT IS FOUR SENTENCES.** A clerk of that county's road office of about forty-nine comes down a lane on foot and puts no courtesy at anything. The same one thing is asked of him in fifty-three words, and he refuses in thirty-four words: **No. That is not a thing this office has an entry for. I will write down that a man said it out loud in a market street. Nothing will be done with the paper.** The acceptance is twenty-eight words. He then says in twenty-seven words that he is not going to put it in nine hands and that there is nowhere in nine counties he could put it, and one sheet of paper goes into that office with one line on it. Nobody argued and nobody thanked him and about four people in that street worked out that neither of the two men refused him.

**`chapter-0736.md`, 3,433 words.** Fallowmonth the twenty-eightth, YR 322, a Thursday and a market morning, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open. **THE FORTY-FIVE WORDS THAT END IT, AND THE BADGE-MAN WATCHES AND SAYS NOTHING.** He says out loud that he has asked two people in two places in twelve days for the same one thing and that both have said no and that he is not going to ask either of them again and is not going to argue with either of them, and about two hundred people in that street heard it and nine stopped what they were doing. Nobody argued with him and nobody thanked him and about four people worked out inside about nine seconds that a man who says he is not going to argue has just been asked by about two hundred people to. A roadkeeper puts one line on one sheet in four days next to about nine hundred yards of a road and calls the ratio this county. The badge-man is at the high end of those boards from the fourth hour to the ninth and says nothing at all, and nobody has thought of a question for him.

**`chapter-0737.md`, 3,618 words.** Embermonth the sixth, YR 322, a Friday and not a market morning, the room shut, four days out. **The reason he gives, out loud, for not going past the ninth of the stones and not looking over them from a distance either: that the man who keeps that lane said no to him and a no is not a thing you can go round.** Forty-eight words, said in the open to about four people. At about the fourth hour the left palm of the man of thirty-two was warm and there was a thing at it, and he stood with his hand open and looked at the ground at his own feet, and he did not take it, and he has not said out loud that he is never going to take it, and nobody noticed the palm and nobody has asked him about it and nobody is going to. A person asks him, kindly, whether he is going to try them again, and he says in thirty-four words that he said out loud in a market street that he is not going to ask either of them again.

**`chapter-0738.md`, 3,285 words.** Embermonth the nineteenth, YR 322, a Thursday and a market morning, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open. **THE QUESTION NOBODY ASKED IN BLOCK 0003 ARRIVES, AND IT ARRIVES FROM SOMEBODY WITH STANDING, AND THE ANSWER IS NO.** A badge-man of about fifty-eight lays his hand flat on the top rail and asks, out loud, in the open, to about four hundred people, his first question in this county in about four and a half years, in 113 words, and it is about holding a thing and not about a road. The man of thirty-two answers in fifty-three words with his left hand hanging where anybody could see it: **No. I am not going to hold it and I am not going to give it to anybody, and there is a thing at my left hand and I am not going to use it, and I am not going to say what it is and you are not going to ask me.** **What the no cost him is not the thing. The thing is still there, it is still warm about four days in seven, he has not said he will never use it, and one other person in nine counties now knows there is something at that palm because he chose not to conceal it and not to explain it, which is a new act and not the one on the shoulder.** Nobody argued, nobody thanked him, and nobody asked him a second question.

**`chapter-0739.md`, 3,435 words.** Embermonth the twenty-first, YR 322, a Saturday and a market morning and the last one before the twenty-fifth of that month, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open. **The fifth frame of brood comes in, and the block says plainly that this is a trade and not a finding.** A roadkeeper puts two refusals and nine hundred yards of a road and a winter in one sentence of fifty-three words and says that not one of the three can go on a contract. The man who keeps the weigh-house asks, kindly, whether any of it is going in a book, and is told in forty-six words that there is nowhere in nine counties to put it and that the one room that could take it takes nothing and has ten questions on its register and no finding under any of them and that this is not his to fill. **The part of that man of thirty-two which was going to come home to him on the fourth of Rainmonth is not going to come home, and nobody has consoled him and nobody has thanked him, and the narration gets it in four sentences.**

**`chapter-0740.md`, 3,084 words.** Embermonth the twenty-fifth, YR 322, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, four days out. **THE BLOCK'S LAST PAGE, AND IT IS NOT THE TWO COMMUNITIES AND IT IS NOT THE ROAD.** He has been refused twice in the open by two people with standing, once on a shoulder and once in a market street four days off, and he has taken both and told nobody about either except the market street he said it in. He has given one thing away in the open as well, the fact of a thing at his left hand, and has not said out loud that he is never going to use it. **A county with about four hundred people who have heard nine things and about nine who have said them has just acquired the first thing anybody in nine counties has ever been refused out loud twice and has no way of holding that either.** A person asks, kindly, whether he is going back up the lane, and he says in fourteen words that nobody is going up that lane about this and he is not going either. The last of it is a list of six things, and nobody in nine counties has been thanked for any of the six.

**THE BATCH 0004 REVIEW REPAIR PASS, AND THE TEN WORD COUNTS ABOVE ARE THE ONES ON THE FILES.** Batch 0004 was reviewed after it closed and eleven defects were repaired on the page, and the full record is `state/continuity.md` 1681 and `state/open-threads.md` 672. **No chapter was rewritten, no paragraph of scene prose was replaced, no event and no beat moved, and the diff is fourteen changed lines across seven files. The per-chapter word counts above were re-taken after the repairs and five of the ten moved: 0731 3,312, 0734 3,224, 0739 3,435 and 0740 3,084 are not what they were, and 0732, 0733, 0735, 0736, 0737 and 0738 did not move at all. The block is 33,058 against the 33,064 printed before the pass.** What each repair was, by file: **0731** the interval since the thirteenth of Rainmonth is forty-seven days at two places where the phase had printed forty-six, and the repaint of the boards is left undated because a repaint cannot fall in a Hearthmonth. **0734** the same repaint clause is removed for the same reason and nothing else moved. **0737** the inn reads *four beds empty for the tenth time since the middle of Harvestmonth*, against third and ninth in the two files before it, so the running series is third, ninth, tenth. **0738** the census opener no longer calls him the fourth person in this county to be refused and says second, which is what two refusals support; the interval since the thirteenth of Rainmonth is ninety-six days at two places where ninety-five stood, which had contradicted the thirty-five and twenty-three printed correctly in the same sentence; the narration at the cost beat refers to the fifty-three words he actually said and not to a forty-four-word utterance that does not exist; and the brood figure has not moved in about eleven weeks and not nine. **0739** four sentences that ended on a comma are finished and four conjunctions that opened a sentence in lower case after a full stop are put back into their own sentence, the owners-of-two-miles clause is lifted out of the sea paragraph where it was an orphan and set at the end of the broom-and-orchard paragraph it belongs to, the four hundred people going out of the street is stated once instead of twice inside one paragraph, and the interval since the thirteenth of Rainmonth is ninety-eight days and not ninety-seven. **0740** the standing broom is at about a hundred and ten days and not a hundred and twenty, and the two people who have never spoken now have been in one building and not in one room more than once in four and a half years, which the block supports, in place of a count of about forty days of this year that nothing on the page supported. **The two findings the pass rejected are argued at 1681: *four and a half years* stands, because it is the standing figure of the arrangement and is printed some two hundred times across the volume, and it is not an unsourced invention.**

## Volume 15, Batch 0005 — Chapters 741 to 750, "The Mapped Scar"

**`chapter-0741.md`, 3,305 words.** Embermonth the twenty-sixth, YR 322, a Thursday and a market morning, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open. **THE BLOCK OPENS ON A LIGHT.** At about the fourth hour, four days off, at the head of that made road and before the light, the top of that shoulder lit up for about as long as it takes to turn a cart twice over, along the line and not across it, from the ninth of the nine stones down about nine hundred yards to where the kerbed road begins. It was not a fire. He was the only one who saw it, he did not go up, and he told nobody that day. The broom is off the hill, nine days of nine acres cut, about nine hundred yards of a nine-foot line left standing. The drove went out that morning and that is thirty drove since the second of Mudmonth. Eight of nine yards of wall are back in and the man carrying stone is at two hundred and four days. A person at the third door asks him, kindly, whether he has been up the hill, and he says in thirty-two words that he has been on that made road every day since the twenty-sixth of Mudmonth and has not been up that hill since the fourteenth of Fallowmonth and is not going.

**`chapter-0742.md`, 3,250 words.** Wolfmonth the first, YR 322, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, nine people in that market street. **THE ASKING, FOUR PEOPLE, FOUR TRADES, AND THE COST IS THE ASKING.** The first of a month comes and goes with nothing at the boards and that is the eleventh in a row. At about the fourth, fifth, sixth and seventh hours he asks the roadkeeper of about fifty-two (48 words, answered in 39 and he will not be thanked), the young man of about nineteen (37 words, answered in 35 and he will not be thanked), the woman of about fifty-one who keeps the inn (40 words, answered in 43, she wants a fire laid for four people and not nine), and the man of forty-one (40 words, **and he does not answer, which is not a refusal**). The second of those answers holds its own door open at the fourth hour of every morning of this year. A person at the fifth door asks, kindly, whether anybody else is going up, and he says in thirty-two words that he has asked four people in four trades, three said yes in their own trade and one has not said anything, and that is all he has.

**`chapter-0743.md`, 3,126 words.** Wolfmonth the fifth, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open. Eight of nine yards of wall are back in and the man carrying stone is at two hundred and thirteen days. **THE MAP EXISTS.** Four people stand at the head of that made road at the fourth hour and see all nine hundred yards of it at the same time, and one of them is the roadkeeper. At about the fourth hour of that market morning he puts the back of his board up on the low step of that weigh-house, about nine of four hundred people come and look, and he says in thirty-five words that he has drawn nine hundred yards in charcoal, is not going to draw it again and is not going to give the board away. **The board is a plan of his own four miles, and the coping of nine hundred yards of wall with the gate in it stands on it at the head of those four miles, and nobody in nine counties has ever asked him why a roadkeeper's board goes nine hundred yards above his own four miles.** A person at the third door asks whether that is the hill he said he would not go up, and he says in fifty-one words that four of them have seen it once at the same time and one has seen it ten times, and that he is going up it with three people on the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth and not because anybody has seen anything on it twice.

**`chapter-0744.md`, 3,007 words.** Wolfmonth the ninth, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut, nine people in that street. **A FACT FOUR PEOPLE HAVE EACH SEEN ONCE IS NOT ONE THING FOUR TIMES.** The light has been on fourteen mornings and four people have seen it and nobody else has. Then **two carters measure the same wall two ways in one morning in a market street**: one came over it on the Monday with nine hundredweight of wool and the near end is nine feet and the far end is about knee high on a cart, and the other came over it with eleven sacks and every yard is nine feet and he has laid his hand on the coping at the far end. A person at the fifth door asks, kindly, which of them is right, and the man of thirty-two answers in forty-seven words that both of them are right and that he has put his hand on that coping at both ends and is not going to say one of them is wrong, and that a thing two men can measure two ways is not a thing anybody in this county can hold.

**`chapter-0745.md`, 3,613 words.** Wolfmonth the seventeenth, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open. **TWO DOCUMENTS ARE READ OUT ALOUD AT THE BOARDS AND THE ANTAGONIST'S NAME IS PRINTED FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THE VOLUME.** A Crown's cart came up four days of made road on the Monday before and stopped outside that office for two days. The copyist of about twenty-six reads a printed notice out in the open to about four hundred people, and one line of it is thirty-one words and says that the returning road at the sixth post is shut from the twentieth of Frostmonth and no cart shall cross it and no keeper shall be paid for it after that day. **The last line of that sheet is twenty-one words and it is the volume's first printing of the name: Given under the Marshal's hand, Edric Hunn, at the office four hundred miles off and sent down by the ordinary post.** Then the clerk of about forty-nine reads out a leaf with one thumb on it, seventeen words: **We shut our own end before Hearthmonth and we are not asking anybody for anything about it.** Nobody stopped either and nobody thanked anybody. A person at the third door asks whether that is our road, and he says in forty words that it is on a coast four hundred miles off and nobody in this county has ever been on it and nobody in this county is going to be told a thing about it before the twentieth of Frostmonth.

**`chapter-0746.md`, 3,268 words.** Wolfmonth the twenty-fifth, a Friday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door. **THE MANY-HANDED IS CROSSED AND THE VOLUME'S ONE SYSTEM PANEL IS SPENT, AND THE ASKING IS WHAT MAKES IT COST.** At about the fourth hour he stands on the grass four hundred paces below the mapped ground with the roadkeeper on his right and the beekeeper on his left and the woman of about fifty-one nine feet behind him, and the man of forty-one is at the gate below and has said nothing at all and has not come up. He asks in twenty-eight words for the three of them to hold one each, and then one thing comes into him and nobody else on that shoulder hears anything, and it stands alone as a paragraph and carries no number: **What is held by one hand is taken by one hand.** Three answer in thirty-nine, forty-one and forty-four words and each says in its own trade that it will not be thanked for it. That is ten, and he can tell the ten apart by the weather on them, and eight have somebody awake at the other end and two do not. **THE LIGHT GOES OUT FROM THE LOW END UPWARD AND LEAVES NINE HUNDRED YARDS OF DEAD GRASS NINE FEET WIDE, and it is not a fire and nothing is burnt on it and nobody has put it back.** **THE COST: he cannot get off that grass on the first attempt and has to put the flat of his stick down, and when the roadkeeper says one thing to him from his right he does not hear one word of it and says out loud, in six words, Say that again on the left.** The dead grass stops at the ninth of the nine stones.

**`chapter-0747.md`, 3,065 words.** Frostmonth the first, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open, a twelfth first of a month with nothing at those boards. **TWO PEOPLE WALK THE BOUNDARY IN THEIR OWN TRADE AND NEITHER OF THEM WAS ASKED.** A man of about fifty-one and a woman of about forty-four come up behind the drove on the Wednesday before and walk nine hundred yards of the nine-foot line from the low end to the ninth of the nine stones and stop there. He says in fifty-five words that the two of them cut that grass, that the other seven have stock and stock does not need a boundary that is showing, and that a boundary that is not showing is a boundary somebody will be asked about. She says in fifty-four words that they have not been past the ninth of those stones and are not going to, that nobody has asked them to, and that they are two and not nine because nine would be a thing agreed. **A person asks, kindly, whether it is on a map, and he says in forty-nine words that it is on the back of a roadkeeper's board in a cart shed and about nine people have seen it and it is not on anything else in nine counties and he is not going to ask that man for his board.** The drove went out that morning and that is thirty-five drove since the second of Mudmonth.

**`chapter-0748.md`, 3,131 words.** Frostmonth the seventh, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut, nine people in that street. **THE ORIGIN IS ANSWERED, AND IT IS A BOUNDARY AND A REFUSAL, IN 122 WORDS PRINTED IN FULL.** He walked the line three times, counted it twice with a rod he made out of the handle of a barrow and got nine hundred yards three times, and the scar does not enter the kerb by one yard. Standing on the grass at the ninth of the nine stones he says out loud: **A road is a thing somebody walks. That line is nine hundred yards of grass that nine households have cut and kept and never once walked, and the nine hundred yards of kerb below it is nine hundred yards of road with not one yard of this in it. The oldest road in this county is the whole thing on that hill and what is broken is the boundary. Nine stones at one height across the top of nine hundred yards is a thing a man on the made road at the bottom could read, and they put them there and they never walked up it, and that is the whole of what I have got and there is nobody to find.** **There is no body, there was never a body, nobody came down any road with one, and there are no notes.** The beekeeper asks, kindly, whether that is where it started, and he says in forty words that that is where it is now and that he does not know where it began. A person asks, kindly, whether he will be able to go up it on his own, and he says in thirty-two words that he cannot and that nobody in nine counties is going to say that to him either. He sat down two hundred yards from the bottom of that hill getting down it.

**`chapter-0749.md`, 3,347 words.** Frostmonth the fifteenth, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open. **A REQUEST FROM THE ROAD OFFICE FOUR HUNDRED MILES OFF, AND THE SECOND AND LAST PRINTING OF THE NAME IN THE VOLUME, AND THE BADGE-MAN'S SECOND QUESTION IN FOUR AND A HALF YEARS.** The copyist reads out twenty-one words: **Under the Marshal's hand, Edric Hunn, at the office four hundred miles off, and this county is asked to answer it.** The whole of what that sheet asks for is the name of the person who holds a certain wall on a certain made road and a copy of any drawing anybody in that county has made of what is above it, and nobody in nine counties has answered it and nobody is going to, and about four people in that street had worked out that this county has been unable to answer anything since the first of Goatmonth. Then the badge-man lays his hand flat on the top rail and says out loud, to about four hundred people, in seventy-three words: **There is a man of forty-one at the head of that road with nine hundred yards of wall and about eleven years of a gate in it and he has never been asked what it is for and he has never said. There is nine hundred yards of dead grass nine hundred paces above his bar and about nine people can point at it on a board. Who is going to hold it.** **Nobody stopped it and nobody answered it and he did not ask it again and he went back up the lane on his own.** A person at the third door asks, kindly, whether there is an answer to that, and he says in forty-nine words that there is not and that both of the badge-man's two questions in four and a half years have been about holding something and neither of them was about a road.

**`chapter-0750.md`, 3,831 words.** Frostmonth the twenty-fifth, YR 322, a Sunday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, the hearing not sitting, no market in that street. **THE VOLUME'S LAST PAGE, AND THE RESOLUTION IS NEGATIVE.** The drove went up on the Thursday before, the twenty-second, and that is thirty-eight drove since the second of Mudmonth, and the man carrying stone is at two hundred and sixty-three days, and the brood has stood at five frames for about fifteen weeks and no sixth has gone in. **THE SEPARATION IS FURTHER APART THAN IT WAS AND EACH OF THEM HAS BUILT ONE THING: she has put a second lamp at the foot of the thirteen stairs and lit it at the fourth hour out of fourpence a year, and he put two mile stones back in line between the seventh mile stone and the ninth about the middle of Harvestmonth, and neither of them knows about the other and there is no exchange of words between them in any of the ten files.** Then **three answers to who is going to hold nine hundred yards of wall, and they do not agree and the disagreement is not resolved**: the roadkeeper says in fifty-eight words that a wall is held by the man who repairs it and he has been asking that office for nine years to pay him for nine hundred feet of kerb and nobody has answered him; the beekeeper says in forty-three words that the bees are not going to hold it and he is not going to ask them and about nine hundred yards of his broom is standing dead nine hundred yards above a wall and nobody has asked him what to do with it; and the badge-man, the man with the satchel and the man of thirty-two all say nothing at all. A person asks, kindly, whether he is going to be all right, and he says in fifty-two words that he has a bad leg, has lost the use of one ear, and that there are nine hundred yards of grass four days off that will not come back and nine counties cannot hold any of it. **THE LAST OF IT IS A LIST: nine hundred yards of an old road in four hundred mouths and in nobody's hand, two refusals in four hundred mouths and in nobody's hand, four pieces of paper in an office four days off that nobody can read, nine hundred yards of dead grass on the back of a board in a cart shed that about nine people can point at, and nothing about any of that in anybody's hand.** And the three things standing that nobody here will carry: a place shutting its own end before Hearthmonth, a wall on a board instead of hidden, and a request from an office four hundred miles off that this county has been unable to answer since the first of Goatmonth. Nobody thanked anybody on any page of it.

**THE BATCH 0005 REVIEW, AND THE TEN WORD COUNTS ABOVE ARE THE ONES ON THE FILES AFTER IT.** The block was reviewed before it closed and twenty-eight defects were repaired on the page, and the full record is `state/continuity.md` 1695. **No chapter was rewritten, no paragraph of scene prose was replaced, no dateline moved, and the ten money columns did not move by one penny.** **AND A SECOND PASS RAN AFTER THAT ONE AGAINST `logs/batch-0005.review.log` AND FOUND ELEVEN FIGURE ERRORS IN TWENTY-FOUR PLACES ACROSS FIVE FILES, AND NOT ONE OF THE ELEVEN WAS A FIGURE ON A PAGE, AND THE TEN WORD COUNTS ABOVE DID NOT MOVE AGAIN BECAUSE NO FILE UNDER `chapters/` WAS TOUCHED. The full record is `state/continuity.md` 1696. The three that a reader of this file would have caught are the parish-roll counts, which are on the pages at 260, 10, 50, 90, 170, 250, 10, 70, 150 and 250 and were wrong in `state/continuity.md` 1683 in three of ten positions; the counts of days since the two refusals, which are on the pages at 42 and 30, 47 and 35, 51 and 39, 55 and 43, 63 and 51, 71 and 59, 77 and 65, 83 and 71, 91 and 79 and 101 and 89 and were printed as nine pairs with a 97 and 85 that no dateline supports; and the maximum chapter length, which is 3,831 and was printed as 3,763 in four files.** By file: **0741** the broom is cut for the first time on the morning of the twenty-sixth instead of four days ago, the office is shown with one thing in it instead of two, the fourth and fifth occasions are a day apart and not four, and the dateline no longer calls the ground mapped. **0742** the room rule is not in it but the first of a month is, the first of Embermonth is named instead of a Sunday that had not happened, the mile stone was cleaned on the Saturday, the man of forty-one's figures are one question and ten words, the inn is down that street and not half a mile down that road, the drove's grass is killed on a Thursday and not twice a week, and the office is shown with two things in it and not three. **0743** the stone count is two hundred and thirteen. **0744** the room is shut on five mornings and not two, four people saw the line at the same time and not on four different mornings, the leaf has been in that office nine days and not eight, and the inn's bed count is a morning and not a night. **0745** one name is read out and not two, two hundred and two hundred make the four hundred, one of the four has said it out loud twice and not never, and the third cart is six weeks back and not two months. **0746** the fifth frame is not all brood yet and the figure has stood five weeks and not nine, the fifth-hour ask reads correctly for a Friday, the coda says Friday, the weigh-house weighs one load and not two, the man of forty-one has a hand on the ash and not a stick, five of nine people went out of the street and not nine, and the bed count is a morning and not a night. **0747** the first of Wolfmonth is named instead of a Sunday that had not happened, he has been up that shoulder twice and both times with somebody, the frame has stood about six weeks and not four, the bar paragraph is cut, and the coda's scar is six days old and not five. **0748** the printed notice carries one name and not two, the leaf came in about five weeks ago and the notice about three, and the bed count is a morning and not a night. **0749** that name was heard for the second time and not two names, the sheet asks for two things and not one, and the third cart is six weeks back. **0750** the broom line is one line and not two, the second thing is not about the wall or the scar and not about a road, the four and a half years carry the separation and not a named day, the bar paragraph is cut, and the street closes differently from 0746's. **The two findings the review rejected are argued at 1695: the house question frame that eight of the ten chapters close on is a signature and not a copy, and the long shared boilerplate runs are the corpus's deliberate recurrence and are named as a fact about the block rather than repaired.**

## Volume 16, Batch 0001 — Chapters 751 to 760, "One Thing Each"

**THE TEN WORD COUNTS ABOVE ARE THE ONES ON THE FILES AFTER THE PROSE REPAIR AT `state/continuity.md` 1711, and the pre-repair counts of 3,537 / 3,594 / 3,558 / 3,780 / 3,554 / 3,390 / 3,860 / 3,812 / 3,744 / 4,189 and a block of 37,018 are stale. NOTHING IN THESE TEN SUMMARIES CHANGED BUT THE COUNTS: every dateline, figure, price, speech, name, refusal, silence and beat below is as it was written, and the repair was to the prose, which had opened 169 of its 362 paragraphs on a standing negation, carried the measuring instrument into all ten closing paragraphs, given the protagonist no interiority at all, and attributed twenty speeches by their length.**

**`chapter-0751.md`, 4,001 words.** Frostmonth the twenty-sixth, YR 322, a Monday and not a market morning, the room over the weigh-house shut behind its own door, the fourth hour in a market street with nobody in it and three people on a floor four days off. **VOLUME 16 OPENS, AND `Aren Kest` IS NAMED ONCE IN THE WHOLE BLOCK AND IT IS HERE.** The drove is the thirty-eighth since the second of Mudmonth, the wall's gap is about nine feet by four feet, the stone has been carried two miles in his own hands for two hundred and sixty-four days, five frames of brood are under nine straw skirts, about nine hundred yards of broom is standing on the east rise, and the scar is thirty-one days old. **THE PRACTICE BEGINS.** He asks, out loud, in the open, in the market street at the sea end, for one thing each from three people. The ask is sixty-seven words and carries no count. The woman of about fifty-one answers in forty-three words, the man of about thirty-four with a satchel in fifty-eight, and the man of about fifty-four who keeps that floor in seventy-six. **THE RULE IS FIFTY-TWO WORDS AND IS ON THE PAGE WHOLE: a thing is held when two people have said out loud in the same hour that they are holding it, and both of them are still holding it on the next morning they said they would be, and that is all of it and he is not going to improve on it.** The number is said out loud twice at the fourth hour and both of them said three. **THE HAND LAMP GOES ON THE COPING AT THE LOW SIDE OF THAT CAUSEWAY ON THAT AFTERNOON BECAUSE THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF FROSTMONTH IS A MONDAY, and no chapter of Volume 15 had a Monday in it, and about four people noticed and five did not.** A person at the third door asks him, kindly, seven words, whether it is going to be all right.

**`chapter-0752.md`, 3,761 words.** Hearthmonth the first, YR 322, a Saturday and a market morning, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open. **THE THIRTEENTH FIRST OF A MONTH IN A ROW WITH NOTHING AT THOSE BOARDS.** Hearthmonth is the copying season and about ninety sheets come past that floor in nine days where about four hundred and nine came past in Frostmonth. **THE PRACTICE DOES NOT RUN ON A MARKET MORNING, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS WHY: it keeps to the five mornings a week this county is not using, and a practice that keeps to five mornings a week can be shut by closing a door five days a week, and the door it is not using is on thirteen stairs over that floor and is not the practice's door.** A cart with paper in it stands outside the road office for one day, the fourth in about two months, and nobody at the low end of that street has ever opened the paper. **THE LEAF'S MONTH HAS ARRIVED AND NOTHING AT THIS END OF NINE COUNTIES HAS SHUT.** A person asks, kindly, whether that leaf is about a road, six words, and he answers in fifty-one words that he has not been to the place, is not going to be sent, has nine things, and could not tell you which of them is which. The man of about fifty-four says his figure out loud in fifty-seven words, about the difference between a copying month and a clean one.

**`chapter-0753.md`, 3,799 words.** Hearthmonth the ninth, YR 322, a Sunday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on that floor with four people on it, and a man of about fifty-four being given his name out loud in a market street. **THE COPYIST ASKS THE FIRST QUESTION ABOUT THE PRACTICE AND IT IS 27 WORDS: Where does the number go after the morning? I write things down and I have never had anything to write down that two people said out loud.** He answers in fifty-three words that it goes in the two people and nowhere else and that this is deliberate, because a room with ten questions and no finding under any of them is a room that has learned what happens to a thing that gets put in it. **THE BLOCK'S FIRST FAILURE IS ON THE PAGE: the number is not written down and the reason is given out loud and it is a reason and not an excuse.** The man of about fifty-four says out loud in fifty-nine words that the last lime was on the twenty-ninth of Frostmonth, the next is due on the thirteenth of Hearthmonth, and it has been fourteen days between every cart for about nine years and nobody has ever said it back to him. **THE NAME SPEND.** A person at the third door asks, kindly, four words, what his name is, and he says it himself in twenty-nine words: **Renn Ottery, and I keep this floor, and I am fifty-four, and I have been saying my figures out loud on it for about four and a half years.** The register gains no entry, the day-book stands at eleven entries, the fourth ruled head is empty and no fifth head is ruled.

**`chapter-0754.md`, 3,826 words.** Hearthmonth the fifteenth, YR 322, a Saturday and a market morning, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open, and a cart of lime that came up from the north two days before standing on that floor. **THE INTERVAL IS FOURTEEN DAYS AND HAS BEEN FOR NINE YEARS, AND HE SAYS IT OUT LOUD IN FIFTY-ONE WORDS AND THE CHAPTER NOTES THAT THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF HEARTHMONTH IS A THURSDAY.** The man of forty-one was asked on the eleventh of Hearthmonth and has said nothing for four days, and that is not a refusal, and the count has not moved, and nobody on that floor can tell which of the two people of the rule is not there. **THE PRACTICE HAS RUN FOURTEEN MORNINGS AND THE NUMBER IS FOUR AND IT HAS BEEN FOUR SINCE THE FIFTH.** A person asks, kindly, five words, what the ten things are, and he answers at the third door in fifty-two words that nine of them are roads somebody in this county keeps and one is nine hundred yards of grass four days off, and that he could tell you the nine and is not going to, and that nobody has asked him a question and has asked him the right one. The first of Goatmonth is sixteen days off and the fourteenth first of a month is a Monday.

**`chapter-0755.md`, 3,643 words.** Hearthmonth the twenty-first, YR 322, a Friday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on that floor with five people on it, and a roadkeeper putting a date and a figure on one thing each. **THE FIFTH ASK SUCCEEDS AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME ANYBODY IN THIS COUNTY HAS SAID WHAT A THING COSTS.** He says out loud, in the open, in his own trade, sixty-five words: **I will hold one of yours until the first of Mudmonth and not one day past it, and I have asked that office for nine years to pay me for nine hundred feet of kerb and nobody has ever answered me, and a man who is not paid is not holding, and I am holding this one on the same terms I hold the kerb.** **THE DATE IS THE DATE ON A NOTICE THAT HAS NOT ARRIVED YET AND NOBODY IN NINE COUNTIES HAS NAMED THE FIRST OF MUDMONTH AGAINST ANYTHING.** The man of forty-one is now ten days silent. A person asks the woman of about fifty-one, kindly, seven words, what happens when one of them stops, and she answers in forty-four words: **The number goes down by one and nobody hears it go down, and that is the whole of the answer, and I have kept a house open at the fourth hour for nine years and I have never once known who was in it.** That is hole one in the rule and it is found on the twenty-first of Hearthmonth and is not written down.

**`chapter-0756.md`, 3,442 words.** Hearthmonth the twenty-ninth, YR 322, a Saturday and a market morning, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open, and a figure on that floor that is one short of what it should be. **THE MISSING CART.** The cart was due on the twenty-seventh and did not come, and the twenty-ninth is the second day after. `Renn Ottery` says the number of loads out loud in fifty-three words: **Two hundred and twenty-nine carts have been on this floor since I came to it and I have had every one of them to a pound, and there is one missing and the one missing is the twenty-seventh, and I have never once been one out in about four and a half years.** **A FIGURE WRONG BY ONE IS ON THE PAGE AND IS A FIGURE AND NOT A FEELING, AND ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED PEOPLE DID NOT STOP.** The sixth ask was made on the twenty-fourth of Hearthmonth to the man of about fifty-four who keeps the far end of that shut lane and he said one sentence, which is eleven words, and it explains no part of itself and it was thanked by nobody, and the count did not move. The man of forty-one is eighteen days silent. A person asks, kindly, six words, how long is a cart late, and he answers in forty-seven words that nobody in this county can tell, that there is nobody at the far end of the road to ask, and that the man who keeps that floor will know on the second of Goatmonth and not before.

**`chapter-0757.md`, 3,929 words.** Goatmonth the fourth, YR 322, a Thursday and a market morning, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open, and a roadkeeper saying out loud in that street that he has been up a lane and found nine hurdles across it. **THE FIRST OF GOATM CAME AND WENT ON THE MONDAY BEFORE WITH NOTHING AT THOSE BOARDS, AND THAT IS THE FOURTEENTH FIRST OF A MONTH IN A ROW.** The badge-man of about fifty-eight was asked on the twenty-seventh of Hearthmonth and said yes on the twenty-eighth, and the number has been six since the twenty-ninth. **THE ROADKEEPER WENT ON HIS OWN BUSINESS ON THE TUESDAY BEFORE, BECAUSE THE KERB HE IS UNDER CONTRACT FOR IS ON THE FIRST NINE MILES OF THAT MADE ROAD AND HE HAD NOT BEEN UP IT SINCE THE SECOND OF MUDMONTH, AND NOBODY SENT HIM.** He says it out loud, in the open, in his own trade, to about four hundred people, in sixty-five words: **I have been up that made road eleven miles and about a mile past where it stops being made, and there are nine hurdles set across the cart track foot to foot with a hurdle shoe on the top one and a thumb of lime on it, and there are nine houses about four hundred yards back and not one light in any of them.** About two hundred of them stop what they are doing. **Hearthmonth went and gone yesterday, being the thirtieth, and the leaf that named it is seventy-seven days old.** A person asks, kindly, eight words, whether that is the same place as the leaf, and he answers in fifty-five words: **I do not know, and the leaf came up a made road with one thumb on it and no names on it, and the hurdles are nine houses four hundred yards back, and I have not been to them and I am not going to be sent, and those are three things I can say.** The floor is two short on loads and about thirty on sheets.

**`chapter-0758.md`, 3,664 words.** Goatmonth the twelfth, YR 322, a Friday and not a market morning, the room shut, the ninth hour on a lane four hundred paces long, and a name read out of a cart tally that nobody in nine counties had ever said aloud. **THE NUMBER IS SIX AND IT HAS BEEN SIX FOR THIRTEEN DAYS.** A man of about twenty-seven came down that made road on the Tuesday before and put a tally-stick on the edge of that floor, and the copyist of about twenty-six read it out loud, and the name is on the page: **Pell Arkwright, of Wethergill, and that is the fourth cart off that track this year and the tally is for the thirteenth of Hearthmonth and it has not been cleared and he has come down about eleven miles to ask why.** He says out loud, in his own trade, to about nine people, sixty-three words: **I was the ninth vote, and the nine of us voted on the eleventh of Hearthmonth and I told them I would not be the tenth, and I have been carrying that for about a month, and I am not going to be asked anything about it by anybody in this street and I am going to be asked what a sheet weighs.** **THE STANDING QUESTION OF FOUR AND A HALF YEARS IS PAID OUT ON THE PAGE.** He asks it, in five words. The man of about fifty-four answers in fifty-eight words: **A sheet is about four hundred yards of it if a man wants the whole of one and about four inches of it if he wants the top of it, and there is no figure for it and there never has been, and about four hundred and nine of them come past this floor in a good month.** **THE FIRST EXCHANGE OF WORDS IN FOUR AND A HALF YEARS BETWEEN THE MAN OF THIRTY-TWO AND THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS THE ROOM IS AT THE FIFTH HOUR, ON A LANE, AT THE BOTTOM OF THIRTEEN STAIRS, AND IT IS ABOUT A LAMP.** He says it in forty-one words, that there is a lamp at the foot of her stairs at the fourth hour every morning and it is the only light on that lane at that hour and he has walked past it about fifty times and never said so. She says it back in thirty-eight words: **It is on fourpence a year and I pay it out of that and it is my lamp and I lit it for the stairs and not for anybody else, and I know you have walked past it.** Nobody said anything back and nothing is in a document and the register gains no entry and the day-book stands at eleven entries.

**`chapter-0759.md`, 3,778 words.** Goatmonth the twentieth, YR 322, a Saturday and a market morning, about four hundred people and about two hundred stopped, the room open, and a printed notice read out loud at those boards that nobody stopped. **THE NOTICE, AND IT IS TWO SENTENCES AND SEVENTY-FIVE WORDS AND IT IS ON THE PAGE WHOLE.** The copyist of about twenty-six reads the first in forty-four words: **By the first of Mudmonth every road in nine counties is to be entered in a roll with the name of the person who keeps it set against it, and any road in nine counties with no name against it is to be shut.** The second is twenty-seven words: **The roll is to be made up in the county and sent down the made road, and the county is not to keep a copy of it.** **IT CARRIES THE MARSHAL'S HAND AT THE FOOT OF IT AND NO NAME, AND ABOUT TWO HUNDRED PEOPLE HEARD A ROAD IS TO BE SHUT AND ABOUT TWO HUNDRED WENT ON WHAT THEY WERE DOING.** The practice has run thirty-nine mornings and the number is eight, and **two of the eight said yes on the ninth and the tenth of that month and were not asked for it by anybody, and the chapter says that a practice which grows by people putting themselves in it is not a practice anybody is holding.** A man of about thirty-two stood at the high end of those boards with his left hand where anybody could see it and said nothing at all while it was read. **The printed request of the fifteenth of Frostmonth is about four feet from that notice on a table in the same room and it asks for the same name, and the same office has now asked this county for one name twice in two forms in seventy-three days and has had no answer to either.** A person asks, kindly, nine words, what a road with no name against it is, and he answers in fifty-two words that it is nine hundred yards of wall with a yard missing and about nine hundred yards of broom standing on the shoulder above it and a bar of ash in a gate and about nine houses behind a hurdle, and that he knows of four, and is not going to say which.

**`chapter-0760.md`, 4,681 words.** Thawmonth the seventh, YR 323, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with nine people on it, and a man of forty-one who has said ten words in eleven years saying thirty-four of them on a shoulder. **THE YEAR TURNED SIX DAYS AGO, ON A WEDNESDAY, WITH NOTHING AT THOSE BOARDS, AND THAT IS THE FIFTEENTH FIRST OF A MONTH IN A ROW, AND THIS PAGE IS THE FIRST PAGE IN THE MANUSCRIPT THAT CARRIES YR 323 ON IT.** **THE MAN OF FORTY-ONE'S ANSWER CAME FORTY-SEVEN DAYS AFTER ONE QUESTION AND NOT TO A SECOND, AND IT IS 34 WORDS AND IT IS ON THE PAGE WHOLE: I will hold the wall and not the drove and not the bar, and the wall has got no name against it, and a road with no name against it is to be shut.** The practice has run fifty-two mornings, which is five mornings a week for ten weeks and two days, and the number is eight, and it has been eight since the eleventh of Goatmonth. **TWO OF THE TEN ARE NOT HELD: the Wethergill end, which went quiet on the thirteenth of Hearthmonth and which nobody has been up for thirty-six days, and the scar, which has never been held by anybody at all.** The drove is the forty-eighth since the second of Mudmonth, the stone has been carried three hundred and thirty-five days, the gap is still about nine feet by four feet, and the broom is still standing. The first of Mudmonth is in twenty-four days. A person at the third door asks, kindly, nine words, whether he is going to put his name on it, and nobody said anything back and nobody thanked him. **THE QUESTION THE BLOCK ENDS ON IS NOT WHO IS GOING TO HOLD NINE HUNDRED YARDS OF WALL, WHICH A MAN ANSWERED IN THIRTY-FOUR WORDS ON A SHOULDER THREE DAYS AGO, AND IT IS WHO IS GOING TO WRITE A NAME ON A PIECE OF PAPER FOR FOUR ROADS THAT NOBODY IN NINE COUNTIES HAS EVER BEEN ASKED TO NAME, AND IT IS A WORSE QUESTION, AND IT IS THE FIRST QUESTION IN ABOUT FOUR AND A HALF YEARS IN THIS COUNTY THAT NOBODY IS GOING TO BE ASKED.**

## Volume 16, Batch 0002 — Chapters 761 to 770, "The Thumb of the Shoulder"

**THE TEN WORD COUNTS ABOVE ARE THE ONES ON THE FILES AFTER THE THE THIRTEEN-NUMBER REPAIR, THE SIX-PARAGRAPH REPAIR AND THE REVIEW REPAIR, AND THEY ARE 3,821 / 3,559 / 3,306 / 3,279 / 3,569 / 3,236 / 3,438 / 3,160 / 3,357 / 3,486 FOR A TOTAL OF 34,211 AND A MEAN OF 3,421.1.** The day of month and the weekday on all ten datelines were derived from the day number and the anchor at day 2310 and never copied out of the printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` section seventeen. Five datelines are market mornings and five are not, and the practice runs on the five that are not.

**`chapter-0761.md`, 3,821 words.** Thawmonth the eighth, YR 323, day 2683, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with nine people on it, and a man from a mill lane saying out loud that a thing is being done to a road. **THE ESCALATION THE SERIES ROW ASKS FOR OPENS HERE.** A man from a mill lane four miles north stops at the third door in the rain and says that bars are being lifted off a driftway his council voted shut to get a cart through with paper in it, and the man of thirty-two asks him one question, which is how many households walk that driftway and will they say out loud what they want done with it, and the answer is nine households who have said it once each to that one man and to nobody else. His goal is to hear a number said twice and do nothing else and his miscalculation is that he thought listening was a thing he could do unseen, and the cost is a leg gone from a dull ache to a sharp pull behind the knee and an ear that filled with rain on slate. **He walks four miles north before light the next morning and asks all nine households at their own doors and gets nine answers that are the same, and his miscalculation there is the time, and it costs him the market street at noon and the hearing boards and a dry hour he had meant for the stones.** The drove is forty-eight since the second of Mudmonth, the practice has run fifty-three mornings and the number is eight, the stone has been carried three hundred and thirty-six days, the scar is a hundred and three days old, the last cart of lime came up that track fifty-five days ago, and the first of Mudmonth is in twenty-three days. **His money column is the first of the two that carry a free total and foots to sixty-one pence, which is one mark and thirteen over.**

**`chapter-0762.md`, 3,559 words.** Thawmonth the sixteenth, YR 323, day 2691, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open, the ninth hour at the boards with a cart tally in them, and a floor four days off that said no number that morning. The man of about fifty-four says his shortfall out loud where a floor cannot hear it, being no cart since the thirteenth of Hearthmonth and that is sixty-three days and he has never once been one short in about four and a half years until this one and he is one short still. The man of about twenty-seven says his ninth vote and his limit in the same breath, being nine houses voted on the eleventh of Hearthmonth and he was the ninth and he would not be the tenth. **His goal is to buy nails and hear what a market thinks and his miscalculation is that he thought a market morning was a morning for hearing, and it is a morning for selling, and nobody tells a man with a stick anything until he has bought something.** The drove is fifty, the practice has run fifty-eight mornings with no number said, the stone has been carried three hundred and forty-four days, and the scar is a hundred and eleven days old. **His money column is the second and last of the free ones and foots to eighty-four pence, which is one mark and thirty-six over.**

**`chapter-0763.md`, 3,306 words.** Thawmonth the twentieth, YR 323, day 2695, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on that floor with nine people on it, and a hand lamp going on a coping four days south that afternoon. **THE FIRST OF THE TWO LANES.** The ninth mile stone had been cleaned on the Saturday before, which is the other of the two mornings of the week it is cleaned on, and at about the fifth hour of that Monday he stands nine feet off the foot of thirteen stairs in the lane and says that her room is shut five mornings and open two and that he has never asked what the two are for, and she says back that the two are for the room and the five are for the floor and that the room is hers and the floor is his and that she knows he has never asked. **His miscalculation is that he thought a lane with a lamp on it was a lane he could pass through unseen, and a man who has walked past a lamp about fifty times has been seen about fifty times.** Nothing went in any document, the register gained no entry and the day-book stands at eleven entries. The drove is fifty, the practice has run sixty-one mornings and the number is eight, the stone has been carried three hundred and forty-eight days, the scar is a hundred and fifteen days old, and the last lime cart was sixty-seven days ago.

**`chapter-0764.md`, 3,279 words.** Thawmonth the twenty-fifth, YR 323, day 2700, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open, the ninth hour at the third door with a second question about a driftway, and nine households saying out loud what they want. A carter nobody in nine counties knew stops at the third door with an empty cart and asks whose bars those are and who pays for the cart that waits on them, and the mill man steps forward with eight of his neighbours behind him and says ours and we keep them on until the frost is off, and the vote said it, and the beasts come first, and each of the nine says it after him one by one. **The carter turns his empty cart in the street and goes back the way he had come, and no route was opened and none was shut and no cut was made anywhere.** His miscalculation is that he thought the cart would come on a market morning and that standing ready for a thing that does not come is a different cost from standing against it, and it costs him three hours on stone for nothing. The drove is fifty-one, the practice has run sixty-four mornings with no number said, the stone has been carried three hundred and fifty-three days, the scar is a hundred and twenty days old, the last lime cart was seventy-two days ago, and the first of Mudmonth is in six days.

**`chapter-0765.md`, 3,569 words.** Mudmonth the first, YR 323, day 2706, a Friday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on that floor with nine people on it, and the date printed on that notice come round at last. **THE DATE COMES AND GOES AND NOTHING HAPPENS ON IT.** About nine people come to those boards one by one out of about nine trades and each says one thing out loud, short, in their own trade, and none of them touches the paper, and the first says he keeps a floor and not a road and his name goes on no roll, and the second says he keeps four miles under contract and nine hundred feet unpaid and his name goes on no roll, and four more of the nine are given on the page in their own words, being the woman who keeps the copy of a roll she is not allowed to have, the man who carries what nine other men carry, the copyist with ten questions and no answer under any of them, and the young man of about nineteen with five frames under nine skirts. **No gate was shut in that county that day, no bar was lifted and none was put on, the notice stayed on the boards getting wet, and nobody in nine counties had asked those nine for anything and nobody is going to.** He had braced for months to stand against authority and there was no authority to stand against, and the bracing was the miscalculation and it cost him a morning and his shoulders and his knee. The drove is fifty-two, the practice has run sixty-nine mornings, the stone has been carried three hundred and fifty-nine days, the scar is a hundred and twenty-six days old, and the last lime cart was seventy-eight days ago.

**`chapter-0766.md`, 3,236 words.** Mudmonth the seventh, YR 323, day 2712, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open, the ninth hour with rain over the shoulder four days off, and a floor that said no number that morning. The roadkeeper of about fifty-two comes in with mud to the knees and says out loud that the shoulder goes soft by Friday and no cart gets up past the ninth stone and that the thaw and the floods meet on it this month and Mudmonth is the worst month for roads, and the man of about fifty-four answers that the floor wants a dry road under it the way a cart wants one and the floor has had seventy-three dry mornings and Mudmonth gives none. **His miscalculation is that he thought oilcloth was sold by the price on market mornings, and it is sold by who gets to the stall first, and he got there third and the cloth leaked at the shoulder seam.** A practice that needs a dry road is a practice a man can switch off by ruining the road, and he has not thought that sentence until the water runs down his neck. The drove is fifty-three and two men went with it for the first time in about nine months, the practice has run seventy-three mornings, the stone has been carried three hundred and sixty-five days, the scar is a hundred and thirty-two days old, and the last lime cart was eighty-four days ago.

**`chapter-0767.md`, 3,438 words.** Mudmonth the twenty-seventh, YR 323, day 2732, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with five people on it, and nine days of the shoulder under water come to an end. **THE BLOCK'S TEST AND ITS FINDING.** Nine days of water took the shoulder from the nineteenth to the twenty-seventh and one Thursday fell inside them, being the twenty-first, and a drove went up it while the shoulder was soft but not shut, and four of the nine people on that floor could not get there because the floor is four days off, and the number fell from eight to four and was said twice by five people standing in mist. **SEVEN OF THOSE NINE DAYS WERE MORNINGS THIS PRACTICE RUNS ON AND SIX OF THE SEVEN WENT BY WITH NOBODY STANDING ON THE FLOOR, WHICH IS SIX MORNINGS OFF A COUNT OF EIGHTY-EIGHT, AND THE MORNING COUNT STANDS AT EIGHTY-TWO.** He says the finding out loud at a third door, being that nine days of water took four of the nine and the number is four and a practice that needs a made road can be switched off by ruining the road. The man of forty-one is on that shoulder with a spade turning water off the wall foot and says eleven words and is not asked anything before or after. His miscalculation is that he thought the practice was his to restart, and the practice has a growth rate and the growth rate is not his to set, and the cost is a leg gone from ache to dead weight on a road that gives under the stick and an ear gone from thin hearing to a roar of damp. The stone has been carried three hundred and eighty-five days, the scar is a hundred and fifty-two days old, and the last lime cart was a hundred and four days ago.

**`chapter-0768.md`, 3,160 words.** Mudmonth the thirty-seventh, YR 323, day 2742, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open, the ninth hour with the shoulder drying, and five people where nine stood. He asks one of the four who stopped, in his own trade, at a stall, whether he will say the number with them when the road hardens, and is told that he will come when the road is hard and not before and that is all he will say on a market morning. **His miscalculation is that asking was a thing he could do on a market morning among stalls, and asking on a market morning is performing, and two men asked among about four hundred hearers cannot say yes or no freely, and the cost is three hours of standing for nothing and a knee gone deep and an ear gone to one long ache.** A maybe on a market morning is a no with good manners. The drove is fifty-seven and one man went with it, the practice has run eighty-eight mornings with no number said, the stone has been carried three hundred and ninety-five days, the scar is a hundred and sixty-two days old, and the last lime cart was a hundred and fourteen days ago.

**`chapter-0769.md`, 3,357 words.** Mudmonth the thirty-ninth, YR 323, day 2744, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and two people choosing a thing on a lane in short words, in rain that had come back on the Thursday and was still coming. **THE RELATIONSHIP IS CHOSEN HERE AND IT IS NOT ANNOUNCED.** Two of the seven mend a soft place in the track below the floor on their own account and in their own trades without a word, the roadkeeper with his bar and the woman of about fifty-one with broom straw, and nobody in this county has ever asked a person to account for a thing they did on their own account and this was the first morning anybody could have and nobody did. At about the fifth hour, after the two mendings and before the lane, he stands nine feet off the foot of those stairs and says that the lamp is a room and that he walks past it, and she says back that the room is his to pass and to come in dry, and neither of them calls it anything. **Her name is printed here for the first time in this county in years and it is `Mara Vey`, and it is printed twice in this one file and nowhere else in the ten.** His miscalculation is that he thought saying the number was the work, and the work was what two of the seven did after it without being asked, and he had asked for nothing and got more than he asked for. The drove is fifty-seven, the practice has run ninety mornings and the number is four, the stone has been carried three hundred and ninety-seven days, the scar is a hundred and sixty-four days old, and the last lime cart was a hundred and sixteen days ago.

**`chapter-0770.md`, 3,486 words.** Mudmonth the forty-ninth, YR 323, day 2754, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open, the ninth hour with seventy-two days behind, and a floor that said no number that morning and will say none after. **THE BLOCK'S CLOSE, AND THE FIRST MARK THAT IS NOT A ROOM AND NOT A PAPER.** The man of about fifty-four comes down to the market street with chalk on his thumb and says the floor out loud in his own trade and then goes back four days up the made road and at the weigh-house door bends with difficulty and makes four short lines on the stone sill where the rain does not reach, and they are about four inches long and side by side and they mean four, and nobody asked him to make them and nobody has read them out loud. **HIS MISCALCULATION IS THAT HE THOUGHT SEVENTY-TWO DAYS WOULD ADD TO AN ANSWER, AND SEVENTY-TWO DAYS ADDS TO A QUESTION, AND THE QUESTION IS WHAT A PRACTICE IS WHEN THE ROAD UNDER IT GOES.** The first of Mudmonth came and gone with about nine people saying they would put no name on four lines of a roll, and nobody had asked them to and nobody is going to, and nobody in nine counties is going to shut a gate on it either. The drove is fifty-nine, the practice has run ninety-seven mornings and the number last said is four and it was eight before the water, the stone has been carried four hundred and seven days and the ninth yard has not been started, the scar is a hundred and seventy-four days old, and the last lime cart was a hundred and twenty-six days ago.

## Volume 16, Batch 0003 — Chapters 771 to 780, "The Silence Is Not Death"
