**THE TEN WORD COUNTS ABOVE ARE THE ONES ON THE FILES AFTER THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS AT `state/continuity.md` 1741 AND THEY ARE 4,031 / 4,251 / 4,045 / 4,041 / 4,055 / 4,026 / 3,665 / 4,241 / 3,998 / 4,062 FOR A TOTAL OF 40,415 AND A MEAN OF 4,041.5, AND THEY WERE 3,940 / 4,054 / 4,039 / 4,035 / 4,010 / 4,017 / 3,654 / 4,229 / 3,992 / 4,045 FOR A TOTAL OF 40,015 AND A MEAN OF 4,001.5 BEFORE IT.** The day of month and the weekday on all ten datelines were derived from the day number and the anchor at day 2310 and never copied out of either printed table. **TWO OF THE TEN DATELINES ARE MARKET MORNINGS AND EIGHT ARE NOT, the two being 2791 and 2826, which are the block's own eighth and tenth days, and the practice runs on the eight that are not and does not run on the two. The five-and-five in this line before the repair pass was wrong and was carried out of the block prompt, and the count of two is the count the ten datelines actually give.**

**`chapter-0771.md`, 4,031 words.** Mudmonth the fiftieth, YR 323, day 2755, a Friday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and a man of thirty-two with a stick going to the other side of a door to look at four lines of chalk. **THE OBJECT THIS BLOCK IS BUILT ON IS ON THE PAGE AND IS NOT EXPLAINED: four short lines under the hood of that door where the rain does not reach, still dry and still sharp after nine days of rain that had been at the yard, with one pale drag across the left end of the top line where a thumb went over it, and he read that drag for a moment as the first stroke of a letter, and nobody has read the four lines out loud and nobody has explained them.** He asks the man of about fifty-four, in that man's trade, ten words, what the floor says when nobody is at it, and gets **thirty-eight words** back: **It says it, and it has said it about four and a half years, and a figure nobody has written down is a figure that dies with the man who said it, and stone is not a man.** **His miscalculation is that he believed four lines side by side were writing, and a mark with no key says one thing to the man who made it and nothing at all to anybody who comes after, and nobody gave the sill a key and nobody in nine counties is going to.** The cost is the leg from four days of made road to a deep grind at the knee when he bent to the sill and it stayed ground, and an ear with the drip off the hood in it turned into a bell that would not stop. He then says out loud to a wet yard that he is going up to the head of that cart track on the Wednesday, and that nobody has sent him and he is not going to be sent, **and he does go up on that Wednesday, at `chapter-0772.md`, and the page carries the promise through.** The drove is fifty-nine, the practice has run ninety-eight mornings and the number is four, the stone has been carried four hundred and eight days, the scar is a hundred and seventy-five days old, and nobody has been up that track for a hundred and eight days. **His money column foots to eighty-five pence, which is one mark and thirty-seven over.**

**`chapter-0772.md`, 4,251 words.** Mudmonth the fifty-fifth, YR 323, day 2760, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and eleven miles of made road walked north between the fifth hour and the ninth by a man with a stick, which is under the six a sound man takes. **THE FIRST TIME ANYBODY IN THAT COUNTY HAS ASKED A PERSON TO ACCOUNT FOR A THING THEY DID ON THEIR OWN ACCOUNT, AND IT IS EARNED IN THAT MAN'S TRADE AND STANDS IN A RUT.** The roadkeeper of about fifty-two says first, **thirty words**: **You are not on my four miles and the road is wet, and a stick going down a made road is a hole in my day and not a guest.** The man of thirty-two stands out of the rut and asks, and gets **nineteen words**: **Carts were cutting it and I keep four miles, and nobody asked me to and nobody was going to.** **HIS MISCALCULATION IS THAT HE THOUGHT GOING TO LOOK WAS HIS OWN BUSINESS, and the four miles the roadkeeper keeps under a written piece of paper are the four miles below that weigh-house, which the man of thirty-two had walked four days earlier and had not once thought of as anybody's, and nobody told him a made road has an owner and that an owner keeps four miles of it and not the eleven. The soft place he is asked about is a mile short of the weigh-house and is not on the roadkeeper's own four miles at all, and the answer says so.** The cost is a leg gone from ache to a hot band across the knee by the sixth hour and an ear that took eleven miles of wind off the tops of the ditches into about nine seconds of ringing at once. He stands at the gate at the trough at the bottom of that cart track in failing light and does not go up it, and sleeps the night on the dry side of it, and comes back down the eleven miles in the morning and is in the four hundred pace lane at about the tenth hour of the Thursday after. The practice has run one hundred and two mornings and the number is four, the stone has been carried four hundred and thirteen days, and nobody has been up that track for a hundred and thirteen days. **His money column foots to one hundred and thirteen pence, which is two marks and seventeen over.**

**`chapter-0773.md`, 4,045 words.** Longlight the first, YR 323, day 2766, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut, and a man with a stick nine hundred yards short of nine hurdles. **THE SEVENTEENTH FIRST OF A MONTH IN A ROW, PRINTED ON THE PAGE, AND NOTHING AT THOSE BOARDS HAPPENED ON IT, AND THE ONE BEFORE IT WAS THE FIRST OF MUDMONTH IN WHICH ABOUT NINE PEOPLE SAID ONE THING EACH.** He goes up at the fourth hour, is at the head of that track at about the sixth, counts the nine hurdles from the left, and does not go past one of them. Nine houses about four hundred yards back in two rows, roofs in order, about four of the nine chimneys unswept that year, and not one smoke off them at about the sixth hour in a clear morning. **He knocks twice, listens about nine minutes, knocks twice more, and gets about nine seconds of sound behind the door and then not.** **HIS MISCALCULATION IS THAT HE HAD THOUGHT HE WAS GOING UP TO LOOK AT A ROAD, and a road with nine houses four hundred yards back and no smoke on them in clear daylight is not a question about a road, it is a question about nine people, and he had not thought about it that way and nobody told him to.** The cost is a hot band across the knee that came back the moment he stood on a rise and a high thin note in the ear since the second day. He comes down lighter than he went up and the page says that a man who comes down a hill lighter is a man who has decided something and not a man who has learned something. The drove is sixty, the practice has run one hundred and six mornings and the number is four, the stone has been carried four hundred and nineteen days, and nobody has been up that track for a hundred and nineteen days. **His money column foots to one hundred and forty-two pence, which is two marks and forty-six over.**

**`chapter-0774.md`, 4,041 words.** Longlight the seventh, YR 323, day 2772, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut, and nine hundred yards of cart track gone over for the second time in six days. He goes up at the fourth hour and stands nine feet short of those nine hurdles from about the sixth hour to about the ninth hour and does not sit down once. At about the ninth hour, **ONE OF THE NINE HOUSES PUTS A LIGHT IN ONE WINDOW, and it is the fourth house from the left in the near row.** He goes down the four hundred yards and knocks twice and there are nine seconds of nothing and then a voice against the inside of the door. It says first, **fourteen words**: **I can see your stick from here, so do not come up the yard.** He says back, **thirty-seven words**: **I am not coming up the yard and I am not going to touch a hurdle. I have been at the head of this track since the fourth hour and I want one thing said out loud.** Then the voice comes again, **twenty-nine words, and it gives the only name anybody at that place has given to anybody from this county in nine years**: **Nine houses and I am the fourth, and my name is Wyn Fell, and the hurdles went in by our own hands and we would like them to stay.** **THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL IS PAID HERE AND IT IS NOT A COMFORT.** He asks nothing else, puts nothing in writing, takes nothing out, the door does not open, and he stands on that step for about nine minutes. **HIS MISCALCULATION IS THE SIX DAYS BETWEEN THE TWO TRIPS, being that he had decided on the Tuesday what he was going to find on the Monday, and a man who decides what he will find does not find it, he recognises it.** The cost is a hot band by the seventh hour, a knee that went down at about the eighth hour and got him up on the second attempt with nobody seeing it but him, and an ear gone from a high thin note to a low one he could not sit inside for nine minutes at a time. The drove is sixty-one, the practice has run one hundred and ten mornings and the number is four, the stone has been carried four hundred and twenty-five days, the scar is a hundred and ninety-two days old, and nobody has been up that track for a hundred and twenty-five days. **His money column foots to one hundred and sixty-three pence, which is three marks and nineteen over.**

**`chapter-0775.md`, 4,055 words.** Longlight the fourteenth, YR 323, day 2779, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut, and a man with a stick at a stone trough at the bottom of a cart track. **He had gone up the eleven miles to that trough on the Saturday before and lain two nights under the hedge beside it, and at about the fourth hour of this Monday he is at the trough with a dead weight below the knee, and there is a man of about thirty-one at it with two buckets and a rope,** **whose name is Abel Chinn, and who fills that trough at about twice a week and carries water up eleven miles on his own back because there is no other water at the top of that track a house will drink.** The man at the trough speaks first, unasked, in his own trade, and says nine hurdles foot to foot and their own hands in September and that he counts what does not come up and that he has carried one woman over them and they went back the same evening. Then he asks seven words, **Are you going to write it down**, and gets nineteen words back, that there is nowhere in this county the man could write it down that a clerk would ever look at it, and gives twenty-six words: **Then do not write it down. Write it on something and put it where the rain cannot get at it, and do not tell me where.** **HIS MISCALCULATION IS THAT HE BELIEVED THE ONLY THING WRONG WITH THE NINTH THING WAS THAT NOBODY HAD LOOKED, and the looking is done and the thing is the same shape, and what is wrong with it is that anybody who knows is on the wrong side of nine hurdles and there is no name to put on a roll for either side.** The cost is a leg gone to a dead weight below the knee by the fourth hour and an ear at a low note all the way down. He gets into that market town at about the noon and does not say the thing in a street with about nine people in it. The drove is sixty-two, the practice has run one hundred and fifteen mornings and the number is four, the stone has been carried four hundred and thirty-two days, the scar is a hundred and ninety-nine days old, and nobody has been up that track for a hundred and thirty-two days. **His money column foots to one hundred and eighty-six pence, which is three marks and forty-two over.**

**`chapter-0776.md`, 4,026 words.** Longlight the eighteenth, YR 323, day 2783, a Friday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and a man of thirty-two on that floor for the one hundred and eighteenth morning not being one of the two. Seven people stand there at the fourth hour, the man of about thirty-four with a satchel says it first and the woman of about fifty-one says it again, and it is four, and the man of thirty-two is one of the seven and says nothing at all. **THE VOLUME'S ONE SYSTEM PANEL IS SPENT HERE AND NOWHERE ELSE, set alone as its own paragraph, not spoken aloud, carrying no number, and it is seventeen words and not the fifteen the state files printed before the repair pass at `state/continuity.md` 1741: Holding is said by two and not heard by nine, and the nine are not the holding.** **HIS MISCALCULATION IS THAT HE BELIEVED STANDING ON THE FLOOR WAS BEING IN IT, and a practice is said by two and the other five are the room, and he has been in the room for about four and a half years and has never once been one of the two, and there is no name in this county for that either.** He asks nobody anything on that floor, not the man of fifty-four about the chalk, not the woman of about fifty-one about the tally-book she is not allowed to have, and not the man of about thirty-four anything at all. Then he goes up to the wall, where the man of forty-one says **sixteen words** in his own trade, unasked and once, **I hold the wall. The gap is nine feet and I am not starting the ninth**, and goes back to the spade, and the man of thirty-two stands in that gap for about nine minutes and puts his hand on the near end of the eight finished yards and takes it off. The cost is a leg that took four hours on a stone floor and went from an ache to a needle from the second hour on, and an ear that had seven people breathing in the dark in it. The drove is sixty-three, the practice has run one hundred and eighteen mornings and the number is four, the stone has been carried four hundred and thirty-six days, the scar is two hundred and three days old, and nobody has been up that track for a hundred and thirty-six days. **His money column foots to two hundred and eight pence, which is four marks and sixteen over.**

**`chapter-0777.md`, 3,665 words.** Longlight the twenty-sixth, YR 323, day 2791, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open, the ninth hour with about four hundred people in it and a floor four days off that said no number that morning, and a man with a stick at a table saying one thing out loud. He says it at the second table past the second door, to about four hundred people and to none of them, in **forty-two words**: **I went up that track at the fourth hour and there was a light in one window of the fourth house at the ninth hour, and nine households have held it shut with nine hurdles, and they have no name against it.** Four people ask him four things and he answers all four, and then the fifth, which is whether anybody is going to lift a hurdle, and he says the second short thing in **twenty-nine words**: **I did not count them and I did not ask and I will not go up there again, and I am not going to tell anybody to lift one.** **HIS MISCALCULATION IS THAT HE BELIEVED FOUR HUNDRED HEARERS AND FOUR HUNDRED HOLDERS ARE THE SAME NUMBER, and a practice is said by two and the rest are the room, and about four hundred people are a room standing in the sun.** The cost is nine minutes standing at a table in about four hundred people's boots and a walk of about four paces that took about nine seconds, and an ear that took the market noise until it sang and had him turning his good side to every speaker. About nine people stopped at the table and about four hundred went on. The drove is sixty-four, the practice has run one hundred and twenty-three mornings with no number said that morning, the stone has been carried four hundred and forty-four days, the scar is two hundred and eleven days old, and nobody has been up that track for a hundred and forty-four days. **His money column foots to two hundred and twenty-nine pence, which is four marks and thirty-seven over.**

**`chapter-0778.md`, 4,241 words.** Rainmonth the fifth, YR 323, day 2800, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut, and a woman of about twenty-nine at the back of a day-book writing a figure down in her own hand for the first time in four and a half years. **THE PRACTICE IS WRITTEN DOWN FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THE VOLUME'S HISTORY, IN A HAND THAT IS NOT HIS, AND IT IS NOT IN ANY DOCUMENT.** She asks first, in her own trade, **twelve words**: **Where would you have me put it so that somebody reads it?** He answers in **thirty-seven words**: **There is no place. A roll wants a name against a line and I have no name and no line, and a register wants a finding and there is no finding under any of the ten questions.** Then she goes up thirteen stairs, shuts the door at the top, is inside about nine minutes, and says over her shoulder **thirty words**: **Then I will write it where a keeper writes the thing that is not an entry, and you will not read it and you will not tell me you have.** He stands in the lane about nine minutes in the rain and does not go up and does not knock, and when the door opens she comes down four steps and says **thirteen words**: **It is on the back leaf and it is not in the book.** **THE DAY-BOOK STANDS AT ELEVEN ENTRIES, THE REGISTER GAINS NO ENTRY, THE FOURTH RULED HEAD IS EMPTY, NO FIFTH HEAD IS RULED, THE COPYIST RULED NO HEAD THAT DAY AND HAS BEEN TOLD ABOUT NONE OF IT, AND A LEAF AT THE BACK OF THAT DAY-BOOK THAT CARRIES NO LINE AND NO RULING IS NOT THE RECORD, AND WHAT IS ON THAT LEAF IS NINE FIGURES AND A DATE AND THE NAMES OF NOBODY.** **IT COSTS HER SOMETHING AND THE COST IS NAMED: a keeper of records has one thing that is hers and it is her hand, and fourpence a year is what a person is paid for the side of the book the record is on.** The stick of chalk came off that stone sill on the Monday after he made it and is in a press drawer and the man of about fifty-four has not asked her for it and is not going to. The drove is sixty-five, the practice has run one hundred and thirty mornings and the number is four, the stone has been carried four hundred and fifty-three days, the scar is two hundred and twenty days old, and nobody has been up that track for a hundred and fifty-three days. **His money column foots to two hundred and fifty pence, which is five marks and ten over.**

**`chapter-0779.md`, 3,998 words.** Rainmonth the sixteenth, YR 323, day 2811, a Friday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and a woman of about fifty-one at the high end of that street asking a man of thirty-two for a figure he has not got. She comes out with a board about four feet long with a line ruled across it and nine spaces on it and a copy of a tally in front of her that she is not allowed to have. She says first, in her own trade, **twenty-seven words**: **I keep a board with a blank for every house on that road, and I cannot fill nine blanks without nine figures, and you went up there.** He answers in **thirty-two words**: **I got one light in one window from four hundred yards and I did not count a house, and if I had counted a house nobody would have believed it off me.** She looks at the nine spaces and says **thirty-eight words**: **Then it is a thing you have got and not a number, and four hundred people in that street have a thing and not a number, and a number is the only thing that goes on a board.** **THE FINDING CANNOT BE USED BECAUSE IT IS NOT A FIGURE, AND THE PAGE SAYS THAT A BOARD WITH NINE BLANKS ON IT IS THE NINTH THING IN THIS COUNTY IN A SECOND SHAPE, AND THE NINE SPACES WERE BLANK ON THE FRIDAY BEFORE AND THEY ARE BLANK NOW.** **HIS MISCALCULATION IS THAT THE WORK WAS FINDING THE NINTH THING, and the work was always the number, and a number is what his whole trade was made of, and a thing found with a leg and an ear and no arithmetic is a thing and not a figure.** The cost is two lengths of that street, a leg with nine feet of ache in it by the second length, and an ear that took the sound of an inn doing about nine beds. The drove is sixty-seven, the practice has run one hundred and thirty-eight mornings and the number is four, the stone has been carried four hundred and sixty-four days, the scar is two hundred and thirty-one days old, and nobody has been up that track for a hundred and sixty-four days. **His money column foots to two hundred and seventy-four pence, which is five marks and thirty-four over.**

**`chapter-0780.md`, 4,062 words.** Harvestmonth the first, YR 323, day 2826, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open, the ninth hour with a floor four days off that said no number that morning, and a man with a stick at the foot of thirteen stairs on the last day of seventy-two. **THE EIGHTEENTH FIRST OF A MONTH IN A ROW, PRINTED ON THE PAGE, AND NOTHING AT THOSE BOARDS HAS HAPPENED ON ANY OF THE EIGHTEEN.** At the boards the roadkeeper of about fifty-two says, in his own trade, to about nine people, **thirty words, and it is not about lifting anything**: **A bar will get up that track if a barrow can and I have said so out loud and I am not going to be one who tries it.** **AND THE PAGE SAYS THIS IS THE FIRST TIME ANYBODY IN THIS COUNTY HAS CLAIMED OUT LOUD THAT A SHUT ROAD CAN BE MADE TO SPEAK, AND THAT A CLAIM IS NOT A HAND ON A HURDLE, AND THAT A MAN WHO CAN MAKE A ROAD SPEAK AND WILL NOT IS THE MOST DANGEROUS THING IN THIS COUNTY, WHICH IS A MAN WHO KNOWS HOW.** The page also says that about nine people said one thing each on the first of Mudmonth and that not one of them has since offered any other person in nine counties a place to put their name either, and **that a refusal which is also a refusal to govern is the shape of the thing this county is going to have to answer.** **HIS MISCALCULATION IS THAT HE SPENT ABOUT NINE MONTHS LEARNING THAT A THING SOMEBODY CAN NAME IS NOT A THING ANYBODY HOLDS, and went up a lane to find out whether a road that has gone quiet is a road that is dead, and it is not, and there is nothing whatever he can do about it with power, and a man of thirty-two who has found that out is not a man with a finding and is a man with a job.** At about the eleventh hour he is nine feet off her at the wall and she comes down four of those stairs with the day-book under her arm and neither of them says anything about the leaf at the back of it or about anything else. The cost is nine minutes on the flags at the foot of those stairs and nine paces to the wall and back, and an ear that took about four hundred people and about four hundred carts until the market was one sound with a high note in it. The drove is sixty-nine, the practice has run one hundred and forty-eight mornings with no number said that morning, the stone has been carried four hundred and seventy-nine days, the scar is two hundred and forty-six days old, the last lime cart was a hundred and ninety-eight days ago, and nobody has been up that track for a hundred and seventy-nine days. **THE NINTH THING IS NOT DEAD AND IS NOT OPEN AND HAD NO NAME AGAINST IT AND WAS HELD BY NOBODY, AND THE PRINTED NOTICE IS PAST ITS DATE BY ABOUT FOUR MONTHS AND HAS SHUT NOTHING YET.** **His money column foots to two hundred and ninety-seven pence, which is six marks and nine over.**

## Volume 16, Batch 0004 — Chapters 781 to 790, "The Roll", days 2827 to 2898

**`chapter-0781.md`, 4,118 words.** Harvestmonth the second, YR 323, day 2827, a Sunday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and a Crown's cart going up the made road in the ordinary post with a roll in it. **THE ROLL ARRIVES AND IS NAILED TO THE BOARDS AT ABOUT THE ELEVENTH HOUR OF A SUNDAY WITH ABOUT NINE PEOPLE IN THE STREET. It is one printed sheet with a ruled line for each of nine counties, the fourth of them this county's, one space on each line for one name, and it prints the twenty-third of Fallowmonth as the day a road with no name against it is to be shut, which is fifty-one days off. AT THE FOOT OF A COVERING PAPER FOLDED OVER THE BACK OF IT, UNDER THE MARSHAL'S HAND, THERE IS A NAME WRITTEN OUT IN FULL, AND IT IS THE ANTAGONIST'S, AND IT IS THE FIRST PRINTING OF IT IN THIS VOLUME AND THE ONLY ONE IN THIS BLOCK, AND IT IS IN A DOCUMENT AND NEVER IN A MOUTH. He looked at it for about as long as it takes to turn a cart twice over and did not ask about it and nobody asked him anything.** The man of about fifty-four's chalk is not on this page. About nine people who said one thing each on the first of Mudmonth did not come and stand at it.

**`chapter-0782.md`, 4,289 words.** Harvestmonth the eighth, YR 323, day 2833, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open, about four hundred people in the street, and a floor four days off that said no number that morning. **TWO OF THE THREE ANSWERS TO THE ROLL ARE ON THIS PAGE. The first is reported from Thursday the sixth of Harvestmonth, day 2831, and is given by BRIDA LENTH, about forty-six, who keeps a lime kiln four miles north of that weigh-house and a stone-walled yard behind it, in thirty-one words, and her name is printed once on this page and is the one name this block spent against the volume's budget of six. The second is live, on this market morning, in forty-two words, and is given by Pell Arkwright, about twenty-seven, who drives the lime out of Wethergill and has not been paid for about two hundred and thirty loads, and he says that a road with no name against it is a road nobody can shut. THE PROTAGONIST SAYS NOTHING AT ALL ABOUT EITHER AND THE PAGE SAYS SO.** The copyist of about twenty-six comes down the stairs with a sheet in her hand and puts it into the hands of the woman of about twenty-nine and not into his, and says nothing about it.

**`chapter-0783.md`, 4,190 words.** Harvestmonth the tenth, YR 323, day 2835, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and the third of three answers to a roll of roads given out loud in a lane with about nine people in it. **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-FOUR SAYS SIXTEEN WORDS IN THAT LANE WHICH USE THE WORD KEEPER ABOUT THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE, AND NOBODY SAYS THE WORD WAS WRONG OR THAT IT WAS RIGHT. THEN SHE COMES DOWN FOUR OF THOSE THIRTEEN STAIRS AND SAYS THE THIRD ANSWER IN FORTY-FIVE WORDS: a form nobody is paid to fill is a form a county cannot fill, and there is nobody in nine counties paid to govern, and about fourpence a year is what a person is paid for the side of a book the record is on. AND THE PAGE SAYS THE THREE CANNOT BE HELD TOGETHER AND THAT NO COUNTY CAN HOLD TWO OF THEM.** He said nothing at all and did not knock and did not ask what the forty-five words had been for.

**`chapter-0784.md`, 3,790 words.** Harvestmonth the twenty-seventh, YR 323, day 2852, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open, about four hundred people in the street, and twenty-five days of a roll of roads on a board with nothing on any of its nine lines. **ABOUT NINE PEOPLE ASK HIM FOUR QUESTIONS AND HE ANSWERS NONE OF THEM, and the four questions are whether the roll is going to be filled in, who is going to fill it in, what happens on the Tuesday, and whether the hurdles at the head of the cart track come down. THE ROADKEEPER OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO IS AT THOSE BOARDS WITH A BAR IN HIS HAND AND SAYS NOTHING OUT LOUD AT ALL THAT MORNING AND GOES DOWN THE MADE ROAD WITHOUT TRYING ANYTHING.**

**`chapter-0785.md`, 3,830 words.** Fallowmonth the first, YR 323, day 2856, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and the nineteenth first of a month in a row arriving in that market street with nothing standing in it. **THE FIRST OF FALLOWMONTH CAME AND WENT AND ABOUT NINE PEOPLE SAW IT COME AND ABOUT NINE SAW IT GO, and the page says that the nineteenth first of a month in a row is not a record and is not a refusal and is not a holding. THE SECOND LAMP AT THE FOOT OF THOSE THIRTEEN STAIRS WENT OUT AT ABOUT THE SIXTH HOUR AND STOOD THERE LIT UNTIL ABOUT THE EIGHTH, and about four people in that lane worked out inside nine seconds that a lamp at the foot of a stair is not a thing a keeper leaves burning for want of oil.**

**`chapter-0786.md`, 3,707 words.** Fallowmonth the thirteenth, YR 323, day 2868, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open, about four hundred people in the street, and ten days between that market and the Tuesday printed on a roll of roads nailed to a board at the low end of that street. **HE CLEANS THE NINTH MILE STONE AT ABOUT THE SEVENTH HOUR AND THEN STANDS AT THOSE BOARDS FOR TWO HOURS, AND ABOUT NINE OF FOUR HUNDRED PEOPLE SAID ONE THING EACH AND NONE OF THEM SAID WHAT WAS GOING TO HAPPEN ON THE TUESDAY.** The page says that a county which cannot say what it will do about a Tuesday is a county which has decided to do nothing, which is a decision.

**`chapter-0787.md`, 4,052 words.** Fallowmonth the twenty-fourth, YR 323, day 2879, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and the morning after a Tuesday that a roll of roads had put ten days off and nothing had happened on. **WHAT HAPPENED ON THE TWENTY-THIRD OF FALLOWMONTH WAS THAT NOBODY FILLED IN THE ROLL AND NOBODY TORE IT UP AND NO GATE WAS SHUT IN THIS COUNTY ON THE STRENGTH OF IT AND NO BAR WAS PUT ON ANY GATE AND NO HURDLE WAS LIFTED OFF ANY TRACK AND NO ROAD WAS SHUT. AND THE PAGE SAYS THE REFUSAL WAS PAID ON THAT TUESDAY AND THAT BEING PAID IS NOT THE SAME AS BEING FINISHED.**

**`chapter-0788.md`, 4,072 words.** Embermonth the first, YR 323, day 2886, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and the twentieth first of a month in a row arriving with nothing standing at the boards and a Crown's cart going up the made road in the ordinary post with a second copy of the roll in it. **A SECOND COPY OF THE ROLL IS NAILED OVER THE FIRST WITHOUT THE FIRST ONE BEING TAKEN DOWN, and inside the covering paper folded over the back of it there is a sheet folded in four with one sentence on it in a hand that is not the hand of any clerk this county has ever had, and the sentence says that nobody in this county is paid to keep a road, and that a form a county cannot fill is not to be filled by writing somebody else's name on it, and that a county which will not govern ought to say so in writing to the people it is not governing. HE READ IT AND PUT IT BACK THE WAY IT WAS, AND NOBODY IN NINE COUNTIES HAS SAID WHO WROTE IT AND NOBODY IS GOING TO BE ASKED, AND THE PAGE DOES NOT SAY WHETHER SHE WROTE IT AND DOES NOT ASK HER.**

**`chapter-0789.md`, 4,227 words.** Embermonth the seventh, YR 323, day 2892, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and a man of thirty-two with a bad leg and one ear planning around that ear for the first time since about the fourth month and going up a made road on a morning when nobody in nine counties asked him to. **THE NEW COST OF THE BLOCK IS NAMED ONCE HERE AND NOT ARGUIED AFTERWARDS: that a thing he said out loud at a table in a market street on the twenty-sixth of Longlight cannot be unsaid, that about four hundred people heard it, that nine hundred people along eleven miles of made road behind that street have still never heard it and will never hear it, and that no second saying of it in a second street will put it into them. AND THE EAR ON THE SIDE THE STICK IS ON IS STATED HERE AS A FACT HE PLANS AROUND AND NOT AS A DISCOVERY, and he goes up four days of made road with his head turned to the left.**

**`chapter-0790.md`, 4,273 words.** Embermonth the thirteenth, YR 323, day 2898, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and the last of seventy-two days with a roll of nine lines on a board with nothing on it and a Tuesday gone and a road not shut. **THE COMPLETED BEAT, STATED ON THE PAGE: the roll is not filled in and is not torn up and has not been taken down and its nine lines are empty on both copies, and the form came back a second time on the first of Embermonth, and a road is not shut, because the twenty-third of Fallowmonth came and nothing was closed and nothing was filled in and nothing was torn up. THE NINTH THING IS STILL SHUT BY NINE HURDLES PUT THERE BY THE PEOPLE AT THE END OF IT WHO WOULD LIKE THEM TO STAY, AND NOBODY WENT UP THERE A THIRD TIME AND NOBODY LIFTED ONE. The four lines of chalk under the hood where the rain does not reach are still not writing and the leaf at the back of that day-book still carries nine figures and a date and the names of nobody. THE BADGE-MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-EIGHT STANDS AT THE ROLL AND READS NINE LINES AND A DATE AND WRITES NOTHING, AND THE PAGE SAYS THAT AN OCCUPATION WITH A GATE ON IT AND A KEEPER WHO IS A LOCAL IS ABOUT A COUNTER AND A DOOR AT THE LOW END OF THAT STREET WITH A MAN BEHIND IT WHO WAS BORN IN THAT STREET, AND THAT NOBODY IN NINE COUNTIES HAS EVER ASKED THAT MAN WHAT A GATE IS FOR.**

**`chapter-0791.md`, 4,408 words.** Embermonth the fourteenth, YR 323, day 2899, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and a man of thirty-two with a stick beginning to stay off that floor on purpose. **THE BLOCK OPENS BY NAMING WHAT THE PRACTICE IS AND WHAT IT IS NOT: there are ten things and about nine people on that floor, the number is said by two of them twice in the hour before light, a thing is held when those two have said out loud in the same hour that they are holding it and are both holding it again on the next morning, nine of the ten things have a person against them and two voices, and one of the ten has nobody against it and never has. HE TELLS NOBODY HE IS GOING TO BE OFF THAT FLOOR FOR ELEVEN MORNINGS AND HE HAS NOT WRITTEN IT DOWN, AND ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE IN NINE COUNTIES HAVE SAID THAT ELEVEN IS NOT A ROUND NUMBER AND THAT A MAN WHO HAS PICKED ELEVEN RATHER THAN TEN RATHER THAN NINE HAS BEEN COUNTING SOMETHING.**

**`chapter-0792.md`, 4,128 words.** Embermonth the twenty-third, YR 323, day 2908, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open, about four hundred people in the street, and a floor four days off that said no number that morning. **THE ONE NAME THIS BLOCK SPENT IS PRINTED HERE AND IT IS THE LAST OF THE VOLUME'S SIX: BESSIE DELLOW, about fifty-one, who keeps the inn at the high end of that market street and keeps a tally-stick.** A carter of about thirty-eight asks her one question, kindly, in his own trade, about what a person does with a second copy of a roll of roads nailed up at the low end of the street, and she answers in forty-three words, and then, when he asks what to call her, she tells him in twenty-four words. About four people in that room had known the inn and not known the name on it and five had not known either, and nobody said anything back to her, and about four hundred people went on buying string.

**`chapter-0793.md`, 4,164 words.** Embermonth the thirtieth, YR 323, day 2915, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open, about four hundred people in the street, the last day of a month, and a lamp that came down thirteen stairs and went back up them again without anybody asking for it. **THE RELATIONSHIP COMES DUE A FOURTH TIME AND IT IS A THIRD PERSON WORKING INSIDE THE TWO OF THEM AS THOUGH THE ARRANGEMENT OF THAT FLOOR WERE ALREADY A FACT OF THE WORK.** At about the fifth hour the copyist of about twenty-six comes down those stairs with the lamp that has been standing at the foot of them since the fourth hour and sets it on the trestle table by that weigh-house floor where about nine people are standing, with the man of thirty-two at the table and the woman of about twenty-nine in the lane with the day-book under her arm, and she changes the cloth on the table and goes back up and says nothing to anybody about it, and at about the seventh hour the lamp goes back up and the room it belongs to was open, and neither of the two of them said one word about it and neither looked up.

**`chapter-0794.md`, 4,185 words.** Wolfmonth the ninth, YR 323, day 2924, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open, about four hundred people in the street, and a man of thirty-two with a stick who has had blood on the inside of his left wrist for four days and has told nobody. **THE BLEED IS ON THE PAGE FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THE VOLUME AND IT IS NOT A PANEL AND IT IS NOT EXPLAINED: it started on the fifth of Wolfmonth at about the fourth hour at the ninth of the nine stones, where the man of about fifty-four stood about nine feet off and did not go past the ninth of them either, and what is beyond the ninth of those nine stones is not on this page and has not been for about a year, and it is warm and it does not stop when it is pressed and it has not faded and he has told nobody and it is in no document and nobody has asked him about it.** Five mornings of the practice have gone without him since the first of Wolfmonth and every one of them was held.

**`chapter-0795.md`, 4,425 words.** Wolfmonth the seventeenth, YR 323, day 2932, a Sunday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with nine people on it, and the man of thirty-two not one of the nine, and the eleventh morning of eleven. **THE GATEHOUSE FALLS AND IS NOT SHOWN AND IS NOT IN THIS COUNTY.** A Crown's cart brings a printed sheet up the made road in the ordinary post, does not stop at the boards, and a carter reads it out loud once in a room at the high end of that street, and about nine people hear all of it and five do not. **The sheet says that at about the sixth hour on the fifth of Wolfmonth, twelve days before, people with a Crown's warrant went up about nine hundred yards of a stair at a place nine days ride off, and that the gate at the top of it is off its hinges and the place and the stair are held, and that the seat which gave the warrant has not sat for eleven weeks and that about four hundred places are writing a paper by hand because nobody has a seal to give them. HE WAS FOUR HOURS OFF ON THAT VERY MORNING AT THE NINTH OF NINE STONES AND DID NOT KNOW FOR TWELVE DAYS, AND THE PAGE SAYS THAT THE NINTH THING IN THIS COUNTY AND A STAIR ABOUT NINE HUNDRED YARDS LONG ARE NOT THE SAME THING AND MUST NOT BE SAID TO BE.** The eleven mornings are named on the page.

**`chapter-0796.md`, 4,251 words.** Wolfmonth the twenty-fifth, YR 323, day 2940, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, twenty days of blood on the inside of one wrist that has not faded, and the last clear thing he will ever have of one woman. **THE SECOND NEW COST OF THE VOLUME IS ON THE PAGE AND IT IS NOT EXPLAINED: at the fourth hour, on a weigh-house floor in the dark with about seven people on it, it came to him that one woman said one sentence to him before he came into this county, and that he had heard it clearly then and had heard it clearly every morning since including that one, and that from that morning he will not hear it clearly again. HE DID NOT SAY IT OUT LOUD AND HE DID NOT PUT IT IN THE NOTEBOOK AND NOBODY ON THAT FLOOR HAS ANY IDEA IT HAPPENED TO HIM, AND THE EAR ON THE SIDE THE STICK IS ON IS THE SIDE SHE SPOKE FROM AND HE TURNED HIS HEAD TO THE LEFT FOR FOUR HOURS OF MADE ROAD BECAUSE OF IT AND NOT FOR ANY OTHER REASON.** He had come back to that floor on the eighteenth and about nine people had not said one word to him about the eleven mornings he was not there. **AND THE REPAIR AT `state/continuity.md` 1820: two kin-terms and nothing else in this file were rebuilt, and this summary carried the same two kin-terms and a wrong floor count of nine, where the ten files of this block print seven on that floor and seven of the nine, and all three figures are corrected here.**

**`chapter-0797.md`, 4,441 words.** Frostmonth the third, YR 323, day 2948, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with nine people on it, and a third copy of the roll coming up the made road in the ordinary post and being nailed over the second without the second being taken down. **THE SECOND AND LAST PRINTING OF THE ANTAGONIST'S NAME IN THE VOLUME IS HERE, IN A DOCUMENT, AT THE FOOT OF AN ENDORSEMENT SHEET THAT ALSO CARRIES A CLERK'S HAND AT ITS HEAD, AND NEVER IN A MOUTH AND NEVER READ OUT LOUD IN ANY ROOM.** The four lines are a date, the fourth line of the roll, a proposal to enter as the keeper of the roads of this county the man who keeps the weigh-house floor by his trade and not by his name, and a statement that the county's agreement is not required. He looked at it for about as long as it takes to turn a cart twice over, did not ask about it, put it back the way it was, and nobody in that street asked him anything, and the fourth line is empty on all three copies, and the man of about fifty-four has not been asked about it by anybody in nine counties.

**`chapter-0798.md`, 4,290 words.** Frostmonth the twelfth, YR 323, day 2957, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open, about four hundred people in the street, a trestle table set up at the boards, and a man of thirty-two with a stick saying one thing out loud in front of about four hundred people and nine of them stopping. **THE RESOLUTION OF THE WHOLE VOLUME IS SAID IN FORTY-TWO WORDS: *I am not the one who keeps any of it and I hold none of the nine, and the fourth line on that board is not mine to fill, and nobody in nine counties has asked me to fill it at all.* **THE SPEECH SAYS HE IS NOT THE ONE WHO KEEPS ANY OF IT, WHICH IS WHAT `outline/volume-16.md` SECTION TEN ITEM FIVE SAYS, and it does not say he is not one of the nine, because the narration of this chapter and of `chapter-0799.md` both say he is one of the nine and has been for about a year and the panel at `chapter-0776.md` prints that holding is said by two and not heard by nine. THE EARLIER WORDING SWAPPED THE TWO AND CONTRADICTED ALL THREE AND IS WITHDRAWN.** ABOUT NINE PEOPLE STOPPED WHERE THEY WERE AND ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED PEOPLE WENT ON AND NOBODY ARGUED WITH HIM ABOUT ANY PART OF IT.** He does not restate the three answers to the roll and is not the fourth voice of any of them. The badge-man of about fifty-eight reads nine lines and a date at the third copy and writes nothing and goes back the way he came, and nobody goes into the office at the low end of that street and nobody asks the man behind that counter what a gate is for.

**`chapter-0799.md`, 4,260 words.** Frostmonth the eighteenth, YR 323, day 2963, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with nine people on it, thirteen days to the first of Hearthmonth, and nothing on any of the boards in that street with a name on it. **THE STATE ON THE PAGE, ITEM BY ITEM: the roll has been up one hundred and thirty-six days and is not filled in and is not torn up and has not been taken down, no road has been shut on the strength of the notice or the roll, nobody has put a name against any road and nobody has written to anybody about why the fourth line is empty, the register holds ten questions with no finding under any of them, the day-book stands at eleven entries, the fourth ruled head is empty, no fifth head is ruled, and the leaf at the back of that day-book carries nine figures and a date and the names of nobody and is not the record.** A county which has answered nothing for four months and about half a month has not been defeated by the two papers and has not refused them either.

**`chapter-0800.md`, 4,396 words.** Frostmonth the twenty-fifth, YR 323, day 2970, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with nine people on it, six days before the first of Hearthmonth, and the last day of Volume 16. **THE COMPLETED BEAT: the roll is not filled in and is not torn up and its nine lines are empty on all three copies and the folded sheet is still inside the covering paper of the second copy in a hand nobody in nine counties has claimed, the four lines of chalk under the hood where the rain does not reach are still four and are still not writing, the ninth thing is alive and shut and unnamed and unheld behind nine hurdles, the tenth thing has never been held by anybody at all, eleven mornings of the practice went without him and every one of them was held, the scar is three hundred and ninety days old and the stone has been carried two miles in one man's own hands for six hundred and twenty-three days and the ninth yard has not been started, the practice has run two hundred and fifty-two mornings and the number is four, and the year is the cost. THE LAST LINE OF THE VOLUME IS A ROOM AND A FLOOR AND A LAMP AND A ROAD THAT SOMEBODY ELSE IS HOLDING, AND NOBODY IS THANKED ON THE TWENTY-FIFTH OF FROSTMONTH EITHER.**

---

## Volume 17, Batch 0001 — Chapters 801 to 810, "The Best Offer Anybody Here Has Been Made", days 2971 to 3012

**`chapter-0801.md`, 4,075 words.** Frostmonth the twenty-sixth, YR 323, day 2971, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open, the practice not running on it, about four hundred people in the street, and the first page of Volume 17. **THE OFFER ARRIVES IN THE ORDINARY POST AND IT IS NOT THE ROLL AND IT DOES NOT GO OVER IT.** A Crown's cart brings a printed sheet up the made road at about the ninth hour, the driver does not know what is in it, and it is nailed to the frame of the boards beside three copies of a roll with four nails and nothing is taken down. **THE COUNT OF THINGS ON THAT BOARD GOES TO FIVE AND THE COUNT OF QUESTIONS ON IT GOES TO THREE, AND THE PAGE SAYS SO, AND THE THREE QUESTIONS DO NOT AGREE WITH ONE ANOTHER.** The sheet asks for the name of one person to stand against the roads of nine counties and says under the Marshal's hand in two sentences that the place nine days ride off is being held so that what is called the Sundering is not done again in this world, and carries a date that is the twenty-ninth of Goatmonth, about nine weeks off. **THE SUPERLATIVE IS SAID OUT LOUD ONCE IN ELEVEN WORDS AND HERE MEANS THIS COUNTY, and the page gives the reason and says that about nine hundred people along eleven miles of made road have not heard it, and that the sheet is true in what it says and false in what it assumes and the volume does not decide who is right.**

**`chapter-0802.md`, 3,665 words.** Hearthmonth the first, YR 323, day 2976, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, five days after the sheet went up, and **the twenty-third first of a month in a row arriving with five things on the boards and nothing standing at them.** The practice runs and the number is four and is said twice by two people who are not him. About fifty-eight days is worked out inside nine seconds to be about eight weeks and about nine weeks off on the day it went up is about eight weeks off five days after it went up. One person reads the two sentences of the sheet out loud once, at the boards, standing up, to about nine people, and then says that it wants a name and that he does not know one in that street.

**`chapter-0803.md`, 4,169 words.** Hearthmonth the twelfth, YR 323, day 2987, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open, about four hundred people in the street, sixteen days after the sheet went up. **`Nell Thrush` IS SPENT, THE FIRST OF THE VOLUME'S SIX NAMES.** The man of about fifty-four who keeps that weigh-house floor asks a woman of about sixty-one one question about her own trade, kindly, in his own trade, and she answers in twenty-nine words that she keeps the weights and the ribbon and the county's one seal in a drawer and has weighed nothing in nine years, and he asks what to call her and she tells him in sixteen words that she would like the paper this county is copying to be worth something. **The four people who said the sheet was the best offer anybody in this county has ever been made are shown saying it out loud once each in four trades to about nine people each and nobody argues with any of them.** **The low room at the back of that office is shut behind its own door all morning and the drawer in it is not opened and nobody in nine counties went into it and nobody asked what a seal is for.**

**`chapter-0804.md`, 4,168 words.** Hearthmonth the fourteenth, YR 323, day 2989, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and the hand lamp put on the coping at the low side of that causeway four days south that afternoon. **`Ninian Crowe` IS SPENT, THE SECOND OF THE VOLUME'S SIX NAMES AND THE SECOND AND LAST SPEND OF THIS BLOCK.** He answers a question about his trade in thirty-one words, gives his name in twenty-four words, and then says one true thing out loud once in his own trade in twenty-seven words, namely that the four miles above the causeway are the smoothest four miles of made road in nine counties and that they were not smooth before somebody with a warrant came up them. **Nobody in nine counties argues with him and about four hundred people along eleven miles of made road do not hear a syllable of it, and the page says that he has begun to think that not having been heard is a kind of safety and that he is right about that and it is not safety.** The protagonist heard all three things and said nothing about any of them.

**`chapter-0805.md`, 3,787 words.** Hearthmonth the seventeenth, YR 323, day 2992, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open, about four hundred people in the street, twenty-one days after the sheet went up. **THE FIRST OF THREE ANSWERS GIVEN OUT LOUD IN ONE STREET IN ONE WEEK BY THREE PEOPLE WHO ARE NOT HIM, and it is a woman of about thirty-nine who keeps a bindle and does other people's washing, and it is forty-two words, and about nine people heard all of it and about five did not, and it is not a refusal of the sheet.** She refuses to stand at the board and say what nine households think, because when she says it they will all say she said it. **About nine people in nine counties had already said that she should not be asked anything and she was asked anyway, in her own trade, and she answered.** The second question on the board is the roll's own and has nine empty lines on each of three copies.

**`chapter-0806.md`, 3,886 words.** Hearthmonth the twenty-second, YR 323, day 2997, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and a man planing hurdle rails against the third door. **THE SECOND OF THE THREE ANSWERS, and it is a man of about forty-four who turns hurdles and hurdle rails and has made four hundred in his life, and it is thirty-seven words, and it is not a refusal of the sheet.** He refuses to put a name on a line, on the ground that every one of his four hundred hurdles went up where the person who wanted it put it. **The page says that this and the first cannot both be carried by a county of about two thousand people, and it does not resolve it, and he answers nothing and does not say it is wrong.**

**`chapter-0807.md`, 3,875 words.** Hearthmonth the twenty-sixth, YR 323, day 3001, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open, about four hundred people in the street, thirty days after the sheet went up and about five weeks before the date at the foot of it. **THE THIRD OF THE THREE ANSWERS, and it is a young man of about nineteen with five frames of brood under about nine straw skirts, and it is fifty-four words, and it is not a refusal of the sheet.** He refuses to promise what five frames will do in five weeks, on the ground that a name on a line does not change which of those two it is. **THE THREE CANNOT BE CARRIED BY ONE COUNTY AND NONE OF THEM IS A DECISION AND NONE IS A MOVEMENT. The second lamp at the foot of those thirteen stairs was lit at the fourth hour by the woman who keeps that room and the copyist did not come down them, and nobody said a word about either and nothing went in any document.**

**`chapter-0808.md`, 3,578 words.** Goatmonth the first, YR 323, day 3006, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open, about four hundred people in the street, **the twenty-fourth first of a month in a row arriving with five things on the boards**, thirty-five days after the sheet went up. **THE TURN: THIS COUNTY'S OWN COPY OF THE HAND PAPER IS BEGUN.** A person nobody in nine counties can name lays a sheet of foolscap on the flap of a stall and rules it into nine lines and writes a trade on each and no name on any and seals nothing and nails it nowhere and sends it nowhere. **The nine trades are a wheelwright, a saddler, a tanner, a slater, a cooper, a miller, a smith, a drover and a woman who keeps a dairy.** Nobody in nine counties has asked where it came from and nobody knows who else has one and that is the whole of what is known about it.

**`chapter-0809.md`, 3,997 words.** Goatmonth the fifth, YR 323, day 3010, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, and the hand lamp put on the coping at the low side of that causeway that afternoon. **THE FINDING: NOBODY CAN PUT A NAME ON A LINE AND EVERYBODY CAN PUT A TRADE ON ONE, AND A LINE WITH A TRADE ON IT IS A LINE SOMEBODY CAN BE ARGUED WITH, and a paper nobody can seal is not a freedom.** The man of about fifty-four who keeps that weigh-house floor says it in thirty-one words, being that a trade can be checked inside nine seconds and a name cannot because there is nobody to ask. **About nine people in that lane were asked what their own trade was and about nine answered inside nine seconds, and not one of the nine trades on those nine lines has been asked about by anybody and not one of the nine people whose trade it is has been asked whether it is there.**

**`chapter-0810.md`, 4,037 words.** Goatmonth the seventh, YR 323, day 3012, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, forty-one days after the sheet went up, and the last day of the first six weeks of this. **THE COMPLETED BEAT, AND IT IS NOT A CLIFFHANGER.** The sheet is on the boards beside three copies of a roll and the fourth line is empty on all three and the roll is not filled in and is not taken down and is not torn up. **The county's own copy of the hand paper has been begun and has nine lines and a trade on each and no name on any.** The four lines of chalk are under the hood where the rain does not reach and are still four and are still not writing. The register holds ten questions and gained no entry and no fifth head is ruled and the day-book stands at eleven entries and the leaf at the back of it carries nine figures and a date and the names of nobody. **The low room at the back of that office was shut behind its own door on every one of the six weeks and the drawer was not opened once and the seal is still in it, and nobody was asked what a seal is for.** **THE BLEED IS NINETY-TWO DAYS OLD AT THIS DATELINE AND HAS NOT FADED AND GETS NO PANEL AND NO EXPLANATION, and the page says that a figure in one woman's hand on the back of a day-book is not a cure and neither is a sentence in an unclaimed hand on a folded sheet and neither is four lines of chalk on a sill.**

---

## Volume 17, Batch 0002 — Chapters 811 to 820, "The Counter and the Door", days 3013 to 3054

**`chapter-0811.md`, 3,862 words as recounted after the review repair.** Goatmonth the eighth, YR 323, day 3013, a Thursday and a market morning, the room over the weigh-house open, the hearing not sitting, the practice not running on it, about four hundred people in the street, forty-two days after a Crown's cart nailed a printed sheet beside three copies of a roll and not over them, and twenty-one days still to run on the date at the foot of it. **`Corryn Falder` IS SPENT, THE THIRD OF THE VOLUME'S SIX NAMES.** The man of thirty-two with the stick asks a man of about fifty-one one question about his own trade, kindly, in that man's own trade, being who keeps the trades' second book of this county and where it is kept and what goes in it, and he answers in fifty-two words that he has kept it about nine years and that every trade in this county is in it against the name of the man who keeps it and that it is the only place in nine counties where a person and a trade stand together. **He is then asked what to call him because somebody wants to know who to ask a second time.** Half an hour later he says one more thing out loud once in his own trade to about nine people, being that he wants a trade against a name in one book instead of two and cannot have it because the woman who keeps the other one will not rule a head for it, **and nobody argues with him and nobody asks him why he thinks she would and nobody asks her either, and the fourth ruled head is still empty.** **The finding is begun here in the narration only, being that a counter and a door and a man who was born in that street is the cheapest instrument an occupation has, being that it needs nobody to travel and nobody to be paid and nobody to agree, and about five have said that the cheapest instrument is also the one nobody in nine counties has looked at, and neither of those is on any paper in this county and neither was said out loud.** **The low room at the back of that office was shut behind its own door on every one of the seven weeks and nobody in nine counties went into it and the drawer eleven inches deep in it was not opened once and the county's one seal and its ribbon and a set of standard weights are still in it and nobody has asked what a seal is for.** **REVIEW REPAIR ON THIS PAGE:** the fourth-hour claim is scoped to the morning and no longer claims that no fourth hour has run in that room for forty-two days, which the block's own non-market datelines contradict; the commentary about the drove, the badge-man, the empty ruled head, the second book, the broom and the nine households is now narration with no fact lost; the bleed's closer no longer repeats the clause; and the cheapest-instrument beat was kept on purpose, being that `state/current.md` item 5 quotes it.

**`chapter-0812.md`, 3,779 words as recounted after the review repair.** Goatmonth the thirteenth, YR 323, day 3018, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, forty-seven days after the sheet went up, and sixteen days still to run on the date at the foot of it. **THE PRACTICE RUNS AND IT IS THE TWO HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-SIXTH MORNING AND THE NUMBER IS FOUR AND IS SAID TWICE.** The six mornings lost in the nine days of water in Mudmonth are still six. The second lamp at the foot of the thirteen stairs is lit at the fourth hour and goes out at the sixth and nothing about the four lines of chalk on the sill goes in with it. **THE CHAPTER IS THE BLOCK'S TEST HALF-WORKED OUT AND IT IS NARRATION ONLY: the man of thirty-two stands opposite the office for about two hours and works out that a counter and a door and a man who was born in that street is the cheapest instrument an occupation has and that about nine hundred people along eleven miles of made road have never noticed the counter at all, and that a counter asks nothing of anybody, which is the other side of the four and a half years he spent learning to stop being asked where a figure came from. HE DOES NOT GO IN AND THERE WAS NO MOMENT AT WHICH HE DECIDED NOT TO, and the low room stays shut behind its own door and the drawer is not opened.** **REVIEW REPAIR ON THIS PAGE:** the census commentary about the counter is now the narrator's own voice and stands in one paragraph where it stood in three, with the finding itself kept; the door, the badge-man, the county length, the nine trades, the sill, the third course, the three series and the leg are all still on the page; the bleed's closer varies; and physical measurements were added to hold the `about` band, being the four-pace room, the eleven-inch drawer, the five-foot frame, the nine-foot coping and the about-two-mile carry.

**`chapter-0813.md`, 3,360 words as recounted after the review repair.** Goatmonth the twenty-fourth, YR 323, day 3029, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open, the practice not running on it, about four hundred people in the street, and **five days left in the date at the foot of that sheet.** **ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE IN THAT STREET HAD WORKED OUT INSIDE NINE SECONDS THAT FIVE DAYS IS A WORKING WEEK AND ABOUT FIVE HAD NOT, and about four hundred people walked past it at the ninth hour and about nine looked at it and nobody in nine counties has been asked what a county is supposed to do on the thirtieth of Goatmonth.** A man who hauls stone for a quarry two miles off is asked one question about his own trade, kindly, about what a person does on a day there is no market, and answers in forty-two words that five days is a working week and a week is how long it takes to get a thing done and not how long it takes to think about doing it. **Nothing is shut on the date and nothing is opened and the roll is two hundred and two days on that frame with the fourth line empty on all three of its copies and this county's copy of the hand paper is twenty-three days old with nine trades on it and no names on any of them.** **REVIEW REPAIR ON THIS PAGE:** the fourth-hour claim is scoped to the morning; the date commentary, the counter commentary and the three-series commentary are now narration; the chapter keeps its census beats, being the working week read inside nine seconds, who heard the stone-hauler and what they heard; and the bleed's closer keeps the old clause, which is now the only page of the block carrying it in that tense.

**`chapter-0814.md`, 3,348 words as recounted after the review repair.** Thawmonth the first, YR 324, day 3036, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open, the hearing not sitting, the practice not running on it, about four hundred people in that street, sixty-five days after the sheet went up, and **two days past the date at the foot of it. THE YEAR TURNS HERE AND IT TURNS ON A MARKET MORNING FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS MANUSCRIPT'S MEMORY, AND THIS IS THE TWENTY-FIFTH FIRST OF A MONTH IN A ROW, CARRYING FORWARD FROM TWENTY-FOURTH AND NOT BEGUN AGAIN.** **The first of Thawmonth comes to those boards and finds five things on them and nothing standing at them, and about four people in that street had worked out inside nine seconds what a year is and about five had not.** The date is two days past and nothing in nine counties is shut on it and about nine hundred people along eleven miles of made road never knew there was a date on it at all. **AND THE PAGE PRINTS WITHOUT MAKING ANYTHING OF IT THAT THE TWENTY-NINTH OF GOATMONTH WAS A THURSDAY AND THAT A DROVE WENT UP A LANE FOUR DAYS OFF THAT SAME THURSDAY AND STOOD IN THE FIELD INSIDE THE STONE WALL WITH TWO MEN, BEING THE NINETY-NINTH TO GO UP THAT LANE SINCE THE SECOND OF MUDMONTH.** A woman of about thirty-one who keeps a bakehouse four miles off, in whose back room the trades' second book has been kept for about nine years, is asked one question about her own trade and answers in sixty-two words that her oven is lit on the first of every month as well as on the two market days and that about four people know that and about five do not and that she has never sold a loaf of bread on the strength of it. **Nobody argued with her and nobody argued with a man of about thirty-eight about four miles of made road.** **REVIEW REPAIR ON THIS PAGE:** the fourth-hour claim is scoped to the morning, **and the frame break *for the first time in this manuscript's memory* is repaired to *for the first time in living memory in this county***; the first-of-a-month commentary, the drove commentary, the naming commentary, the sill, the brood, the nine households and the third course are now narration; and the chapter keeps three census beats including the year read inside nine seconds.

**`chapter-0815.md`, 3,229 words as recounted after the review repair.** Thawmonth the fourth, YR 324, day 3039, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, three days after the year turned, and **five days past the date at the foot of that sheet.** **THE MAN OF FIFTY-ONE COMES UP THAT STREET ON A TUESDAY FOR THE FIRST TIME IN ABOUT NINE YEARS AND STANDS AT THE FLAP OF THE STALL WITH THE NINE LINES ON IT.** He is asked one question about his own trade, kindly, about what a person does with a book on a day there is no market, and answers in fifty-five words that he has kept that book about nine years and that about nine hundred names are in it against a trade and that there are nine trades on that flap with no name on any of them and that in about nine years nobody has ever put one of those two things beside the other and looked at them. **He then says he is going to go to the woman who keeps the other book, because that is the only place in this county where what he wants is a ruled head and not an argument, and the two of them go in opposite directions and neither says anything else.** The practice is the three hundred and first morning and the number is four and four of the seven say it straight away and about three wait for somebody else. **Nothing is shut and the low room stays shut behind its own door and the drawer is not opened.** **REVIEW REPAIR ON THIS PAGE:** the commentary about interrogation, about the book as a roll of roads, about the counter noticing and about the unjoined papers is now narration; a paragraph of concrete detail was added about the byre floor four days off and the wet in the night, being that this was the shortest page in the block at 3,168 words and the census cut would otherwise have taken it under three thousand; and the bleed's closer now says that no person in nine counties has ever been shown it.

**`chapter-0816.md`, 3,414 words as recounted after the review repair.** Thawmonth the sixth, YR 324, day 3041, a Thursday and a market morning, the room open, the practice not running on it, about four hundred people in that street, seventy days after the sheet went up, seven days past the date at the foot of it, and **a hundredth drove going up a lane four days off at the sixth hour.** **THE DOORWAY, AND THE PERSON IN IT IS NOT THE PROTAGONIST.** **At about the tenth hour, at the low end of that market street, a man of about thirty-four and unnamed stands in the doorway of the low room at the back of the Crown's road office and does not go in.** There were about nine people in that street at that hour and **about four of them saw him stand there and about five did not.** He had come up eleven miles of made road with an empty cart behind a horse that did not need to be there. He stood in the doorway for about as long as it takes to read the sheet at the low end of the street twice, and he did not touch the door and did not knock and did not sit on the clerk's table four feet inside it, **and at about the eleventh hour he went away and the door was shut behind him and nobody followed him and nobody asked him why he had come.** **THE MAN OF THIRTY-TWO WAS FOUR HUNDRED PACES AWAY AT THE FRAME AT THE LOW END OF THAT STREET AT THE TIME AND WAS NOT AT THAT DOOR, and he found out about it afterwards and did not go and look at the doorway.** The man behind the counter, who was born in that street and has been at that counter for thirty-one years, says it once out loud in his own trade in sixty-three words and then adds that he has the key to that drawer in his pocket and has not used it, and that nobody has opened it in about nine years. **The hundredth drove went up at about the sixth hour into a field with a stone wall round it and two men, and nobody in nine counties has ever watched one go up, and about four people in nine counties have said that a hundred is a figure people argue about and that nobody has ever argued about this one. The drawer was not opened and the low room was not entered by anybody and nobody in nine counties has asked what a seal is for.** **REVIEW REPAIR ON THIS PAGE:** the fourth-hour claim is scoped to the morning; **the doorway beat is kept whole, including the four who saw him and the five who did not, which `state/continuity.md` 1827 quotes**; the hundredth-drove commentary and the counter commentary are now narration; the second time the counter-man came out to the boards is no longer dated to seven weeks, which did not foot; and the bleed's closer now says there is no box for it and no shelf for it and no register it could go in.

**`chapter-0817.md`, 3,207 words as recounted after the review repair.** Thawmonth the tenth, YR 324, day 3045, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, a week after a person stood in a doorway and did not go in, and **the hand lamp put on the coping at the low side of that causeway four days south that afternoon.** **THE MAN BEHIND THE COUNTER IS ASKED WHAT HE DOES ALL DAY, IN HIS OWN TRADE, AND ANSWERS IN SIXTY-ONE WORDS, being that he sells a copy of the notice at a penny and a copy of anything else at a halfpenny and writes the day and the weather in a book, and that about nine things a week is the whole of it, and that he has stood behind that counter thirty-one years and nobody has ever asked him what a gate is for.** Nobody argued with him about any part of it. **About four people in nine counties have said that a penny is the cheapest instrument anybody in nine counties has ever had for telling nine counties what a county has been offered, and about five have said that a penny is also the cheapest instrument anybody has ever had for telling a county what it has been offered by, and that those two things have not been said in the same sentence by anybody in nine counties.** The practice is the three hundred and fifth morning and one of them says she has thought about the hundred all week and worked out that it is a figure she did not make. **The low room stays shut behind its own door, the drawer is not opened, and the man of about thirty-four who stood in that doorway a week before has not come back and nobody has asked where he went.** **REVIEW REPAIR ON THIS PAGE:** **the dateline's *a week after a person stood in a doorway* is corrected to four days, and the same slip at the frame and in the cost coda is corrected with it, being 3041 to 3045**; the counter commentary, the penny commentary and the two-books commentary are now narration; the counter-man's one question is dated to the last month instead of seven weeks, which did not foot; and the bleed's closer now says that no clerk has ever entered it in anything.

**`chapter-0818.md`, 3,233 words as recounted after the review repair.** Thawmonth the fifteenth, YR 324, day 3050, a Saturday and a market morning, the room open, the practice not running on it, about four hundred people in that street, seventy-nine days after the sheet went up, sixteen days past the date at the foot of it, **a man of fifty-one going up thirteen stairs for the first time in about nine years, and a woman of about twenty-nine saying no. THE COST.** He asks the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room over that weigh-house for a fifth ruled head on that page, in her own trade, asked to a person who keeps records, on a Saturday morning with about four hundred people in the street below and none of them on those stairs, **and she says no in fifty-three words**, on the ground that a ruled head is a head somebody has to be wrong about and that she has not been wrong about anybody in that room since the first of Hearthmonth and is not going to start on a Saturday. Nobody said anything back to him for as long as it takes to turn a cart twice over. **He asks her not for an account of it but whether she would take a fortnight, and she says a fortnight is longer than four and a half years and that it would be the same answer on the thirtieth of Goatmonth and on the first of a month in the year after that.** **THE COPYIST OF ABOUT TWENTY-SIX CAME TO THE TOP OF THOSE STAIRS WITH THE DAY-BOOK UNDER HER ARM AND WAS IN THE ROOM FOR THE LAST OF IT AND WAS TOLD ABOUT NONE OF IT AND RULED NO HEAD, and the fourth ruled head of that page is still empty and no fifth head is ruled anywhere in this county.** He comes down the stairs at about the eleventh hour and says nothing on the way down. **THE PROTAGONIST WAS FOUR HUNDRED PACES DOWN THAT LANE AT THE BOARDS AND WAS NOT ON THOSE STAIRS AND WAS NOT ASKED WHAT A HEAD IS FOR.** **THE LOW ROOM STAYS SHUT BEHIND ITS OWN DOOR AND THE DRAWER IS NOT OPENED AND NOBODY HAS ASKED WHAT A SEAL IS FOR.** **REVIEW REPAIR ON THIS PAGE:** **the page's *a hundred and two days of a sheet* is corrected to seventy-nine, which is 3050 less 2971 and which its own dateline already said**; the fourth-hour claim is scoped to the morning; the refusal and the register and the cost paragraphs are untouched in substance and the hearing beats are plain narration now; the bleed's closer keeps the clause and adds that nobody is going to give it one; and the fifty-three word refusal still foots.

**`chapter-0819.md`, 3,485 words as recounted after the review repair.** Thawmonth the seventeenth, YR 324, day 3052, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, eighty-one days after the sheet went up, and **the hand lamp going on a coping four days south that afternoon.** The practice is the three hundred and tenth morning and the number is four and nobody asked the man who came down those stairs with nothing on Saturday about it, and about four people in nine counties have said that is the correct number of things to ask him about. **A man of about sixty who puts that hand lamp on that coping every Monday afternoon in the winter for about nine years is asked one question about his own trade and answers in fifty words that he puts it out about two hours before the light goes and takes it in about as long after it, and that about four hundred people on the far bank have never once seen it lit, and that about nine have told him they have seen it from their own door.** **THE MAN OF THIRTY-TWO WORKS OUT THE SHAPE OF IT, being that about nine hundred names in a book four miles off and about nine trades on a flap of a stall are enough to answer a paper with one line on it, and that the one thing they cannot do is be written down anywhere in this county, and that about four and a half years is how long it has taken this county to arrive at having everything it needs and nothing it may use.** The hearing with no bench in it cannot be called and does not sit and the twenty-sixth first of a month in a row is a fortnight away. **About four hundred yards of laid kerb go north on the far bank of that causeway and then grass and a shut door, and about four people in nine counties have said that about nine hundred yards of kerb on the wrong side of a causeway is a road somebody has been paid to lay since the fifth of Wolfmonth, and about five have said that nobody on this side has asked by whom, and about four have said that a road which is somebody else's decision is still a road and still gets walked on. Nobody went up that cart track and the low room stays shut behind its own door and the drawer is not opened.** **REVIEW REPAIR ON THIS PAGE:** the lamp, the bell, the practice, the kerb and the refusal commentary are now narration; the chapter keeps the hearing beat at the lamp-man's speech and the thumb-drag beat at the sill; the bleed's closer now says it is the only thing about him that has never once been written down anywhere and that he has never once mentioned; and the fifty word speech still foots.

**`chapter-0820.md`, 4,112 words as recounted after the review repair.** Thawmonth the nineteenth, YR 324, day 3054, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, the hearing not sitting, no market in that street, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, eighty-three days after the sheet went up, **twenty days past the date at the foot of that sheet**, and about four hundred places writing a paper that nobody in nine counties can seal. **THE CLOSE, AND THE FINDING, AND IT IS STATED ONCE AND IT IS NOT RESOLVED.** About four hundred places are writing a paper by hand because nobody has a seal to give them, and **no two of about four hundred copies agree with one another because nobody has asked them to.** This county has joined them, and its copy has nine lines on it with a trade on each and no name on any and no seal on it, **and nobody in nine counties has been asked whose trade is on any of its nine lines and nobody in nine counties knows who else has a copy.** **A PAPER NOBODY CAN SEAL BINDS NOBODY. THAT IS SAFE.** **A COUNTY WHICH CAN ONLY REFUSE CANNOT HOLD. THAT IS NOT.** **Neither half of that is a rescue and neither is a plan, and a paper nobody can seal is not a freedom and is not the same as a paper nobody has to obey, and about nine hundred people along eleven miles of made road could walk up to that frame tomorrow morning and take all five things down and about four of them might and about five would not and nobody in nine counties could say they had stolen anything.** About four hundred people in this county have everything it would need to hold and no place it may put any of it. **THE FIVE THINGS ARE ON THE BOARD AND THREE QUESTIONS ARE ON IT AND THEY DO NOT AGREE WITH ONE ANOTHER, THE FOURTH LINE OF THE ROLL IS EMPTY ON ALL THREE COPIES AND IS STILL EMPTY, THE ROLL IS NOT FILLED IN AND IS NOT TAKEN DOWN AND IS NOT TORN UP, THE NEW SHEET STAYS NAILED UP BESIDE THE ROLL AND NOT OVER IT, THE FOUR LINES OF CHALK UNDER THE HOOD WHERE THE RAIN DOES NOT REACH ARE STILL FOUR AND ARE STILL NOT WRITING, THE REGISTER HOLDS TEN QUESTIONS AND GAINS NO ENTRY, NO FIFTH HEAD IS RULED, THE DAY-BOOK STANDS AT ELEVEN ENTRIES, THE LEAF AT THE BACK OF IT CARRIES NINE FIGURES AND A DATE AND THE NAMES OF NOBODY, THE BLEED IS ONE HUNDRED AND THIRTY-FOUR DAYS OLD AND HAS NOT FADED AND GETS NO PANEL AND NO EXPLANATION AND A BLEED THAT HAS NOT FADED IN ONE HUNDRED AND THIRTY-FOUR DAYS IS NOT A CURE, THE LOW ROOM WAS NOT ENTERED AND THE DRAWER IN IT WAS NOT OPENED AND THE PROTAGONIST WAS NOT AT ITS DOOR, NOBODY IS PAST THE NINTH OF THE NINE STONES AND THE FAR SIDE OF THEM IS NOT SHOWN AND THE OMISSION IS A DECISION, NOBODY WENT UP THAT CART TRACK AND NO HURDLE WAS LIFTED AND NO BAR WAS TRIED, THE NINTH YARD OF THE WALL DOES NOT START AND THE MALLET IS NOT PICKED UP AND THE THIRD COURSE OF SILL IS UNLAID, AND NOBODY WAS THANKED FOR ANY OF IT.**
 **REVIEW REPAIR ON THIS PAGE:** ***was refused in his own trade nine days ago* is corrected to four days, which is 3054 less 3050**; **the drawer's span is corrected from six weeks to the eighty-three days since the twenty-sixth of Frostmonth, in the paragraph and in the cost coda, and the hand paper's age is corrected from about six weeks to forty-eight days, which is 3054 less 3006**; the commentary about four hundred places and about neither half being a rescue is kept, being the close of the block; and the bleed's closer keeps the clause and the four things that are not a cure at one hundred and thirty-four days.

## Volume 17, Batch 0003 — Chapters 821 to 830, "What the Notes Would Cost", days 3055 to 3096
