**`chapter-0821.md`, 3,236 words.** Thawmonth the twentieth, YR 324, day 3055, a Thursday and a market morning, the room over the weigh-house open and the hearing not sitting, about four hundred people in that street, eighty-four days after a sheet went up on those boards, twenty-one days past the date at the foot of that sheet, and four copied pages coming up the made road in the ordinary post with the man who had copied them. **THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL BEGINS AS AN ORDINARY DELIVERY.** A Crown's cart, the fourth up that road since the twenty-sixth of Frostmonth, carries two satchels and its driver does not know what is in them, and on the seat beside him is a man of about forty-four with a bundle about nine inches deep and about eleven inches wide wrapped twice in brown paper and oiled cloth. Each of the four sheets inside is foolscap in a different hand, and each carries a column of nine roads down the left of it and beside each of those nine one fixed point out of that road's own past, being a season and a year and in two cases a month and in one case an hour and in one case nothing at all. **`Tosh Sennen` IS SPENT, THE FOURTH OF THE VOLUME'S SIX NAMES, and he is named the way everything in this county gives a name:** the man of thirty-two asks him one question about his own trade, kindly, at the low end of that market street, about what a person in this county does with a document that no clerk will touch, and he answers in forty-seven words that he copies what people here cannot pay a clerk to copy, has copied about nine hundred of them in about nine years, that the copy is the whole of what he does and that the paper it is on is theirs and not his. **He is then asked what to call him because somebody wants to know who to ask a second time.** About nine people in that street did not ask him whether the four sheets were right and he had been waiting about nine years for one of them to. **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE, WHO KEEPS THAT ROOM, HAD KNOWN WHAT WAS IN THE BUNDLE BEFORE IT CAME UP THE ROAD, and about four people in that street had worked that out inside nine seconds and about five had not, and she says one sentence to him in her own trade in forty words, being that he is to take them up and set them on the board by the sill and that by the second hour tomorrow she will tell him whether what is on them is what somebody put on them, and that is the whole of it.** Neither of them wrote it down. **THE REGISTER GAINED NO ENTRY AND THE DAY-BOOK GAINED NONE AND THE FOURTH RULED HEAD IS STILL EMPTY AND NO FIFTH HEAD IS RULED, and the copyist of about twenty-six was in the room for the whole of it and was told about none of it and ruled no head.** The low room at the back of that office was shut behind its own door and the drawer eleven inches deep in it was not opened once, and the county's one seal and its ribbon and a set of standard weights are still in it, and nobody has asked what a seal is for.

**`chapter-0822.md`, 3,373 words.** Thawmonth the twenty-fourth, YR 324, day 3059, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, eighty-eight days after a sheet went up, twenty-five days past the date, four sheets of foolscap on a board under a sill in four hands, and a man of thirty-two going up the made road at about the sixth hour for four days. **THE PRACTICE RUNS AND IT IS THE THREE HUNDRED AND FIFTEENTH MORNING AND THE NUMBER IS FOUR AND IS SAID TWICE, and the six mornings lost to the nine days of water in Mudmonth are still six.** He goes off with his stick and a crust of bread in a fold of cloth at about the sixth hour and about four people in nine counties knew where he had gone and about five did not. **The four hands are described on the page: a narrow upright hand with thin downstrokes, a fast hand, four words to a line with a good deal of white between, and a hand that writes low on the page close to the edge. Nobody in nine counties knows who wrote any of the four, or when, or for whom, or for what, and nobody has asked. Nothing on them says where anything is shut, nothing asks anybody for anything, and nothing carries a seal.** The woman of about twenty-nine asks `Tosh Sennen` one question about his own trade, being how many of the things he has copied he has read back to the person he copied them for, and he answers in one word: nine. **He then says that about nine hundred people who cannot pay a clerk have never once been asked it by anybody, and she says in twenty words that read back is the half of the trade that finds out whether the paper is what was put on it, and he says that he had begun to think of reading back as a favour he does people and that it is not a favour.** The copyist of about twenty-six is in the room for the whole of it and is told none of it. He goes back down eleven miles of made road in the ordinary post with nothing in his hands, having allowed himself a fortnight and used six days of it. **The hand lamp goes on the coping at the low side of the causeway four days south that afternoon, and it is a Monday.** The low room is not entered and the drawer is not opened.

**`chapter-0823.md`, 3,095 words.** Thawmonth the twenty-seventh, YR 324, day 3062, a Thursday and a market morning, about four hundred people in that street, ninety-one days after a sheet went up, twenty-eight days past the date, four copied pages authenticated in the room over the weigh-house in her own trade, and a man of thirty-two the fourth day off a made road with nothing to do with any of it. **THE AUTHENTICATION, AND IT MEANS THAT WHAT IS ON THE PAGES IS WHAT SOMEBODY PUT ON THEM, AND SHE SAYS SO IN SIXTY-FOUR WORDS AND ADDS NINE, AND THE NINE ARE THAT A PAGE THAT IS AUTHENTICATED IS NOT CONSENTED TO. IT IS NOT A SAYING THAT THE ROADS ARE THE ROADS AND IT IS NOT ANYBODY CONSENTING TO ANYTHING, and about four people on that floor heard the whole of it and about five heard the middle of it and went on looking at the boards.** She shuts the door at the top of those thirteen stairs, which she does not do on a market morning. **THE REGISTER GAINED NO ENTRY AND THE DAY-BOOK STOOD AT ELEVEN ENTRIES AND THERE IS NOTHING IN IT ABOUT FOUR PAGES, and the copyist of about twenty-six ruled no head and was told about none of it.** The four sheets go into the press on the top shelf, above the half leaf, which is face down where it was. **Then she turns the fourth sheet over on the board and reads the last line of it out loud, because it is on the fourth sheet and she had read the whole of it, and what is written on that last line is: Written to be given to Orla, and she is not at this address, and the place it was written in is not named here. THAT IS THE ONLY NAME ON ANY OF THE FOUR PAGES AND IT IS THE ONLY NAME ANYBODY IN THIS COUNTY HAS READ OUT LOUD IN A ROOM IN ABOUT FOUR AND A HALF YEARS. HIS MOTHER'S NAME IS PRINTED HERE AND ONLY HERE IN VOLUME 17.** **The man of thirty-two was the fourth day off a made road and about nine miles short of the head of it, at a wall of eight yards that is back in and nine that is not, with the line of stone on the bank and the mallet on top of it. He was not in that room and was not on that street and was not in this county. HE HAS NOT BEEN CALLED BY THAT NAME SINCE HE WAS CARRIED OUT OF A PLACE THESE FOUR PAGES DO NOT NAME, and it was not said to him and he was not told it that week and nothing about it went into a book.** The low room is not entered and the drawer is not opened and nobody asked what a seal is for.

**`chapter-0824.md`, 3,081 words.** Mudmonth the first, YR 324, day 3066, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, the first of a month and a month carried at sixty days, ninety-five days after a sheet went up, thirty-two days past the date, and a man of thirty-two back down a made road and told a thing. **THE FIRST OF MUDMONTH CAME TO THOSE BOARDS AND FOUND FIVE THINGS ON THEM AND NOTHING STANDING AT ANY OF THEM, AND THAT IS THE TWENTY-SIXTH FIRST OF A MONTH IN A ROW TO DO SO, and the count carries forward from twenty-fifth and is not begun again.** The practice runs and it is the three hundred and twentieth morning and the number is four and is said twice. **AT ABOUT THE SEVENTH HOUR HE GOES UP THOSE THIRTEEN STAIRS AND SHE TELLS HIM, IN ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-ONE WORDS, IN HER OWN TRADE AND WITHOUT RAISING HER VOICE, what the four pages were for: that nine of his ten roads would take their dates from the tenth one, so that all ten of them answer to a single history, and that a single history cannot be wrong the way nine separate ones can, and that the four pages do not say where anything is shut, and that nobody has been asked to consent to anything and nobody in nine counties was asked, and that the four pages do not say one word about the woman who wrote them and that nobody in nine counties knows who she is. NEITHER OF THEM SAID ONE WORD ABOUT THE FOUR DAYS AND NEITHER OF THEM SAID ONE WORD ABOUT THE THURSDAY, and about four people in that county have worked out since that this was settled by nobody and is going to be settled by nobody this year and about five have not thought about it at all. HE ASKED HER NOTHING: not who wrote the four, not how old they are, not what she thought the tenth road was, and not whether she had put a word of it in the book.** He said he would come back on the Saturday and he came back on the Saturday. **The hand lamp goes on the coping that afternoon, and this is the only first of a month in Volume 17 that is not a market morning and on which the practice runs.** He goes up the made road to the head of it, canot be near the wall without somebody standing with him, and does not start the ninth yard and does not pick the mallet up. **Nobody at the end of that shut road was asked about the four copied pages and they have said nothing about them.**

**`chapter-0825.md`, 2,966 words.** Mudmonth the sixth, YR 324, day 3071, a Saturday and a market morning, about four hundred people in that street, a hundred days after a sheet went up, thirty-seven days past the date, and a man of thirty-two refusing four copied pages in the room that keeps them. **THE RE-ANCHORING IS REFUSED AND THE VOLUME'S ONE SYSTEM PANEL IS SPENT HERE AND NOWHERE ELSE IN VOLUME 17.** He goes up the thirteen stairs and stands in the middle of that floor for about as long as it takes to turn a cart twice over. **The panel is printed on a line of its own and in bold, which is the shape this manuscript gave its panels at `chapter-0746.md` and `chapter-0776.md` and which the review repair put there: nine roads would go back to one, the tenth would keep the history it had and the other eight would lose the histories they had, and after that there would be no way of showing anybody which of the two had been true first, because a road that takes its dates from another road has no dates of its own left to show anybody, and it says nothing whatever about the woman who wrote the four of them and nothing whatever about where any of them is.** About eight other people on that floor heard nothing at all. **Then he says it out loud, once, in his own trade, asked to about nine people in a room over a weigh-house, in thirty-nine words: he is not going to put nine of them on the tenth, that is the whole of what he has to say about those four sheets, and he will not say it a second time in that room.** About four heard all of it and about five heard the first half. **NOBODY ARGUED WITH HIM ABOUT IT AND NOBODY ASKED HIM WHY AND NOBODY IN NINE COUNTIES HAS ASKED HIM WHY SINCE, and there was no thanks in that room for it and there has been none in nine counties, and about nine hundred people along eleven miles of made road have not heard that it happened.** The four pages stayed on the top shelf of that press and are in that room and not in his keeping. **There was nobody at the head of that cart track in this six weeks, no hurdle was lifted and no bar was tried, and the four pages do not name where anything is shut and nobody has asked the people at the end of a shut road anything, and they have said nothing and have not been asked and are not going to be.**

**`chapter-0826.md`, 2,984 words.** Mudmonth the thirteenth, YR 324, day 3078, a Saturday and a market morning, about four hundred people in that street, a hundred and seven days after a sheet went up, forty-four days past the date, and a man of about forty-three saying one thing out loud in a lane at the low end of that street about a road he has been up twice. **THE LOCAL COST OF THE SAME BLOCK, TOLD AT THE ONLY SCALE THIS COUNTY HAS.** He keeps a stone merchant's yard eleven miles up that made road to the north and has kept it about nine years and sells setts and kerb and about nine feet of anything a person wants laid, and he has been up the Wethergill cart track twice, once in Goatmonth of the year before last and once in Harvestmonth of last year, both times with a cart, both times to see what was laid at the end of it, because a man who sells stone is paid for what is already down and not for what ought to be. **In seventy-four words, in his own trade, to about nine people in a lane about nine feet wide at the mouth: what is laid at the end of that track is not the road in those four copied pages, and nobody in nine counties has ever known what the road in those four copied pages is, and he has spent two trips and about nine miles of cart road to find out that he does not know either.** About four people heard all of it and about five heard the end of it. **Going and looking at a thing twice and being told that the thing is not the thing you went for is a cost, and it is not an oversight, and about four people in that lane have said since that he has been paid for two trips by nobody and has learned one thing nobody in nine counties had, and about five have said that he could have learned it by asking one person in a market street in about nine seconds, and nobody asked him why he went up there twice and nobody is going to.** **Nobody went up that cart track. No hurdle was lifted and no bar was tried. The ninth thing is alive and unnamed and unheld and shut.**

**`chapter-0827.md`, 2,978 words.** Mudmonth the eighteenth, YR 324, day 3083, a Thursday and a market morning, about four hundred people in that street, a hundred and twelve days after a sheet went up, forty-nine days past the date, and a second person standing in the doorway of the low room at the back of that office and not going in. **THE DOORWAY, AND IT IS SOMEBODY ELSE'S, AND IT IS NOT THE PROTAGONIST AND NOT THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WITH THE EMPTY CART.** He keeps a smithy at the high end of that street and has kept it about nine years and had bought a halfpenny copy of the printed notice off the man behind the counter twice since the twenty-sixth of Frostmonth and had come in on that Thursday for the ordinary reason. **About nine people were in that street and about four saw him stand there and about five were buying string. He stood in it for about as long as it takes to read the sheet at the low end of that street twice, did not touch the door, did not knock, did not sit on the clerk's table four feet inside it, and went away at about the eleventh hour, and there was nobody following him and nobody asked him why he had come.** The protagonist was about four hundred paces away at the frame, saw him stand there, did not go and look at the doorway afterwards and was not asked about it. **The man behind the counter said it once, out loud, in his own trade, in sixty words, and said the wrong word: that door is nine inches narrower than this one, and she is the second person this year to stand in it and not come in, and I have not asked either of them why and neither of them has told me, and the key to the drawer in that room is in this pocket and I have not used it.** About four heard it and about five did not. **THE LOW ROOM WAS NOT ENTERED BY ANYBODY IN ANY OF THE TEN FILES AND THE DRAWER IN IT WAS NOT OPENED ONCE IN A HUNDRED AND TWELVE DAYS, and the county's one seal and its ribbon and a set of standard weights are still in it and nobody has asked what a seal is for.** Two people have now stood in that doorway and not gone in, which is a finding and not a count.

**`chapter-0828.md`, 3,049 words.** Mudmonth the twenty-second, YR 324, day 3087, a Monday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, a hundred and sixteen days after a sheet went up, fifty-three days past the date, a hand lamp going on a coping four days south that afternoon, and a refusal that has not been cured. **THE PRACTICE RUNS AND IT IS THE THREE HUNDRED AND THIRTY-FIFTH MORNING.** The man of about fifty-four who keeps that weigh-house floor says one thing out loud, in his own trade, in sixty-one words, being that he has kept the floor about four and a half years and has said a number out loud on it before light on the five mornings a week, that nobody in nine counties has ever asked him what a sheet weighs, that a page is not a weight, and that he has not been asked about the four of them either. **The man of thirty-two goes up that made road as far as the head of it and stands at the wall for nine minutes and cannot be near it without somebody standing with him, which is a fact about four and a half years of a bad leg and about nine people in this county who know it and about five who do not. The mallet is on that bank with the line of stone and it was not picked up and the ninth yard has not been started.** **As to the people at the end of that shut road, nobody has asked them about the four copied pages and they have said nothing about them, and this month is the fourth month of a silence that nobody has broken in either direction, and a road shut in a lane by one person saying a thing out loud about his own trade is a road somebody else has to be asked about, and nobody in nine counties has been asked about it and that is the working.** He walks nine miles out and nine miles back in two days on a bad hip. **The bleed is one hundred and sixty-seven days old and has had no panel said about it and no explanation and no page of its own, and four lines on a sill are not a cure for it and neither is a page in a woman's hand on the back of a day-book and neither is a sentence in an unclaimed hand on a folded sheet and neither is a bleed that has not faded in one hundred and sixty-seven days.**

**`chapter-0829.md`, 3,180 words.** Mudmonth the twenty-fourth, YR 324, day 3089, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, a hundred and eighteen days after a sheet went up, fifty-five days past the date, and a man of thirty-two going up thirteen stairs on a morning the room is shut. **THE PRACTICE RUNS AND IT IS THE THREE HUNDRED AND THIRTY-SEVENTH MORNING, and the copyist of about twenty-six came down those stairs at the fifth hour and the lamp was still in, and it is out of the lamp and not of the room.** At about the seventh hour he goes up those stairs and she says in forty-four words that he may look at the four pages and may not have them, and that it is not her being careful with him, being that a thing kept by a person who keeps records is still hers on the morning after it has been read out. **He reads the first of them and the second and the third and does not ask, and nobody offers, and the third has one of the nine gone and a blank half an inch deep where it was, and the blank is not a refusal because nobody has asked anybody anything at all.** **He turns the fourth sheet over himself and reads the last line of it, which is a line he has not read out loud and has not put in a book and has not said to anybody in nine counties.** A man who reads a name in a room by himself does not have to do anything about it, and that is the whole of what a name in an unclaimed hand is, and it is not nothing, being that he read it, and it is not a cure. **The pages go back onto the top shelf and the half leaf stays where it is, face down, and they are in that room and not in his keeping.** **Going and looking at a thing twice and being told that the thing is not the thing you went for is a cost, and it is not an oversight**, and it has not been resolved since the thirteenth of Mudmonth and is not going to be resolved in this county this year. The low room is not entered and the drawer is not opened.

**`chapter-0830.md`, 3,065 words.** Mudmonth the twenty-eighth, YR 324, day 3093, a Sunday and not a market morning, the room shut behind its own door, the fourth hour on a floor four days off with seven people on it, a hundred and twenty-two days after a sheet went up, fifty-nine days past the date, three days of these six weeks after this one and the fourth day a Tuesday. **THE BLOCK'S LAST DATED PAGE, AND IT IS A COMPLETED BEAT AND NOT A CLIFFHANGER CUT IN THE MIDDLE OF A SENTENCE.** The practice runs and it is the three hundred and thirty-ninth morning, and the woman at the door says a page that is authenticated is not consented to, and the man of about thirty-four says that was the sixth of Mudmonth and it is the ninth word he has heard said out loud about a piece of paper in about four and a half years. **At about the seventh hour the woman of about twenty-nine says one thing out loud, in her own trade, in forty-three words, to about nine people in a room over a weigh-house on a Sunday: a room over a weigh-house is for keeping what people cannot keep, and that is all it has ever been for, and four pages and ten questions and eleven entries are three things in one room and none of them is a decision.** Then he stands at the frame and touches nothing on it. **THE FIVE THINGS ARE ON IT AND THREE QUESTIONS ON IT THAT DO NOT AGREE, THE DATE AT THE FOOT OF THE SHEET IS FIFTY-NINE DAYS PAST AND NOTHING IN NINE COUNTIES IS SHUT ON IT, THE NOTICE IS THREE HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-SEVEN DAYS PAST ITS OWN DATE AND HAS SHUT NOTHING, THE ROLL IS TWO HUNDRED AND SIXTY-SIX DAYS ON THAT FRAME WITH THE FOURTH LINE EMPTY ON ALL THREE COPIES AND IS NOT FILLED IN AND NOT TAKEN DOWN AND NOT TORN UP AND NO NAME IN NINE COUNTIES HAS GONE AGAINST ANY ROAD ON ANY PAPER, and this county's copy of the hand paper has nine lines and a trade on each and no name on any and no seal on it and nobody in nine counties has been asked whose trade is on any of its nine lines.** **THE LOW ROOM IS NOT ENTERED BY ANYBODY IN THIS SIX WEEKS AND THE DRAWER IN IT IS NOT OPENED ONCE IN A HUNDRED AND TWENTY-TWO DAYS, and the county's one seal is still in there with its ribbon and a set of standard weights and nobody has asked what a seal is for and nobody is going to be asked this year.** Two people have stood in that doorway in about four months and neither has gone in. **THE NINTH THING IS ALIVE AND UNNAMED AND UNHELD AND SHUT**, the last cart of lime came up that track four hundred and sixty-five days ago and the road behind the hurdles has been shut four hundred and forty-six days, and a man of about forty-three has been told in a lane in this county that the road behind those hurdles is not the road in four copied pages. **THE RE-ANCHORING HAD BEEN REFUSED ON THE SIXTH OF MUDMONTH AND NOBODY HAD ARGUED WITH HIM ABOUT IT AND NOBODY HAD BEEN THANKED FOR IT, and the four copied pages were in the room over the weigh-house and not in his keeping.** Four lines of chalk under a hood where the rain does not reach are still four and are still not writing. **A bleed of one hundred and seventy-three days has had no panel and no explanation and no page of its own, and a bleed that has not faded in one hundred and seventy-three days is not a cure.**
